# Implementation status (M0 + M1), 2026-10-07

Status words follow the spec's section 0: implemented, partial, missing, unverified. "Unit-tested" means the logic passed Node tests; nothing has been run in the game yet, so every in-game behavior is unverified until `M1_test_plan.md` is done.

| Area | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Packs, manifests, stable modules | implemented, unverified in game | `/bedrock-new`, then hand-edited; pack checker clean (2 warnings: vanilla rabbit animations, confirmed in Mojang's samples) |
| Record store, migrations, quarantine | implemented, unit-tested | chunked JSON in world dynamic properties, 8,000 characters per chunk |
| Park clock | implemented, unit-tested | 20-minute park day, pauses with no member online |
| Roles | implemented | owner / manager / keeper / visitor stored and checked (park/rules.js); Members page lists everyone who ever joined, online or not; the park can be handed over; players hear their role on joining |
| Zoo Coins ledger | implemented, unit-tested | idempotent by operation id; keeps the newest 2,000 entries |
| Habitat validation | implemented, unit-tested | walking animals only (steps, drops, water crossing, gates, low ceilings); aquatic, flying and climbing validators are later milestones |
| Feeder | implemented, unverified | stock in records, three looks, returns rations when broken; no hopper support (spec: manual first) |
| Zoo rabbit | implemented, unverified | vanilla look and coat, no damage, home restriction; no breeding (spec: needs juvenile support first) |
| Welfare | implemented, unit-tested | five needs, bands, illness without death; shelter and enrichment items come in M2 |
| Carriers and journal | implemented, unit-tested | reload recovery, stale-token rejection, recovery holding |
| Visitors | partial | numbers plus a few cosmetic visible guests; Ticket Booth price, Viewing Point sightlines, guest paths, Snack Stands, Benches, Litter Bins, Donation Boxes and the guests' main complaint; no gift shop, restrooms or visitor profiles yet |
| Clipboard, park menu, sidebar, action bar | implemented, unverified | ActionForm / ModalForm / MessageForm; live CustomForm dashboard is M2 |
| Tutorial, Zoo Workshop, otter, staff | missing | M2 and later |

## Decisions and assumptions made in M1

- Starter species: rabbit, using the vanilla look (the plan's default). Custom animals get original models from M2.
- Items are crafted at a vanilla crafting table until the Zoo Workshop arrives in M2.
- Recipes not in the spec were proposed here: Park Entrance Gate (planks, gold, iron), Cleaning Rake (sticks, iron nuggets), Exhibit Clipboard (paper, planks, copper), Park Deed (paper, gold), Leafy Ration (leaves + wheat, 4 kelp, or 4 bamboo, each makes 2).
- Catching a rabbit needs one Leafy Ration as bait and a validated exhibit with room, so an animal is never caught with nowhere to go.
- Zoo animals can't be hurt. `/kill` sends the animal to recovery holding instead of deleting it.
- A summoned `zoocraft:zoo_rabbit` without a record is removed.
- Guests pay per in-game minute in small batches rather than once a day, so a new park sees income quickly. Each batch has its own operation id.
- The sidebar shows the park score (0 to 100); the star rating is in the park menu.
- Peaceful care (needs don't drop) is a park setting, off by default.

## Known gaps

- Explosions and pistons that open a fence are caught by a re-check of occupied, loaded exhibits about every 2 minutes, not instantly. Player edits are caught within 2 seconds.
- An animal outside its exhibit is reported, not moved back (spec: never teleport to hide poor pathing). Recovery is by carrier.
- The plugin's `/bedrock-new` wrote the plugin's absolute container path into `package.json`; the scripts were rewritten here without it. Worth fixing in the plugin.

## Fixes from in-game testing

- 0.1.1 (2026-10-07): the Small Carrier couldn't catch anything (test step 9). The carrier lookup reported every carrier as occupied, because the item-id field `occupied` overwrote the flag. The flag is now `full`, with a unit test. Also added a backup catch path: using an empty carrier while looking at a rabbit within 5 blocks catches it even if the game doesn't send an entity interaction.
- 0.1.3 (2026-10-07): step 22 showed "Prepare an exhibit first" four times. The pen (6 x 6 = 36 blocks) only fits one rabbit; two need 48. The catch message now names the exhibit and the blocks needed versus available, and a catch attempt is handled once per half second (the entity interaction and the item use both fired, and holding use repeats). Test plan step 3 now asks for 7 x 7.
- 0.1.4 (2026-10-07): taking an animal out of Recovery holding needed an empty carrier in hand, but the Park Deed has to be in hand to open the menu. It now uses an empty carrier anywhere in the inventory.
- M2 part 1 (2026-10-07): Zoo Workshop block (equipment recipes moved to it; rations and the deed stay at the crafting table too), Park Ledger dashboard on the Park Deed (overview with top warnings, Animals, Exhibits, Finances, Journal), caretaker tutorial (9 steps checked from the park's real state, 100-coin reward, replacement starter kit at most 3 times, one per park day). The spec's "speak to the caretaker guide" step waits for an NPC in the world template (M6).
- M2 part 2 (2026-10-07): the otter, the first custom animal: original 64 x 32 model and skin, walk/swim/idle animations, a wild otter (spawns in rivers and swamps, spawn egg) and the zoo otter (no damage, stays home). Otters alternate a swimming mood and a bank mood so guests see both. Fish Rations (1 cod or salmon makes 2). Land+water exhibits: water surface counts as space for otters, and an otter exhibit needs at least 35% water, 25% dry bank and 4 spots 2 blocks deep. Animals only eat rations matching their diet, with a "wrong food" warning. A separate Fish Feeder block was not added: the Basic Feeder holds any ration.
- 0.1.7 (2026-10-07): otters float back up and climb onto the bank in their bank mood (behavior.float in mood_bank; before, one 2 blocks down stayed on the pool floor). Supply shop sells rations back: Sell rations, 1 coin each for Leafy and Fish Rations (takes the items before paying).
- 0.1.8 (2026-10-07): float removed (Austin: looked terrible). Otters swim up and out like vanilla turtles: move_to_land/move_to_water search the whole pool every tick (search_count 0, height 6, range 10), random_swim y_dist 4, navigation can_path_over_water false, water_movement drag 0.9, underwater speed 0.2.
- M2 part 3a (2026-10-07): exhibit equipment. Water Trough (16 water, a bucket adds 8, 1 per animal per day; land animals without drinking water have comfort capped at 50; otters drink from the pool), Hanging Food Basket (holds 8 ration units, forage enrichment while it holds food the species eats; breaking it returns the food), Floating Toy (a bobbing ball entity on an exhibit pool, swim enrichment; interact to pick up), Water Filter (an unfiltered pool adds dirt in proportion to its water share; green light when linked), Keeper Gate (2 tall, staff-only, auto-closes after 3 s; two block types, the script places and removes the top), Recovery Station (opens recovery holding; with one in the park, held animals recover 60 need points a day and unwell animals critical for a park day move to holding). Each enrichment kind counts once per exhibit at 12 per day. All crafted at the Zoo Workshop.
- M2 part 3b (2026-10-07): guests. Ticket Booth (within 8 blocks of the Park Entrance Gate it sets admission from 0 to 40; the fair price is 10 + 2 per star; each coin over it costs 8 value points and 3% of guests, each coin under adds 2% up to 20%). Viewing Point (counts when it's inside the park, outside every exhibit, has a 2-high standing spot on solid ground beside it, and has a clear sightline within 24 blocks to at least one animal; glass, fences, bars and water don't block, solid blocks and leaves do; it keeps counting for 5 park minutes while animals hide; green light when it counts). Viewing satisfaction per occupied exhibit: 40 + 0.6 x welfare when seen, 0.4 x welfare when not. The Park Ledger shows the guests' main complaint (viewing, cleanliness or value under 60). Visible guests: up to 6 cosmetic people (one per 4 expected daily admissions) appear at working Viewing Points and the entrance near players, wander within 6 blocks, watch the animals, say something when you talk to them, and leave after 1.5 to 3.5 minutes; never saved. New tutorial step: add a viewing point, before opening the zoo.
- M3 part 1 (2026-10-07): guest paths and services. Guest paths: allowlisted vanilla blocks (gravel, dirt path, bricks, tiles, polished stone, smooth stone, cobblestone, concrete, planks, slabs, stairs) with two blocks of headroom; one bounded walk (up to 3,000 blocks, inside the park) from the path blocks within 3 of the Park Entrance Gate, re-run when someone builds in the park or every 2 minutes; a feature counts when a reached path is within 2 blocks sideways. Viewing Points now need a path too (spec section 12). Snack Stand: holds 64 snacks, stocked from bread, cookies, apples, melon, baked potatoes, pumpkin pie, cake or sweet berries, or bought in at 2 coins each; price 1 to 15 (fair 4: 35% of guests buy one; 3% fewer per coin over, 3% more per coin under, at most 50%); a broken stand gives back bread. Bench: seating, 1 per 8 expected daily guests, at least 2. Litter: half a unit per guest and 1 per snack; Litter Bins (32 each) catch it, the rest lands on the paths (up to 60); a keeper empties a bin by using it, and the Cleaning Rake on a path sweeps 25. Donation Box: within 16 blocks of a working Viewing Point; 1.5 coins per guest at perfect welfare with 2 boxes. Services = 40% food + 30% seating + 30% tidy paths (it was a flat 50); the services complaint names its weakest part. Tutorial: "Lay a guest path" after "Open the zoo", then "Add a viewing point".
- 0.1.15 (2026-10-08): bigger service furniture (Austin). Snack Stand: a kiosk on a 2 x 2 base, 2 blocks tall (counter, tray and till, menu board, striped roof on posts; the gap between counter and cupboard is walkable). Bench: 2 blocks wide, 1 tall. Both are script multi-blocks: the placed block is the main part with the record, the others are hidden part blocks; placing needs the space clear, breaking any part breaks all and drops one item, a click on any part acts on the main one, and stands or benches placed earlier get their missing parts on the next check if the space is clear. A path beside any ground cell reaches them. Litter Bin: 12 x 14 x 12 with a 13-wide rim (was 8 x 11 x 8).
- 0.1.16 (2026-10-08): playtest fixes (Austin). Guests: half the speed, prefer path blocks when pathing (preferred_path), no random wandering; they walk between Viewing Points, Snack Stands, Benches and Donation Boxes within 24 blocks, stop at a working Viewing Point to watch for 25 to 50 s facing the exhibit, stay 200 to 400 s, then walk out through the gate. 80% arrive under the entrance arch. Park Entrance Gate: a multi-block arch 5 wide and 4 high (brick pillars, beam, ZOO sign); the placed block is a walk-over threshold, old gates grow the arch when there's room. Ticket Booth: breaking the booth that sets the price resets admission to the default. Viewing Points: a point in a glass tunnel through a pool counts as the guest side once the exhibit is measured; coral fans, coral and sea pickles don't block the view (water and glass already didn't). Animals can be renamed from the clipboard or with a vanilla name tag (keeper permission; up to 24 characters).
- 0.1.17 (2026-10-08): Austin's playtest of 0.1.16. Bench and Snack Stand halves showed mirrored (each half's outer end in the middle): Bedrock block geometry is mirrored in x against the world, while the script places the parts with model +x = world +x. The bench, stand and entrance-arch models are now mirrored in x (gen/mirror_x.py; a test checks the bench frames and the arch's beam stubs). Ticket Booth: a Park Entrance Gate becomes the entrance as soon as it's placed inside the park when there's no working entrance yet, and a booth with no entrance pairs with a gate within 8 blocks; before, a new gate only counted after it was used once.
- 0.1.18 (2026-10-08): guests walked too slowly and watched too long (Austin). Walking speed 0.35 (was 0.5 before 0.1.16, then 0.2); a Viewing Point stop lasts 10 to 20 s (was 25 to 50).
- M3 part 2 (2026-10-08): the capybara, the second custom animal (original 64 x 64 model and skin; walk, swim, idle, rest and look animations). Wild capybaras spawn in swamps, mangrove swamps and jungles in groups of 2 to 4; zoo capybaras can't be hurt and stay home like otters. Mostly on the bank (a two-to-one mood split with swimming), resting now and then; they swim low with the head out. Diet: Leafy Rations (also tempted by wheat, melon and sugar cane). New habitat profile medium_wetland: 96 blocks for the first animal and 24 per extra, at least 20% water, 45% dry bank and 2 spots 2 deep. New Livestock Crate carrier (Zoo Workshop: planks, an oak fence and an iron ingot; shop 60 coins); the carrier message now names the right carrier.
- 0.1.20 (2026-10-08): guests could stand inside each other (Austin). Guests are now pushable by other entities (minecraft:pushable_by_entity), so they spread out at Viewing Points and the entrance like vanilla mobs.
- 0.1.21 (2026-10-08): capybara fixes from Austin's playtest. Face: 2 x 2 eyes with a white glint under a lighter brow, and two small brown nostrils instead of the black bar that read as a second pair of eyes. Capybaras slid around in the lying-down pose: the animation now switches to walking whenever they move and only shows the rest pose while they stand still, and sitting outranks being led by food or pulled home, so a resting capybara stays put until it gets up.
- 0.1.22 (2026-10-08): capybara eyes, second pass (Austin: "more white and realistic", from his reference block). Each eye is 3 x 3: white, with a 2 x 2 black pupil in the top corner, so the white makes an L along the bottom and one side. The eyes sit on the head's front corners and wrap round onto the sides, pupil on the corner.
- M3 part 3 (2026-10-08): keepers. New Staff Room block (Zoo Workshop: planks, a chest and an iron ingot) holds up to 256 rations and houses 2 keepers (6 at most in the park). Hire keepers from the Staff Room or Park Ledger > Staff for 40 coins each park day; a keeper who can't be paid stops work until the park has the coins. Each keeper looks after up to 4 exhibits: refills feeders and food baskets from the Staff Room's rations, tops up water troughs and cleans when cleanliness drops under 60, most urgent job first. With a player within 48 blocks the keeper appears in the exhibit (khaki uniform, green cap), walks to the job and works in view; otherwise the job is done out of sight. Keepers move supplies, they never make food. Staff menu: stock, take out or buy rations, hire, assign exhibits, rename, dismiss.
- 0.1.24 (2026-10-08): capybara eyes, third pass (Austin): each eye is a plain black strip 2 pixels wide and 1 tall on the side of the head, high up and 2 pixels back from the front. No white, and nothing on the front of the face; the two nostrils stay on the snout.
- 0.1.25 (2026-10-08): the Staff Room is a building the players make (Austin: "like the animals and the exhibit"). The Staff Room block goes inside a closed building (walls, floor and roof with no gaps; doors count as wall, open or shut) or against its outside wall, and the building needs open space at least 3 x 3 x 3 inside (the block's own spot counts; torches, carpets and signs don't take space, furniture does). The room is measured when the block is placed or used, when someone builds or breaks within 3 blocks of it, and every 2 park minutes. Only working rooms house keepers (2 each) and supply them; the Staff menu says in red what's missing, and a room that stops working tells the park's members.
- M3 part 4 (2026-10-08): Field Journal and Research Desk (spec sections 4 and 8). Field Journal (Park Ledger): one page per species, levels Unseen, Observed, Care notes, Ready to move. Park members fill it in by looking at an animal, wild or in a zoo, within 24 blocks: 2 s to spot one, 3 s of unbroken watching for each behaviour (otters and capybaras: swimming, and out on the bank; rabbits only need spotting). Care notes show food, space, water, company, enrichment and carrier. A wild animal can only be caught once its species is ready to move; species a park already keeps start complete. Research Desk (Zoo Workshop: 3 books, planks and a crafting table): four projects that open as the journal fills in and cost parts and coins: Bulk Feeders (feeders hold 48), Park Guidebook (10% more guests), Keeper Training (6 exhibits per keeper, jobs a quarter faster), Veterinary Care (recovery twice as fast).
- M3 part 5 (2026-10-08): Gift Shop (spec section 12, shops). New Gift Shop block (Zoo Workshop: 3 paper, planks and a chest). Like the Staff Room it goes in a building the players make (at least 3 x 3 x 3 inside, the block inside or against the outside wall), and a guest path from the entrance has to reach it (through the door; a plank or gravel floor inside counts). Stock it with wool (2 plush toys each), paper (postcards) or paintings (3 prints each), or buy 8 souvenirs for 32 coins; it holds 64. Guests buy souvenirs on their way out: about 25% at the fair price of 8 after a memorable visit, which depends on the welfare of the animals guests can see from Viewing Points and on seeing more than one kind (one kind: 75% of the sales). Several shops share the guests. A shop whose building breaks closes and tells the park's members; a broken shop drops its stock as paper.
- M3 part 6 (2026-10-08): the deer (spec section 5.2: herd grazing and alert posture; quiet cover, browse feed, group space), the third custom animal: an original 64 x 64 model with long legs, a long neck, big ears and a white tail; bucks have antlers, does don't. Its idle cycles between grazing (neck down, chewing) and an alert look (head up, ears up, tail flicked), offset per animal. Wild deer spawn in small herds (2 to 4, 3 does to 2 bucks) in forests, birch forests, taigas and meadows; they keep 10 blocks away from players who aren't sneaking, unless the player holds food (Leafy Ration, wheat, apples, sweet berries). Medium terrestrial exhibit (100 usable blocks for one, 30 per extra, 4 high), Leafy Rations, Livestock Crate, groups of 2 to 8. Field Journal: spot one, watch one grazing (standing still) and watch one with its herd (another deer within 8 blocks). Quiet cover: at least 10% of the exhibit's usable ground needs leaves or a roof somewhere 4 to 11 blocks above it; without it comfort can't rise above 60 and the clipboard says how many blocks are covered.
- 0.1.30 (2026-10-08): deer fixes from Austin's playtest. Rump: brown with a white patch under the tail instead of a solid white end, and the lower flanks fade to pale instead of a hard white band. Tail: brown on top with a white tip, white underneath. Deer now prance now and then (wild or in a zoo, near a player, about once every 30 s each): 3 to 5 bounding hops in one direction, under a block high so fences still hold them, with a leap pose in the air (front legs forward, hind legs back, tail up).
- M3 part 7 (2026-10-08): penguins and the Cooling Unit (spec section 5.5: "colony waddling, diving; cold pool and dry nesting bank"; item list: Cooling Unit). The penguin is the fourth custom animal: an original 64 x 64 gentoo (black back, white belly, white bonnet over the eyes, orange beak and feet) that waddles on land (body rocking, flippers out), swims lying flat with its flippers beating, and in its idle loop stretches its flippers and calls with its head up. Wild penguins spawn in colonies of 3 to 6 in frozen biomes; zoo penguins can't be hurt and stay home. Half the time on the bank, half swimming; slow on land, fast in the water. Fish Rations (also tempted by cod and salmon), Small Carrier, colonies of 3 to 12. New habitat profile small_polar: 64 blocks for the first bird and 12 per extra, at least 40% water with 4 spots 2 deep, and 30% dry bank. Field Journal: spot one, watch one swimming, watch one with its colony (another penguin within 8 blocks). Cold: a penguin exhibit needs a Cooling Unit (Zoo Workshop: 3 iron ingots, 2 packed ice, a copper ingot and redstone; packed ice instead of the spec's ice because ice needs Silk Touch) standing beside the floor or the pool, stocked with ice (snow block 1, ice 2, packed ice 6, blue ice 18 cold; holds 32; uses 2 a park day per exhibit). Without cold, penguin comfort can't rise above 40 and the exhibit shows a red warning; the unit's light is blue when stocked, amber when low and red when empty. The supply shop sells packed ice (8 for 32 coins). Keepers don't carry ice yet. The pebble nests (Nest Box) wait for a later slice.
- M3 part 8 (2026-10-08): giraffes, the Elevated Browse Feeder and the Reinforced Crate (spec section 5.5: "elevated browse feeders", "giraffes need greater shelter clearance"; item list: Reinforced Crate). The giraffe is the fifth custom animal: an original 128 x 64 reticulated giraffe (orange-brown patches on cream, mane, ossicones, tufted tail) that walks with a pace gait and in its idle loop stretches its neck up to browse, flicking its ears and swishing its tail. Wild giraffes spawn in savannas in herds of 2 to 4; zoo giraffes can't be hurt and stay home. Leafy Rations (also tempted by acacia leaves and apples), 3 units a park day each, herds of 2 to 6. New habitat profile tall_terrestrial: 180 blocks for the first and 60 per extra, with 7 blocks of headroom. Field Journal: spot one, watch one browsing (standing still), watch one with its herd. Feeding: giraffes eat only from an Elevated Browse Feeder (a native two-block-tall multi-block; Zoo Workshop: a Basic Feeder between two sticks, raised on two planks), and nothing else can reach one; the clipboard warns either way, and keepers only fill the feeder the animals can reach. Breaking either half drops the feeder and its rations. Transport: the Reinforced Crate (Zoo Workshop: iron ingots around a Livestock Crate; shop 120 coins) carries one giraffe.
- 0.1.34 (2026-10-08): natural trees now give deer cover (Austin). A natural tree's canopy usually hangs 2 or 3 blocks above the animals' feet, inside the exhibit's headroom, so the ground under it was measured as a low ceiling: not usable, and not counted as cover. Exhibit measuring now reads tree leaves separately: animals can stand under a canopy (it no longer cuts the usable area, for any species), and leaves from 2 blocks above the feet up to 8 above the headroom count as cover. A solid roof still has to clear the headroom.
- 0.1.35 (2026-10-08): briefly went back to the stricter rule (leaves must clear the headroom) with clearer warnings, before Austin chose.
- 0.1.36 (2026-10-08, Austin chose "Trees count"; the rule "could get confusing"): the 0.1.34 tree rule is back, and the cover rule is spelled out everywhere. The cover warning says what counts (tree leaves 2 to 11 blocks up, or a roof at least 4 blocks up, for deer); a new warning counts floor under a roof lower than the headroom (unusable, and never cover); the clipboard's Ground line shows those blocks as "too low to use"; the Field Journal care notes list the cover rule for species that need it.
- 0.1.37 (2026-10-08, Austin: "players should have to walk to those buildings"): the Park Ledger no longer has a Staff button; keepers are hired and managed only at a Staff Room block (the ledger shows the keeper count and says so). The Gift Shop and Research Desk menus were already only at their blocks.
- 0.1.38 (2026-10-08, Austin: waddling "makes their flipper go into their side"): every penguin flipper rotation had the wrong sign (in Bedrock a positive z rotation swings a hanging left (+x) bone inward), so flippers swung into the body. Walk, swim and idle now swing them outward; the waddle itself is unchanged.
- 0.1.39 (2026-10-08, Austin: the giraffe head "is looking up too much"; make the body-to-head joint "more realistic"): the neck leans further forward and the head sits nose-down; browsing lifts the head to level instead of tipping it up, and looking at players no longer throws away the pose. New withers on the shoulders and a thick neck base make the neck grow out of the chest instead of sticking out of the corner.
- 0.1.40 (2026-10-08, M3 part 9, Austin: "Next module"): elephants, the mud wallow and the Enrichment Ball. Wild elephants live in savanna herds (2 to 4); watch one spot, one feeding with its trunk (standing still) and one with its herd, then move it in a Reinforced Crate. They need the large exhibit size (256 blocks for one, 80 more each, 6 blocks of headroom), eat 4 Leafy Rations a day, and want a mud wallow: at least 6 blocks of vanilla mud (a water bottle on dirt) in the exhibit floor, or comfort stays at 55 or below. The care warning, the clipboard (Ground line "N mud" and the empty-exhibit hint) and the Field Journal care notes all say so. The Enrichment Ball (Zoo Workshop: 4 leather around a slimeball) is used on the ground inside an exhibit; it becomes a ball animals push about, gives "manipulate" enrichment (elephants, otters), and using it picks it back up. The idle animation feeds with the trunk, then sprays a mud bath over the back.
- 0.1.41 (2026-10-08, Austin: giraffe "neck and head are not spotted like the rest", elephant eyes hard to see, and real-world sizes for every animal against a 6 ft player): the giraffe's neck front and neck base are patched like the body and its head has small patches all over (muzzle pale). Elephant eyes are 2 x 2 at the very front of the head, clear of the ear roots, in a paler ring, and wrap onto the face so they show from the front. Sizes (1 block is about 1 m; models and skins unchanged, drawn with the client "scale" script, collision boxes set to match): giraffe x1.55 (head 4.9 m, about 16 ft; shoulder 3.2 m), elephant x1.5 (shoulder 3.0 m, head 3.4 m), deer x0.9 (shoulder 1.0 m, head 1.6 m), capybara x0.7 (0.6 m at the back, 1.1 m long), gentoo penguin x0.75 (0.75 m), otter x0.6 (0.3 m at the back, 1.1 m with tail). The zoo rabbit already matched a real rabbit and is unchanged. Exhibit headroom (giraffe 7, elephant 6) still clears the bigger animals.
- 0.1.42 (2026-10-08, M3 part 10, Austin: "next module"): breeding controls (spec section 9). Breeding is opt-in per exhibit (Exhibit Clipboard: "Turn breeding on"), and the clipboard always says what it is waiting for. A pair starts expecting when the exhibit is enclosed with no problems, has an unrelated adult female and male (parents and young, or brothers and sisters, never pair), room for the young (young count as half an animal), at least 2 park days of food, a sheltered corner (tree leaves or a roof over 10% of the floor, at least 2 blocks), and every adult at welfare 60 or more for 2 park days in a row. Then: rabbits 1 park day (2 kits), otters and capybaras 2 (2 pups), penguins 2 (1 chick), deer 3 (1 fawn), giraffes 4, elephants 5 (1 calf); the exhibit rests 2 to 10 park days after a birth. Young eat half rations, need the shelter (comfort stays at 60 or below without it, with a warning), are drawn smaller (the client scale reads query.is_baby; a "zoocraft:juvenile" group adds minecraft:is_baby and a smaller collision box), and grow up in 2 to 8 park days (the mob is swapped for an adult one). Every animal now has a sex (deer: doe or buck); animals caught earlier get a fixed one. Births are journaled ("births") and never pay money. The Field Journal care notes list the rules. Test plan: docs/Breeding_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.43 (2026-10-08, M4 part 1, Austin: "next module"): the Nether wing starts with the Basalt Tortoise, the Thermal Regulator, Mineral Pellets and the Thermal Carrier (spec 5.3, 10). Wild Basalt Tortoises live in basalt deltas and walk onto magma blocks to bask; watch one spot, one basking on magma and one gnawing at the rocks. Catching one needs a Thermal Carrier (Reinforced Crate, 4 basalt, 2 magma cream) and the new Heat Systems research (3 species ready to move, 2 magma cream, 4 copper ingots, 200 coins). Their exhibit needs a fuelled Thermal Regulator beside the floor (charcoal or coal 2 heat, blaze rod 6, coal block 18; it uses 2 a park day) before one can move in, and at least 4 magma blocks in the floor to bask on (comfort stays at 55 or below otherwise). Without heat comfort stays at 30 or below, the warning says so at once with a countdown, and after half a park day (10 minutes) every animal in that exhibit goes to recovery holding; nothing catches fire. They eat Mineral Pellets (basalt or blackstone with bone meal, or the supply shop, which also sells charcoal and the Thermal Carrier). Sized like a giant tortoise: about a metre high and 1.5 m long. The shell glows softly while it basks. Test plan: docs/Basalt_Tortoise_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.44 (2026-10-08, Austin: "Feeders should look different on the inside depending what food it is", "the basalt tortoise should drink lava instead of water", "We need a lava trough too"): Basic Feeders and Hanging Food Baskets show the food in them: leaves for Leafy Rations, silver fish for Fish Rations, grey pellets with cream and orange flecks for Mineral Pellets (a new zoocraft:food block state beside zoocraft:fill; low and full looks in the feeder). The Elevated Browse Feeder keeps its leafy browse rack, since only leaf-eaters use it. New Lava Trough (blackstone with a copper band, 2 blackstone + bucket shape at the Zoo Workshop: "S S", "SBS"): fill it with lava buckets; the lava glows and gives light. Basalt Tortoises (species.drinksLava) drink only from a Lava Trough; a pool or a Water Trough is no use to them, and other animals never drink lava. Trough records from a Lava Trough have liquid "lava". Keepers top up Lava Troughs too. Warnings, the clipboard and the Field Journal say so. Test plan: docs/Feeder_food_and_Lava_Trough_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.45 (2026-10-09, M4 part 2, Austin: "next module"): Ember Fox (spec 5.3: charcoal fox with ember ear and tail tips; digs warm ash and curls in a heated den). Wild in soul sand valleys and crimson forests; Thermal Carrier; small_terrestrial (36 blocks); needs a fuelled Thermal Regulator, a Lava Trough (drinksLava), 4+ Warm Ash blocks in the floor (species.ash, geometry ashCells) and an Ember Den (fixture type "den", no collision, in PASSABLE_PATTERNS so its cell is floor; foxes move_to_block into it). Ember Ration (blaze powder + charcoal + raw meat -> 3; diet "ember", feeder and basket look zoocraft:food 3). Enjoys forage and manipulate (basket, Enrichment Ball). Comfort advice (Austin, 0.1.44 confusion): adviceFor(need, species, ex.comfortCauses) names only what caps comfort; none left = "Nothing to fix ... recovering". Viewing Points: chooseViewed picks the exhibit at whose fence the point stands (viewing.besideRange 3), else the nearest with an animal in view (they flipped between exhibits). Elevated Browse Feeder: both material instances alpha_test (content log error). Test plan: docs/Ember_Fox_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.46 (2026-10-09, M4 part 3, Austin: "Next module. One thing, let's add another toy for nether creatures"): Lava Ray (spec 5.3), the Thermal Feeder, Molten Plankton Feed, and two Nether toys. The Lava Ray swims in lava (strider-style navigation, lava_movement, rise_to_liquid_level; wild in basalt deltas and Nether wastes, Thermal Carrier). New habitat profile lava_pool: 80 blocks for the first and 32 per extra (bank plus lava surface), at least 60% lava and 8 spots 2 deep. Exhibit measuring takes opts.swimLava (lava cells count, geometry lavaCells/deepLavaCells) for Lava Rays and for empty exhibits; an empty exhibit whose lava leads out is measured again as walkers see it (a lava moat). Rays drink the lava they swim in and need no Thermal Regulator (species.lavaSwimmer; equipment/rules drinksFromPool). Molten Plankton Feed (magma cream + 2 kelp -> 4; diet "plankton", ration.thermal, plural "Molten Plankton Feed" via rationPlural/rationCount) goes only in a Thermal Feeder (feeder record `thermal`, reached from lava cells: isReachable(cells, pos, "lava")); the Basic Feeder refuses it; baskets show it (zoocraft:food 4) and hang over lava too. Carriers release rays into the nearest lava cell; pups are born in the lava. Field Journal: spot, glide, drift (discovery inLava). Nether toys (balance.enrichment.speciesToys, species.toys): Blaze Bone (fixture type "bone", "dig") for Ember Foxes, Wither Skull Ball (type "skull", "manipulate", crafted with a wither skeleton skull) for Basalt Tortoises; nobody else plays with them. Clipboard, Field Journal and warnings say lava sets wooden fences on fire. Test plan: docs/Lava_Ray_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.47 (2026-10-09, Austin: "a lava type in the resource pack that can be seen through ... clear lava, it is formed when you put a bucket of lava into a lava filter"): Clear Lava. Bedrock add-ons can't add liquids (custom blocks only support water), so it's a block (zoocraft:clear_lava): blend render, animated flipbook texture, light 15, no collision and no selection box, not mineable; faces culled against more Clear Lava (RP/block_culling, condition same_block). Lava Filter (zoocraft:lava_filter, Zoo Workshop: copper, hopper, glass, blackstone, magma) turns a lava bucket into a Clear Lava Bucket; the bucket pours one block (into the first open space from the aimed face up; it doesn't flow) and an empty bucket scoops one up (equipment/lavaFilter.js, a line-of-sight walk since the block can't be aimed at). animals/lavaSwim.js, near players: things in it burn like lava (items burn up; players sink slowly and rise while holding jump); Lava Rays in it are steered by animals/glide.js (turn toward a random Clear Lava cell, glide forward in arcs, sometimes drift; walking speed held at 0 while in it, gravity cancelled with a self-trimming depth hold). Exhibits, sightlines and the Field Journal treat it as lava (data/blocks.js classifyBlock "lava", VIEW_THROUGH). Test plan: docs/Clear_Lava_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.48 (2026-10-09, M4 part 4, Austin: "next module when ready"): Striders (spec 5.1: "Striders | Nether thermal | Lava pool, warm resting zones, thermal transport"; build plan M4 "strider support"), the last Nether animal. zoocraft:zoo_strider is a zoo copy of the vanilla strider (vanilla geometry, skins and animations; caught from minecraft:strider in a Thermal Carrier; a saddle comes back and riders get off). species.lavaWalker (livesOnLava: rays or striders): new profile lava_shore (64 + 24 each, 40% lava at any depth, profile.onLava, minDryFraction 0.2 bank), measured with swimLava, drinks from the pool, no Water Filter warning. Warm resting spots: bask { minCells: 4, rest: true } (magma blocks on the bank; own warning and advice text). The entity is warm on lava or magma (environment_sensor in_lava / on_hot_block) and shivers with the vanilla cold skin (minecraft:is_shaking, render controller query.is_shaking) anywhere else. Food: plain warped fungus (RATIONS diet "fungus", plural Warped Fungi) in a Basic Feeder; shop sells it, doesn't buy it back; food look 5 on the Basic Feeder (states 0-3 and 5) and the Hanging Food Basket. Field Journal: spot, stride (walking across the lava), ashore (off lava and magma, shivering). Test plan: docs/Strider_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.49 (2026-10-09, Austin: "can you make the clear lava behave like normal lava, how it flows. The texture is perfect though"): Clear Lava flows like lava. equipment/lavaFlow.js has the rules, run on a pretend world in the tests: cells are a source, falling (zoocraft:falling state on zoocraft:clear_lava, full height) or flowing levels 1-7 (blocks zoocraft:clear_lava_flowing_1..7, 14 to 2 px tall, same texture, material and culling file); down first, sideways one level shallower a block (step 2, 30 ticks, toward a drop within 2 in the Overworld/End; step 1, 10 ticks, 4 in the Nether), flowing lava landing on lava stops, a source over a hole spreads sideways only with 3 source neighbours, flowing cells recompute from neighbours and dry up without a source; water above or beside: source to obsidian, flowing to cobblestone; falling onto water: stone; soul soil + blue ice: basalt; plants burn away (LAVA_REPLACES). equipment/clearLavaFlow.js runs it: a queue of cells due after lava's delay, filled by every lava write, player break/place, bucket use and bucket pours/scoops, capped at 40 updates a tick; every Clear Lava block also has minecraft:tick (sources 80-160, flowing 40-80) as a safety net after a reload. All Clear Lava is minecraft:replaceable. The bucket pours a source into open space or flowing Clear Lava and scoops sources only. Rays swim only in full blocks; burning, exhibits, sightlines and the Field Journal count every level as lava. Not like lava: stepped flat surfaces, no flow direction look, no fire spread. Test plan: docs/Flowing_Clear_Lava_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.50 (2026-10-10, M5 part 1, Austin: "give me the next 4 modules together to test. Still work on them 1 at a time"): Chorus Deer (spec 5.4: "Slender lavender grazer, branching antlers | Browses chorus growth in groups | Chorus browse, quiet retreat, herd room"), the first End animal. Original model (gen/chorusdeer.py: stepped neck, chorus-stem antlers on both sexes with glowing flower tips, two-segment legs; entity_emissive_alpha for the eyes and flowers) in two coats picked by minecraft:variant through the render controller (Lavender, 3 in 4 wild; Dusk, deep violet with cyan flecks). Wild herds of 2-4 spawn on end stone wherever has_biome_tag the_end, any light; wary of players who aren't sneaking (avoid_mob_type), tempted by Chorus Browse. Species: custom_end, homeDimension the_end, medium_terrestrial, cover like deer, and species.endGround { minCells: 8 }: a new "end" block category (data/blocks.js END_PATTERNS, read only when asked for like magma and ash; geometry countEnd/endCells; exhibits ex.endCells; welfare hasEnd, comfortCauses "end", COMFORT_FIX "end stone in the floor"; balance.endGround.comfortCap 55; care warning, clipboard Ground line and Field Journal line). Chorus Browse (3 chorus fruit -> 4 at a crafting table or the Zoo Workshop; diet "chorus", plural "Chorus Browse", shop 4 each, sells back 1): feeder and basket look zoocraft:food 6 (basic_feeder states 0-3, 5, 6). Stabilized Carrier (spec section 8: ender pearls above and below a Reinforced Crate braced with obsidian, at the Zoo Workshop; shop 240) is CARRIERS.stabilized, gated by the new research project Dimensional Preparation (4 species Ready to move; 2 ender pearls, 4 obsidian, 1 eye of ender, 250 coins). Breeding like deer (fawns). gen/sizes.py chorus_deer 0.95 (shoulder 1.0 m). Test plan: docs/Chorus_Deer_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.51 (2026-10-10, M5 part 2): Void Ray (spec 5.4: "Wide dark ray with a luminous underside | Glides through the air in slow arcs, rests on perches | Flight room, perches, chorus browse"), the first flyer. Original model (gen/voidray.py: three wing segments a side with trailing frills, curling head lobes, whip tail with a glowing spine and fin; entity_emissive_alpha so the star spots, wing rims and underside glow) in two coats by minecraft:variant (Void, 3 in 4 wild; Nebula). No gravity and no walking AI: animals/flight.js steers them a tick at a time near players with the Lava Ray's glide maths (glide.js FLIGHT, gravity 0; pitchOf for the nose): spots in the exhibit's flight room (exhibits.js cellsOf), a free Void Perch to settle on (one ray a perch, 15-40 s), the air over the feeder, hovers, loops the loop (8%), and anyone within 10 blocks holding Chorus Browse draws one down to hover over them; wild rays stay 2-6 blocks over ground. Three entity properties (zoocraft:gliding, resting, looping, client_sync) drive the client's fly/hover/rest/loop animation states and the Field Journal's "soar" and "loop" tasks (discovery flightSight). Species: custom_end, large_aerial { aerial, minPerches 2, minHeight 10 } (profiles.js AERIAL_HEIGHT), species.flies, endGround 16, stabilized carrier, chorus diet from a Basic Feeder on the floor. Flight room: geometry.js floodAerial, a six-way flood through the air from the seed (cells land/air/perch, flightCells, perchCells; usableArea = floor with minHeight air above it; an open top or a hole anywhere is an escape; open gates recorded). exhibits.js runs it as the exhibit's own flood for a flying species, and as a second look (ex.aerial { enclosed, usableArea, lowCells, escape, perchCount }, ex.flightCells, ex.perchCount) for an empty exhibit with a Void Perch inside its walls (hasPerchInside), so room.js habitatArea/aerialProblem can let a ray in; the walking flood treats perches as solid. Void Perch (zoocraft:void_perch, blocks.js PERCH_PATTERNS "perch" for readers that ask, VIEW_THROUGH): a floating end-stone ledge with a purpur rim and a crystal lamp (light 8; collision box the ledge; placement_direction), fixture type "perch" (rules.js serves: a "perch" cell over it and an "air" cell under it; fixtures.js message), 2 from an end rod, 3 end stone and 3 purpur at the Zoo Workshop. Baskets also serve in the air. Clipboard Ground line shows the flight room; Field Journal flight line; empty-exhibit advice. gen/sizes.py void_ray 1.2 (2.9 m wingspan), collision 1.5 x 0.5. 97 tests. Test plan: docs/Void_Ray_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.52 (2026-10-10, M5 part 3): Rift Ferret (spec 5.4: "Long curious animal with shimmering markings | Short puzzle-route teleports | Active containment anchor, hiding tunnels"), the third End animal, with the Containment Anchor and the Rift Tunnel. Original model (gen/riftferret.py: a long, low body in two halves, neck, head with muzzle, nose and round ears, four two-part legs, a three-part tail, four rift shards on the spine; entity_emissive_alpha so the eyes, the shimmer trail, the tail rings and the shards glow) in two coats by minecraft:variant (Twilight, 3 in 4 wild; Moonlit). Blinks: animals/rift.js every 10 ticks near players, with the pure rules in animals/riftRules.js (RIFT tuning, blinkSpot, landingOk, wildOffset): a zoo ferret blinks about every 20 s between floor cells of its exhibit's last measurement (exhibits.js cellsOf), mostly along the route of its Rift Tunnels (sorted by position; from a tunnel to the next, from elsewhere to the nearest), only with a charged Containment Anchor; every landing is re-checked on the spot (its cell and the one above open, solid ground under it); without charge or without an anchor it doesn't blink and park members nearby get a message once per 2 min (AC-11). Wild ferrets blink 5-9 blocks away when a non-sneaking player without Chorus Browse comes within 4, and now and then anyway; a nearby player holding a Stabilized Carrier or food holds a zoo one still. Teleport with minecraft:mob_portal + endrod particles and mob.endermen.portal. Three entity properties (zoocraft:rifted, hiding, peering; client_sync) drive the client (two controllers: move idle/walking/peer/hide and a blink overlay; peer stands it on its hind legs with a zoocraft:still component group holding it still; hiding = standing inside a Rift Tunnel, found by the script) and the Field Journal (discovery riftSight: spot, blink, peer). Species: custom_end, small_terrestrial, chorus diet, stabilized carrier, endGround 6, species.anchor and species.tunnels { min: 2 }. Containment Anchor (zoocraft:containment_anchor; items list: 4 end stone, 2 obsidian, 1 ender pearl, 1 redstone at the Zoo Workshop): fixture type "anchor" with units of charge like the Thermal Regulator (balance.anchor: capacity 24, lowAt 4, 2 a park day per exhibit, charge ender pearl 6 / eye of ender 12 / popped chorus fruit 2; rules.js chargeValue, isAnchored; serves beside the floor like a heater); zoocraft:fill 0/1/2 swaps the crystal texture and light (0/4/10); fitFor anchorProblem gates the move-in; care tick useStock anchorUsed; comfort cap 45 (welfare "anchor", COMFORT_FIX); care warnings; clipboard "containment anchor (N charge)"; Field Journal line. Rift Tunnel (zoocraft:rift_tunnel; 6 end stone bricks + 2 purpur -> 2): no collision (blocks.js PASSABLE, its cell is floor), placement_direction, light 5, fixture type "tunnel" (serves on the floor like the den; hasTunnels, balance.tunnel.comfortCap 55, warning, advice, clipboard count, journal line); the zoo entity walks into one with move_to_block (stay 240). gen/sizes.py rift_ferret 0.6 (0.35 m at the back), collision 0.5 x 0.4. 99 tests. Test plan: docs/Rift_Ferret_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.53 (2026-10-10, M5 part 4): End expedition and End research (spec phase 4 "End research quests"; section 4 stages "End discovery center: complete dimensional research and establish End access"). The End expedition (quests/expeditionSteps.js, pure; quests/expedition.js the driver) is the campaign chain after the caretaker tutorial: eleven steps (research Dimensional Preparation, craft a Stabilized Carrier, set foot in the End, spot all three End animals, bring home a Chorus Deer, lay its End ground, raise a flight room, bring home a Void Ray, charge a Containment Anchor, bring home a Rift Ferret, settle the End wing with nothing serious on the clipboards), each ticked off every 2 s from the park's real state (research records, members' inventories and dimension, Field Journal levels, exhibit, animal and anchor records; an End animal already at home proves the carrier and the trip), in order and never backwards (quests/steps.js now holds the general stepsDone/nextStep the tutorial also uses), one shared record "end_expedition" in the quests collection, the reward (balance.expedition.reward 300) paid once by operation id grant:end_expedition (AC-15). Shown on the Park Ledger's Journal page once the tutorial is done, and on the sidebar ("End expedition steps") and the overview once the first step is done. End research (data/research.js): project needs can now name an earlier project (`after`) and particular Field Journal pages (`species`: id -> level), rendered by discovery/rules.js projectNeeds (research.js passes the researched ids; data/validate.js checks the table). Anchor Tuning (after Dimensional Preparation, Rift Ferret ready to move; 4 ender pearls, 8 redstone, 4 glowstone dust, 240 coins): anchors hold 48 and use 1 a day (balance.research.anchorTuning; research.js anchorCapacity/anchorUsePerDay read by fixtures.js and the care tick). End Guidebook (after the Park Guidebook, the three End species observed; 8 paper, 4 chorus fruit, 2 ink sacs, 200 coins): guestFactor(endOnShow) adds 10% guests while an occupied exhibit holds an End animal (visitors.js). 101 tests. Test plan: docs/End_Expedition_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.54 (2026-10-12, Austin: content log on joining showed a Molang error in void_ray.animation.json): the Void Ray fly animation tilted the body with query.x_rotation, which is not a Molang query, so a gliding ray never tipped its nose up or down and the log showed two errors on load. Now query.target_x_rotation (the pitch flight.js sets with setRotation), clamped to 35 degrees as before, like every other head pitch in the pack. Also docs/Playtest_0.1.49_to_0.1.53.md: one playtest table for everything since Clear Lava started flowing (0.1.49 to 0.1.53).
- 0.1.55 (2026-10-12, Austin: "The field journal does not update for watch a ray gliding through lava, it should be clear lava"; "The ears on the deer look off, they go inward clipping through the head"): Lava Ray journal in Clear Lava: Clear Lava is a block, not a liquid, so the game's view raycast (getEntitiesFromViewDirection) can stop at the pool's surface and never reach the ray inside. discovery.js throughLava walks the line of sight (glide.js cellsAlong) when the raycast finds nothing, and rules.js alongSight (pure) picks the first lava-living animal within a block of the line with only air and lava in the way; the glide and drift task texts say "(Clear Lava counts)". Chorus Deer ears: earL/earR z rotation -12/12 leaned the tops inward into the head; now 35/-35 (positive z leans the top of a +x upright bone outward), clear of the antler bases; gen/chorusdeer.py matches. 102 tests.
- 0.1.56 (2026-10-12, Austin: "I need special end fences that can keep the rift ferret and other end animals inside so they cannt teleport outside the zoo area"): End Fence. Two blocks (gen/endfence.py): zoocraft:end_fence (end-stone-brick post, purpur cap and rails; a full block of collision) and zoocraft:end_fence_top (rift crystal spire, glowing beams, light 7, half a block of collision, hidden from menus), placed and removed together by equipment/endFence.js like the Keeper Gate, so the fence stands a block and a half and nothing jumps it. Four link states zoocraft:n/e/s/w draw the arms (bone_visibility) and the collision (15 permutations); endFence.js relinks a fence and its neighbours on place, break and every player block change (main.js fenceBlockChanged; rules.js fenceLinks: an arm toward End Fences, solid blocks, walls, fences and closed gates). Rift Ferrets never blink across one, wild or in a zoo: riftRules.js crossesFence walks the line between the two cells at the ferret's height and the block above, corner cells included, and rift.js rejects such landings (zoo blinks retry blinkSpot up to RIFT.tries). data/blocks.js reads both halves as a barrier (the validator closes a pen with it; guests see over it). Recipe at the Zoo Workshop: end stone brick, end rod, end stone brick in two rows -> 4. Use with an empty hand explains it; Field Journal care note on the Rift Ferret page. Textures reuse tunnel_brick, perch_trim and tunnel_crystal. 104 tests. Test plan: docs/End_Fence_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.57 (2026-10-12, M6 Polish part 1, Austin: "let's do the next module"): Conservation releases (spec 9 and 15; phase 5 "conservation quests"). quests/conservationRules.js (pure): releaseCheck (grown-up, not expecting, well, welfare 70+, 3 park days settled, the species' Field Journal at care notes, no social animal left alone, one release per species per 4 park days), siteCheck (home dimension, outside the park and 192+ blocks from its centre, the species' ground: open ground, end stone, snow or ice, or lava within 2 blocks for lava animals, air above), guestBoost (2% guests per release up to 10%), summarize, and the 7-step CONSERVATION_STEPS chain (care notes page, thriving group, zoo-born animal, a release, a zoo-born release, three species, Reintroduction Science; 250 coins once). quests/conservation.js (runtime): checkRelease, siteCheckAt, wildSpot, commitRelease (release record in the "releases" collection via quests/releases.js with a running summary; retireAnimal removes the animal record; 80 coins once by transfer id; messages; animalReleased event), tickConservation and conservationStatus like the expedition, releaseNote for the Field Journal. transport/carriers.js: packForRelease (exhibit -> carrier with transfer.purpose "release"; the lore says where to go) and releaseToWild (a carrier bound for the wild used outside any exhibit: site check, spawns species.wildSource with the animal's name and coat, transfer phase Released (journal.js), record retired); used inside an exhibit it houses the animal as before. UI: the animal's page on the Exhibit Clipboard shows a Conservation line and a Release to the wild button with a confirm; Park Ledger overview and Journal carry the totals and the chain; sidebar row; Field Journal care note. Research: Reintroduction Science (after the Park Guidebook and 1 release; paper, book, compass, 180 coins) halves the per-species cooldown to 2 days; research needs can name `releases`; guestFactor adds the reputation boost. 106 tests. Test plan: docs/Conservation_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.58 (2026-10-13, M6 Polish part 2, Austin: "It works, next module"): Sandbox mode and park roles (spec sections 2 and 19; phase 5 "multiplayer roles, sandbox options"). park/rules.js (pure): ROLE_RANK, NEEDS_ROLE, ROLE_LABEL and ROLE_TEXT (what each role can do, in the menus' words), allowed, denyLine (names the owner to ask), welcomeText (the line every player hears on joining a world with a park), memberRows (everyone who ever joined, online first, then by rank), canHandOver and handOver (the old owner stays on as a manager), and sandbox: sandboxOn, balanceText ("unlimited (sandbox; N banked)"), coinsLabel (the sidebar row becomes "Zoo Coins (sandbox)"), settingsText, sandboxLine, SANDBOX_TEXT. park.js: can and denyText through the rules; notePlayer/knownPlayers (the "players" collection now carries name and lastSeen, merged with gotDeed); transferPark; setSetting("sandbox", true) sets park.sandboxUsed for good. economy.js: in sandbox mode spend and charge write a 0 entry under the same operation id ("... (sandbox: free)"), so Finances lists what was taken and nothing runs twice; earn is unchanged. research.js: projectState opens every project free in sandbox mode and runProject takes no parts. UI: Park Ledger overview shows the balance text, the sandbox line and the role with what it allows; Members page (owner) lists every player who ever joined with role and online state, a page per player to give Visitor/Keeper/Manager or hand the park over (confirm), and tells the player if online; Settings page explains both toggles (sandbox, peaceful care) and whether sandbox was ever used, and tells members online when sandbox switches; Research Desk, Staff Room, Finances and the shop show the balance through balanceText; main.js welcomes every player with their role on spawning. Also: letting a Lava Ray or Strider go back to the wild now counts Clear Lava as lava (Austin: "Clear lava also has to work for lava ray exhibits"; the exhibit measure, Thermal Feeder and viewing already did). 108 tests. Test plan: docs/Sandbox_and_roles_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.59 (2026-10-13, Austin: "Fences glitch a bit, and they are hard to place while holding down the trigger"): End Fence placing. gen/endfence.py: the selection box (one box in Bedrock) is the bounding box of the post and its arms per permutation, like a vanilla fence's, so aiming beside a fence line hits the ground there instead of the fence's full-block box. equipment/endFence.js: beforeOnPlayerPlace sets e.permutationToPlace with the link states already worked out (linksAt/linkedPermutation), so a fence appears joined at once instead of its arms popping in a tick later; a fence placed against the crystal top of a standing fence (the natural aim with the trigger held) is cancelled and put on the ground beside that fence instead (placeBeside: the item is used up outside Creative, the stone placing sound plays), never floating beside the top; the top half is set with its beams in one permutation (finishFence, also used by the redirect; safe to run twice). Block files changed: full Minecraft restart. 108 tests. Test plan rows 16-18 in docs/End_Fence_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.60 (2026-10-13, Austin: "allow NPCs to travel up stairs and across bridges to different far away exhibits"): guest journeys. visitors/routes.js (pure): nearestNode (the network column within 2 blocks of a spot, feet one above the path block), routeBetween (breadth-first over the walked path network, so stairs, slabs and bridges are routes like flat paths), waypoints (turns and every step up or down), feetAt (half a block lower on a bottom slab), walkStep (a straight ramp between waypoints, facing the way), pickDestination (far spots favoured, unwatched ones first), routeTicks. visitors/guests.js: a strolling guest sets off every 6-20 s (balance.guests.tripAfterSeconds) to a Viewing Point that sees animals, a Snack Stand or a bench more than 6 blocks away on the network, within 160 route blocks (tripRange), walking 0.11 blocks a tick (walkSpeed) by teleport each tick (loop "journeys" in main.js, every tick) with patience of twice the walk; on arrival the vanilla goals take over again (watching at the point); leaving guests walk the network back to the entrance. New mode "travel" with the entity's zoocraft:travelling group (no move goals fight the script), the zoocraft:travel event, and a client-synced bool property zoocraft:walking that swings the legs and arms in guest.animation.json. network.js exports currentNetwork. Talking to a guest on the way says where it's going. Entity and animation files changed: full Minecraft restart. 109 tests. Test plan: docs/Guest_journeys_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.61 (2026-10-13, Austin: "Next slice", M6 part 3a): mixed-species exhibits. exhibits/mixing.js (pure): COMMUNITIES (Meadow: rabbit, deer, capybara; Wetland: otter, capybara; Savanna: giraffe, elephant; Basalt flats: basalt tortoise, ember fox; End meadow: chorus deer, rift ferret), communitiesOf, companions, canShare, companyText (the journal's line), mixProblem (why a release is refused), widestProfile, spaceFactor (balance.mixing.spaceFactor 1.25: "a quarter more room"). exhibits.js: ex.speciesIds (lead first; addResident/removeResident keep it), speciesIn/leadSpecies, profileOf(ex, extra) = the widest resident profile, capacity × spaceFactor with cap.mixed, fitFor checks mixProblem first, the measurement counts whatever any resident wants (cover/mud/magma/ash/end stone), a habitat check per species, "Crowded: ... of mixed species (a quarter more room)". animals.js: the care loop runs per species group (speciesGroups; own troughs, food, cover, heat...; shared fixtures and thermal failure once per exhibit; comfortCausesBy per species, causesFor); feederDaysLeft = the species that runs out first. staff.js: jobs per species group, one per target; the keeper feeds the species eating what the feeder holds. nursery.js: breeding follows the lead species; growth and births by each animal's own species. room.js noRoomReason(…, shares). carriers.js: sharing check before the release and for the catch. visitors.js/services.js/expeditionSteps.js count every species on show. clipboard: species line "Deer + Rabbit (shared: a quarter more room)", "(mixed)", breeding "(Deer, the first species here)", per-species advice, sharing advice on an empty exhibit. Field Journal: company line. 110 tests. Test plan: docs/Mixed_species_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.62 (2026-10-13, M6 part 3b): the nocturnal house and the Hedgehog. gen/hedgehog.py: an original Blockbench-style hedgehog (a dome of banded spines on short legs, a pale pointed face) in two coats (Brown, Blonde; minecraft:variant), walk, idle (sniffing, looking about, scratching), curl and sleep animations driven by the entity properties asleep and curled; zoo and wild entities (held still by the zoocraft:still event while asleep or curled); wild ones spawn in forests, plains and meadows in the dark. animals/nightRules.js (pure): sleepNow (asleep by Minecraft day, up at night; a nocturnal house turns that round), roofNeeded, isNocturnalHouse (balance.nocturnal.roofFraction of the usable floor under a solid roof, not leaves), nocturnalLine and stateText for the clipboard. animals/night.js: the loop (every 10 ticks, hedgehogs near players) sets the properties, holds them still, and curls an awake one for balance.nocturnal.startleTicks when someone who isn't sneaking comes within startleRange. exhibits/geometry.js roofedCells (opts.roofScan) and exhibits.js ex.roofedCells for nocturnal residents; platform/mc.js isNight. Burrow Shelter (blocks/burrow_shelter.json: a leaf-litter mound over a log with a dark way in, no collision; fixture type "burrow"; species.den "burrow" and equipment/rules.js denType/hasDen; care warnings, advice and the journal name the right den). Insect Hatchery (blocks/insect_hatchery.json: zoocraft:grown 0..3 raised by the block's own tick every 1200 to 1800 ticks; equipment/hatchery.js takes the batches out as Insect Rations) and the Insect Ration (RATIONS insect, FOOD_LOOK 7 with feeder and basket looks, sold in the shop too). A Viewing Point doesn't count an animal that is asleep (visitors/points.js entityAsleep), so the house is what puts hedgehogs on show. Discovery tasks forage and curl (discovery.js nightSight). Field Journal nocturnal, den and hatchery lines; clipboard nocturnal-house line, roofed count, burrow count, "Now:" on a nocturnal animal's page and hedgehog advice; mixing.js Hedgerow community (rabbits and hedgehogs). 113 tests. docs/Nocturnal_house_test_plan.md. Full Minecraft restart (new entities, blocks, textures).
- 0.1.63 (2026-10-13, Austin: "It should have to be a fully enclosed building with darkness at a certain level like a real zoo maybe we can have purple and red lights"): the real nocturnal house. The roof fraction goes; the measure is the game's own light on every floor block (exhibits/light.js measureHouse after the walk: sky light and light level per land cell, read with Block.getSkyLightLevel/getLightLevel; ex.house = { cells, daylit, bright, lit, brightest }). animals/nightRules.js: houseFromReadings, isNocturnalHouse = measured, no daylight on any block and nothing brighter than balance.nocturnal.maxLight (7), houseProblems and nocturnalLine say what's wrong (daylight reaches N blocks: solid walls and roof, doors shut; too bright on N blocks: torches, lanterns, glowstone) or how lit it is (blocks in lamplight; pitch-dark blocks where guests can't see). Nocturnal Lamps (gen/nightlamp.py): zoocraft:red_night_lamp and zoocraft:purple_night_lamp, light 7, a lantern on a plate placed on a floor, wall or ceiling (minecraft:block_face placement, transformation per face), crafted at the Zoo Workshop from glass, dye, iron nuggets and redstone (two a craft). visitors/points.js: a nocturnal animal in pitch dark isn't counted (night.js hiddenInTheDark), so the lamps are what put hedgehogs on show. Field Journal and clipboard texts follow. 113 tests. docs/Nocturnal_house_test_plan.md rewritten. New blocks and textures: full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.64 (2026-10-13, Austin: "We need to extend further than 128 blocks ... Let's make it scan within 500 blocks, do not take any shortcuts and get it done"): the 500-block park. balance.park.radius 500; park/rules.js boundaryRadius and park.js parkRadius: a park founded at 128 grows with the balance (the saved radius is a floor), so insidePark, exhibits, gates, points, services and the path walk all reach 500; conservation.minDistance 600 (the wild starts past the boundary). The path network with memory (visitors/paths.js rememberingReader, forgetColumn, columnsOf/columnsInto/columnsHash; network.js): chunks far from players are unloaded, so the walk falls back on the last network where the ground is unloaded and carries on, records the first unchecked edge (net.unloadedAt), forgets the path blocks a placed or broken block touches at once, runs from a remembered entrance even when its chunk is unloaded, re-runs when a player comes near the unchecked edge (paths.frontierRange 40) or every 30 s, and is saved in the "paths" collection (written only when it changed, a slice a tick) so the map survives leaving the world; maxNodes 50000, perYield 120. Park Menu "Guest paths" names the remembered count, the cap and the unchecked edge. Routes: routes.js routeSearch is a generator (searchPerTick 500 nodes a tick; routeBetween drains it for tests); guests.js planRoute runs it as a job. Far travel: a guest out of every player's range (or whose chunk unloaded) goes on as a ghost record (toGhost: entity removed, token key), keeps walking its route, strolling, watching, setting off and leaving on its own clock, and gets an entity again (materialize: same look, same errand) when a player comes within spawnRange of where it has got to; maxGhosts 12; tripRange 1200. Tests 114. docs/Guest_journeys_test_plan.md rewritten (0.1.64 rows), Conservation_test_plan.md distances updated. Scripts only: rejoin the world.
- 0.1.65 (2026-10-13, Austin on the decision card: "Guided tours", then "Let's add the void ray changes and tours as well next slice"): guided tours. visitors/tourRules.js (pure): tourShare (fair price 6, fewer buyers per coin over it, up to shareMax), stopsFactor (how much of the park the walk shows), tourSales (a share of each visitor step's guests, carried fractions, never past the seats left), seatsPerDay, guideLimit, orderStops (nearest first), planTour (a job over the path network: desk to each stop and home, unreachable stops left out, maxLength), tourTicks, legFeet, guideLine (what the guide says: who lives at the stop, how they are, their wild home), routeText. visitors/tours.js: Tour Desk records in "equipment" (problem outside/inside/path/stand/unchecked, price, soldToday, waiting, toursToday, spot), guide records in "staff" (role guide; wages owed/paid like keepers), the desk's route re-planned when the path network or the working Viewing Points change, tourStep from the visitor step (opId tour:<desk>:<step>), the walk (a run per guide: legs of feet positions, walkStep every tick, talk phase at each stop with guideLine to players within talkRange, followers trailing on a trail ring), the guide and up to 3 followers shown only near players (ghost pattern), initTours removes stray guide entities. ui/tourMenu.js at the desk only (hire, price slider, rename, dismiss). Tour Desk block (gen/tours.py: counter, map board, pennant; recipe paper/compass/red wool/planks), Tour Guide mob (guest geometry plus a flag bone on the right arm, two looks, flag sway animation; events idle/travel/talk). Park Menu "Tours:" line. balance.tours. 116 tests; docs/Tours_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.66 (2026-10-13, Austin's ideas 5 and 7: "Let's add the void ray changes and tours as well next slice"): the Void Ray changes. Giant adults: client scale 2.5 (young 1.25), collision 2.0 x 0.8 (young 1.0 x 0.4), large_aerial now 400 + 120 per extra ray with 14 blocks of air (old ray exhibits show "needs more room" until enlarged), FLIGHT a little faster and wider with a 2.5-block look-ahead. Taming and riding (animals/mount.js): the wild ray gets a "zoocraft:wild" group (despawn, tameable with Chorus Browse at 0.3, event zoocraft:on_tame) and a "zoocraft:tamed" group (is_tamed, persistent, family void_ray_mount so flight.js and the carriers leave it alone, rideable with two seats); flight.js gives pre-0.1.66 wild rays the wild group when first met; tickMounts flies a ridden ray from the front rider's inputInfo (forward/strafe along the view yaw, look pitch climbs and dives, jump rises, sneak dismounts), an idle tamed ray hovers in place and comes to Chorus Browse within 24 blocks, and a rider who gets off high up gets slow falling. End Cities (animals/endCities.js): every 30 s per player in the End with a shulker within 48 blocks, 1 to 2 wild rays spawn 6 to 12 blocks over a shulker while fewer than 6 are within 64, up to 8 per 64-block city a session. The Expedition (visitors/rideRules.js pure: rideShare/rideSales like tours, rideSeatsPerDay = rays x 6 rides x 4 seats, loopStops nearest first within 160 blocks up to 6, planLoop climb/stops/return/land at max(platform + 12, highest roof + 8), rideTicks, loopStep, loopText, stopLine; visitors/rides.js: "platform" equipment records with price/soldToday/waiting/ridesToday/rayIds, checkPlatform (park, exhibit, path, 16 blocks of open sky), assign/release rays, rideStep from visitors.js with opId ride:<platform>:<step>, runs board 6 s -> fly -> hover 5 s per stop -> land -> rest 30 s -> home, a numeric ride every tick with the ray shown within 64 blocks and nudged by impulses (teleport if 4 blocks behind), guest riders as zoocraft:guest entities addRider'ed, park members ride free via "Ride now", welfare >= 60 and 6 rides a day per ray, animals.js watch() skips a.onRide, homing on reload; ui/rideMenu.js at the block only; the zoo ray entity has 4 guest seats and main.js cancels other interactions on it). Expedition Platform block (deck, lamp posts, sign; light 8; recipe end rod, Chorus Browse, end rod / purpur x3 / end stone bricks x3) from gen/rays.py. Park Ledger "Rides:" line. 118 tests. Test plan docs/Void_Ray_changes_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.67 (2026-10-13, Austin's 0.1.66 content log and "Void ray is also not flying", a wild ray summoned on superflat): fixes. Both ray mobs lose minecraft:can_fly (new in 0.1.66; most likely the hover move control re-enabled gravity whenever the ray had no path, so the script's lift lost to it); animals/push.js moves flown rays by impulse and carries a ray by short teleports (checkForBlocks) once it has been told to move into open air and stayed still for a second (pure pushState, flight.js and the unridden mount use it). Tour Desk flag render method opaque (one render method per block; its texture has no see-through pixels). Expedition Platform recipe minecraft:end_bricks (Bedrock's End Stone Bricks). guest.json path list drops cobblestone_stairs (Bedrock's is stone_stairs, already listed). Tests check every block uses one render method, recipes carry no Java-only ids, the rays have no can_fly. 120 tests. Test plan docs/Void_Ray_fixes_0167_test_plan.md (full restart).

- 0.1.69 (2026-10-14, Austin on 0.1.68: "They move and turn around and stuff, they just never change XYZ coordinates", no flight errors in the log): the game ignores the rays' impulses, so rays move by teleport steps. animals/mover.js flyStep (the step, else level, else vertical, only into air at the position and 0.45 above; keepVelocity false; rotation with it) used by flight.js (flying and resting), mount.js (idle, called and ridden, with reseat putting dropped riders back and logging it once) and rides.js (moveEntity/holdEntity set the ray at the ride's position each tick). /zoocraft:rays (commands.js, flight.js rayReport): rays near the player with position, state, spot, last step and velocity, and the flight errors so far. keeper.json gains the zoocraft:walking property its shared guest walk animation reads (four Molang errors). Tests: no applyImpulse in the ray movers, stepOptions, and every client mob's query.property reads exist on its server mob. 122 tests. Test plan docs/Void_Ray_moves_0169_test_plan.md (full restart). Lava Rays (lavaSwim.js) and the deer prance still use impulses: asked Austin whether Lava Rays still swim.
- 0.1.70 (2026-10-14, Austin on 0.1.69: "They wont move, detection also fails", /zoocraft:rays "0 Void Ray(s)" beside them; "figure this out through research, do not take shortcuts"): root cause found. Since 0.1.66 flight.js wakeWild gave wild rays without a tameable component the zoocraft:wild event, which removed the "tamed" group; removing a component group removes its components outright, even ones in the base components (Microsoft, Entity Behavior Introduction, "Removing Components"), and the tamed group holds minecraft:type_family, so the rays lost "void_ray" and the family search never found them again (0.1.69's "game ignores impulses" was wrong: none were sent). wild_void_ray: zoocraft:wild event removes nothing and adds zoocraft:wild + new zoocraft:wild_family (the wild family). Rays found by type (platform/mc.js entitiesOfType/entitiesNear, one search per type a tick; animals/rayKinds.js RAY_TYPES, isTamedRay by is_tamed/rideable, hasFamily) in flight.js, mount.js and endCities.js; wakeWild gives the event to any untamed wild ray lacking its wild group or its family. animals/mover.js flyStep pushes again (clearVelocity + applyImpulse, as 0.1.51-0.1.65) and checks each open-air push against the distance moved: a ray carried under 25% over 20 pushes (1.5+ blocks asked) moves by teleport steps from then on, logged once. rides.js back to 0.1.68 (nudge, teleport if 4+ behind). Taming announced (main.js dataDrivenEntityTrigger zoocraft:on_tame -> mount.js tamedRay message). Same flaw in hedgehog, wild_hedgehog, rift_ferret, wild_rift_ferret: zoocraft:move removed "still" and the movement component with it; new zoocraft:moving group (0.2 / 0.28), events swap still/moving; rift.js gives a ferret "move" when first met. /zoocraft:rays (animals/rayReport.js) lists every loaded ray by type with kind, families, state, push/step and the family-search counts. Generators gen/rays.py, hedgehog.py, riftferret.py match. Tests: no event removes a base component without adding it back (all mobs), the wild ray's family, rayKinds, the push check with fake rays, the hedgehog/ferret groups. 126 tests. Test plan docs/Void_Ray_family_0170_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.71 (2026-10-14, Austin: "It works! Next slice", then "Animal wave" on the card: raccoon, then flamingo, then zebra): the Raccoon (spec 5.x "raccoon | Raccoon | Manipulates puzzle feeders | Climbing; attracted to unsecured bins"; spec 6 "climbing species need their own validator"), the first climber. exhibits/geometry.js floodClimb: from every floor and water cell the walking measure reached, a climber clings to the side of anything but glass and glass panes (data/blocks.js climb readers: log, glass, pane, frame), climbs up and down, pushes through leaves, steps over the top of what it holds and drops to the first place it can stand, swim or hold; it can't hang under a ceiling, so an inward overhang or a roof stops it. The first block it reaches outside the exhibit's footprint is the way out (path, escape; via: the top of the wall, a tree's branches over it, or a hedge), and it counts climbing trees (3 or more logs beside the floor; a 2 x 2 trunk is one); balance.climb.rise 24. exhibits.js measures climbers' exhibits and empty ones: one a resident could climb out of is unsafe with the issue text, a raccoon can't be released into one, and the clipboard has a Climb-proof line. animals/climbRules.js (pure): climbIssue, climbLine, hasClimbTree, canWash, trunkBeside, climbRoute, waterNear, binRaid, stepsBetween, upLegs, downLegs, escapeLegs, pickInterest, hugFor. animals/raccoon.js: every half second a raccoon near a player picks something to look for (the entity's seek_* events swap in a move_to_block for logs and Climbing Frames, water, Puzzle Feeders or Litter Bins), then climbs (zoocraft:climb: no gravity, a glide a tick at a time, letting go if something is built in its way), looks out from the top by day, naps there at night, clings under leaves, comes down head first, washes at the water, works a stocked Puzzle Feeder, raids Litter Bins at night when it's loose, and climbs out of an exhibit that isn't climb-proof along the measured way out and on down to the ground; kits hug nearer the bark (species juvenileScale). The act shows through the int property zoocraft:act. Species: diet fruit, insect or fish; the Fruit Ration (1 apple, or 2 sweet berries, glow berries or melon slices, makes 2; in the shop); comfort capped without a climbing tree or Climbing Frame (55) or water to wash in (60); drinks from its pool; enrichment manipulate, climb, forage; Field Journal spot, climb, wash. Puzzle Feeder (a crate with a holed lid, slider, pegs, levers and a drawer; a feeder animals work at: manipulate enrichment while it holds food they eat). Climbing Frame (the native multi_block trait, 3 parts up: a bark post with rope bands, pegs and a platform; fixture "frame", counts as a climbing tree, breaks whole into one item). Litter Bins: zoocraft:latched and zoocraft:tipped states (a padlocked hasp; lying on its side with litter spilled), the Bin Latch item; a raccoon knocks an unlatched bin over (its fill and balance.raccoon.scraps onto the paths) and a knocked-over bin catches nothing until stood up; emptying an unlatched bin says so. gen/raccoon.py: an original raccoon (hunched back, black mask with white brows and muzzle, white-edged ears, dark hands and feet, a bushy ringed tail) in Grey and Cinnamon, 11 animations (walk, scramble, idle with sniff, look, face rub and stretch, climb, cling, down, lookout, sleep with closed eyes, wash, puzzle, rummage) with the climbing poses on their own bones; the fruit look for feeders and baskets (zoocraft:food 8). gen/sizes.py takes species names now (a full run would shrink the giant Void Rays). 130 tests. Test plan docs/Raccoon_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.72 (2026-10-14, the Animal wave, second animal): the Flamingo (spec 5.2 "flamingo | Flamingo | Flock wading | Shallow water and mud nesting | Expansion"; spec 9 breeding needs "suitable nesting"). Habitat profile wading (amphibious, 30% water, 25% dry bank, 16 spots of water 1 block deep: room.js says how many shallows there are; deeper water they swim in and don't need). exhibits/geometry.js countShore: usable floor on mud right beside the water (level or a block lower) is a nest site (ex.nestSites, the first 64 in ex.nestSpots), measured for species with a nest. Species: wild in mangrove swamps, swamps and warm beaches by day in flocks of 4 to 8, walking away from anyone within 8 blocks who isn't sneaking or holding Flamingo Pellets; Small Carrier; social 3 to 16; diet pellet or insect; Flamingo Pellets (beetroot, dried kelp and a raw cod or salmon make 4; in the shop) are the pink ration. Pink (a.pink 0 to 100, caught birds 100): a park day eating mostly pellets +34, any other -20; rosy from 70, fading from 35, else pale, shown through the int property zoocraft:pink (three skins); peaceful care still colours them by what's in the feeders; chicks are grey and grow up pale; care warnings and the animal page say why. Breeding (species.nest): a flock of 6 grown-ups (each Flock Mirror counts as 2 more, up to twice the flock), 2 or more nest sites, every grown-up rosy; the pair builds a Flamingo Nest on the free site nearest the mother (animals/nursery.js keeps the block: a bill-scraped heap the first park day, the mound with one chalky egg, then hatched with bits of shell), the parents sit on the egg in turns, the chick hatches on the nest and is fed crop milk bill to bill, and the nest crumbles back to the mud when the chick grows up. animals/flamingoRules.js (pure): shadeOf, pinkAfterDay, pinkLine, pinkWarnings, flockLine, pickNestSpot, nestStage, sitterTurn, pickMood, footing, pickAct, standable, marchPath, walkPath, turnBack, yawTo, flocksOf. animals/flamingo.js: each flock (an exhibit's birds, or wild birds close together) has a mood (wade, rest, display); wading it filter-feeds in the shallows head upside down or stamps; resting it stands on one leg or preens; displaying it head-flags, gives wing salutes and now and then marches together one way, turns sharply and marches back; a keen flock (breeding on and big enough, or a Flock Mirror) displays more. The engine swaps wading (walking on the bottom, no float) and swimming (afloat) by depth through an environment sensor and the bool property zoocraft:swim. The Flock Mirror (a fixture at the water's edge: an iron stand with a glass panel showing a pink reflection). gen/flamingo.py: an original Greater Flamingo 1.5 m tall (an S-neck in four segments, a bent pink bill with a black tip, coral wing coverts and black flight feathers that show in the salute, long pink legs with the backward ankle), in rosy, fading and pale, and a grey chick with a straight bill; 14 animations (walk, march, swim, idle with wing-and-leg stretches and head shakes, filter feeding, stamping, preening, resting on one leg with the head laid back, head-flagging, the wing salute, building on the mound, sitting on the nest, feeding crop milk, the chick); the nest in three stages, the mirror, the pellet look for feeders, baskets and Puzzle Feeders (zoocraft:food 9). 132 tests. Test plan docs/Flamingo_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.73 (2026-10-15, Austin on 0.1.72: "Hedgehog looks terrible. Cannot even see a face or legs, and its puny" and "Also do research on Flamingo walking and standing. Flamingos stand on only one foot"): the Hedgehog redrawn from photos of European hedgehogs (gen/hedgehog2.py; gen/hedgehog.py still writes everything else of it).
