Bigger: 0.83 blocks nose to tail and 0.38 high (was 0.5 by 0.3), collision box 0.6 x 0.45. A low dome of banded spines (dark brown flecked with cream tips, lying back in rows, 15 tufts standing out) from a hood over the forehead down to a skirt over the legs; a pointed face under the hood (black eyes with a shine painted on its front corners, paler cheeks, small brownish-pink ears, a tapering snout on its own bone, "snout", with a glossy black nose); short furry legs showing 3 units under the skirt with dark paws and pale claws; a stub tail. Curled: bone "ball" (a ball of erect spines with tufts) shown instead of the hedgehog, its nose and eyes (bone "peek") coming out after 2.4 s. Asleep: on its belly, chin and snout on the ground, eyes shut (bone "lids"), front paws out beside the snout, hind legs folded under. A hoglet's own coat (soft pale spines, pinkish face): the render controller picks Array.coats[query.is_baby ? 2 : query.variant]. Same animations, controller and properties (asleep, curled). The Flamingo stands on one leg (Chang and Ting 2017, PMC5454233: the standing foot right under the body, the leg leaning in; photos): idle, preen and rest fold the other leg up out of sight under the body (thigh swung forward, shin folded back), swapping legs now and then; the wing-and-leg stretch reaches back from one leg. Walk and march: the ankle (the "backward knee") folds the foot back under the body as it lifts, toes curled, higher steps in water; stamp and build the same way. gen/render_pose.py: a positive pitch looked up from below; it looks down now. Tests: the hedgehog's size, bones, coats and part visibility, the flamingo's tucked leg and ankle. 132 tests. Test plan docs/Hedgehog_flamingo_looks_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.74 (2026-10-15, the Animal wave, third animal): the Zebra (spec 5.2 "zebra | Zebra | Coordinated herd movement | Savanna mixing and broad grazing | Expansion"; items list "Hay Rack | Block | 2 | Wall-mounted grain and browse feeder for paddock and herd animals" and "Grain Ration | 1 | Farm animals, horses, zebras | Wheat, hay bale"). Research and design in research/zebra_plan.md. Habitat profile grassland (160 blocks for the first, 48 more for each, 4 high). Broad grazing: data/blocks.js has a "grass" category (grass blocks) for readers that ask for it; exhibits/geometry.js countGrass counts usable floor standing on grass (ex.grassCells, measured when a resident grazes); species.graze { perAnimal 30, share 0.5 }: 30 grass blocks each (a foal half) give them up to half their food (the satiety drain is that much lower, so the feeders last longer: feederDaysLeft and the Feeder low warning follow), and with too little grass comfort is capped at 55 (cause "graze", balance.graze.comfortCap); grazing never changes a block. Harems (species.harem): one stallion with his mares and foals; two or more grown stallions with grown mares are rivals (animals/breeding.js rivalStallions): comfort capped at 45 for the herd (balance.zebra.rivalComfortCap, cause "harem"), a care warning naming them, no breeding, and the nursery warns when a colt grows up into one; a bachelor group (no mares) is fine. Rank at the food (species.feedRank): the stallion, then the mares in the order they came to the exhibit, then any second stallion; feeding places at the feeders holding their food (balance.zebra.places: Basic Feeder 2, Puzzle Feeder 1, Hay Rack 4): grown-ups beyond them, the lowest-ranking first, are pushed off the food (satiety no higher than 50, simulateAnimal ctx.satietyCap), and the care warning, the animal page ("At the food: 3 of 3 in rank, pushed off: room for 2 at the feeders") and their advice say so. The Hay Rack (zoocraft:hay_rack): a slatted wooden rack on a back board with a tray, iron brackets under it, hung on the fence a block above the floor (or stood on the floor), facing the one placing it; a feeder (record `rack`) that holds only hay, Grain or Leafy Rations; zebras, deer and elephants eat from it (species.rack, equipment/rules.js reachesFeeder), reached from the floor a block below it or level with it (exhibits/geometry.js isReachable dys); its hay shows how full it is (zoocraft:fill 0 to 2: low in the tray, or heaped up behind the slats with wisps pulled out between them), golden for grain and green for leafy (zoocraft:food 10 or 0); sticks, planks and a hay bale at the Zoo Workshop. The Grain Ration (hay with a little grain mixed in, as zoos feed zebras): 2 wheat make 2, a hay bale makes 9, at a crafting table or the Zoo Workshop; in the shop; the grain look in Basic Feeders, Hanging Food Baskets and Puzzle Feeders (zoocraft:food 10). Species: wild in savannas by day in herds of 3 to 6 (on grass, coarse dirt or dirt), moving off from anyone within 8 blocks who isn't sneaking, coming to a Grain Ration, keeping together with no one near (a wild zebra drifts toward a herdmate within 12 blocks till 6 off; an exhibit's zebras don't, their pen holds them); Livestock Crate; social 3 to 10; Burchell's and Grant's coats; a caught zebra keeps the sex it had in its wild herd (zebraRules.js wildSex: about a third are stallions); one foal; the savanna community with giraffes and elephants. animals/zebraRules.js (pure): roleOf, rankOrder, feedingPlaces, pushedOff, grassNeeded, grazeShare, hasGrass, namesOf, herdWarnings, herdLine, rankLine, pickMood, isMidday, fileOrder, fileTarget, filePace, findWay, smoothWay, clearLine, wayLength, pointAlong, yawTo, turnToward, besideOf, footprintOf, placeRect, overlapDepth, crowding, cellsUnder, threatOf, headingsAway, headingIndex, runLine, herdsOf, wildSex. animals/zebra.js: every half second each herd near a player has a mood: an exhibit's zebras, or a wild herd (each within 16 blocks of the next, zebraRules.js herdsOf), met whole once one of it is within 48 blocks of a player and kept till 56; a wild herd keeps its mood, fright and calm when it comes apart or joins up again. Grazing: spread out heads down with a slow step on now and then, up to the feeders and racks in rank order (the low-ranking ones wait when there's no room; foals don't go, they live on milk) and to the trough; the stallion herds a straggler back (head low and snaking); two greet nose to nose; foals nurse at the mother's left flank and play in bursts (bucking, or a gallop round her); rival stallions face off, rear, strike and bite, and it ends with a kick out behind; bachelors spar; each comes round on its time when those in it are standing about or grazing (they look up for it), or is tried again a few seconds later. Trekking: the herd walks off in single file (the mare with the youngest foal in front, each foal behind its mother, the stallion at the rear); the others walk up to where the lead set off, each once the one in front of it is on its way, and follow in its steps, trotting to catch up, never closer than their gap (3 blocks, a foal 2); one standing or grazing in the file's way steps off it, and at the end they step clear of one another and graze. Resting (more around midday): dozing on their feet, grooming pairs side by side facing opposite ways, resting pairs head to tail with a chin on the other's back, one lying down, maybe for a roll in the dust and a shake. Night: lying down, some flat out asleep, one standing watch in turns. Someone sprinting up who isn't sneaking (6 blocks from a zoo herd, 16 from a wild one), a hurt zebra (even in the calm after a fright, or as they stare), or now and then nothing at all startles the herd: one barks, the rest look, they gallop off together the same way, front first (one right behind another sets off a moment later; one with a tree or a rock in its way swerves round it), ease to a trot and a walk, then stop clear of one another, turn and stare, the stallion nearest the threat; then they stay calm for half a minute. A part of a wild herd cut off from the rest calls (a bark) and walks back to it, several in single file, while the rest stay about. A newborn's mother keeps the others off on its first park day. Room: each zebra's footprint (body, and neck and head) is measured off the model standing, bucking, lying and asleep; none stands, walks or lies in another: one about to graze, doze or lie down where another is steps off a block or two first (one boxed in by others lying round it steps out between them; a wild one never settles within 8 blocks of someone who isn't sneaking, who would only move it on again), one walking somewhere goes round those standing in its way, two setting off the same way go one behind the other, where two ways cross the one lower in rank stops to let the other by, one strolling about stops short of another (and grazes there), and one standing about with another in it steps off; close together on purpose only (nursing, grooming, resting head to tail, meeting, fighting). Turning, a zebra steps round, never snaps round; only one walking about where the engine takes it can be pushed. Walks, trots and gallops glide a tick at a time at the animations' stride speeds (0.94, 2.25 and 4.95 blocks a second) over the exhibit's measured floor or open ground (a breadth-first way, corners cut); everything else is the engine's walking, given back whenever the script is done (zoocraft:move / zoocraft:still swap groups that both hold movement and physics). What each zebra does goes to the client through the int property zoocraft:act (0 to 26). Field Journal: spot, graze (only while really grazing), file, groom. gen/zebra.py: an original plains zebra 1.3 m at the withers (32 units a block, client scale 0.5) with jointed legs (upper arm, forearm, cannon and hoof; thigh, gaskin, cannon and hoof), an upright mane, a tufted tail, and a jaw, ears and eyelids of their own; stripes painted in 3D after a side photo of a Burchell's zebra (fine and upright over the neck and shoulder, broadening and turning back over the flanks and croup, a fan on the haunch, bands down the legs and onto the belly, arcs round the muzzle and jaw) on 512 x 256 skins: Burchell's (brown shadow stripes, paler lower legs), Grant's (crisp black and white to the hooves) and a brown and cream foal (a bigger head, longer legs, a fluffy mane). Animations: a four-beat walk, the trot and the gallop from a gait solver (gen/zeb_gait.py), the stride kept in time with the ground speed when the engine runs them; idle (breathing, flies swished, busy ears, looking about, sniffing, a fly shaken off, a forefoot stamped, a hind leg rested); grazing; alert; barking; dozing; grooming; resting a chin; shaking; nursing; play; sparring; fighting and the two-legged kick (hooves gathered up off the ground, kicked out and set back where they stood); drinking at a trough; eating at a Hay Rack hung up or on the floor and at a feeder; greeting; herding; standing watch; and lying down, lying, rolling, settling to sleep, waking and getting up from a pose solver (gen/zeb_poses.py), so hooves, knees and hocks stay where they touch the ground; the legs' layers (a hind leg rested, a forefoot ahead or stamped, down on both knees) play by weight outside the controller so a leg leaves a pose the way it came in. 134 tests. Test plan docs/Zebra_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.75 (2026-10-15, Big predators, first slice): secure keeper access and the Lion (spec 5.2 "lion | Lion | Pride rest and scent investigation | Group dens, secure service access | Expansion"; items list "Meat Ration | 2 | Lions, tigers, crocodiles, wolves", "Scent Post | Block | 2 | Scent trails for wolves, lions, foxes" and the Keeper Gate's "two in a row make the double service gate"; Austin's cards "Big predators" and "Warn, then swipe", and "Also make them very dangerous in the wild"). Research and design in research/predators_plan.md and research/predators_facts.md. Everything below keys off species flags (dangerous, dens, fasts, ledge, manes), never a species name, so the tiger (0.1.76) and the crocodile (0.1.77) reuse it. The Meat Ration (zoocraft:meat_ration, diet "meat", 1 unit): one from each raw beef, mutton, porkchop, chicken or rabbit at a crafting table or the Zoo Workshop; in the shop (4 coins, bundles of 8); its look in Basic Feeders, Hanging Food Baskets and Puzzle Feeders (zoocraft:food 11). The Holding Den (zoocraft:holding_den with zoocraft:holding_den_top; stone bricks, iron and a tripwire hook at the Zoo Workshop): a two-block concrete den set into the fence with its steel guillotine door facing the exhibit (it faces away from the player placing it), a mesh window with eyes glinting behind it while an animal is shut in, and the keeper's winch, padlock hasp and danger plate outside; an equipment record ("holding": pos, facing, held, exhibitId), linked to the exhibit whose measured floor is in front of its door (predatorRules.js denServes); used from behind only (from the door side it says so), staff only: "Call one in" (the nearest loose grown animal of the exhibit trots to the door and goes in: entity group zoocraft:denned, hidden, held still, no collision, door down with a clank), "Let <name> out" (onto the ground in front of the door, facing in), and, with a Reinforced Crate in hand, "Crate <name>" (transport/carriers.js crateFromDen; the den empties, nobody let out); breaking a den lets its animal out first. Dens needed: one per grown animal plus species.dens.spare (lions 1: "three dens for two cats", the Scottish DWA guidance); cubs need none. Too few: comfort capped at 60 (balance.predators.dens.comfortCap, cause "dens") and a care warning. The double Keeper Gate: a Keeper Gate in the fence and a second outside it, in line, 2 to 4 blocks apart (predatorRules.js servicePairs; gate records "gate" kept by predators.js gateRecord), measured into ex.service; the interlock (equipment/fixtures.js, predators.js interlockText): while one gate of a pair is open the other won't open ("one at a time"), and one open gate of a pair doesn't count as an open exhibit (airlockHolds). Danger ("Warn, then swipe", predators.js tickPredators every 10 ticks, predatorRules.js dangerStep): only a loose grown animal of a dangerous species in an exhibit, only toward a player in Survival or Adventure standing inside that exhibit's measured cells: within 8 blocks it warns (crouched, ears flat, a growl, the action bar line), within 5 for 3 seconds (6 sneaking) or within 2.5 at once it charges for up to 2 seconds, and within reach it swipes once for 6 damage (3 hearts) and a 1.2 shove, then backs off 6 blocks and won't swipe that player for 10 seconds (5 after a miss); out of the exhibit or 12 blocks off, it calms down. Keepers (staff/staff.js): a dangerous exhibit's jobs need the double gate and enough dens (keeperStop, else the keeper gives up with the reason and it's a care warning); in view, the keeper first calls every loose grown animal into a den each (predators.js shiftForKeeper, the status "Shifting the lions into their Holding Dens (1 of 3 in)", then "Counted: all 3 lions shut in"), gives up after 90 park seconds if one won't come, then appears just inside the inner gate and works; when the job ends every animal that keeper shut in is let out (releaseAfterKeeper), not those a player shut in. Fast days (species.fasts): one park day in 7 the keepers skip feeding and the satiety drain is a quarter (balance.predators.fastEvery, fastDrain). The Scent Post (zoocraft:scent_post; logs, string and a fern at the Zoo Workshop): a weathered post with a rope coil, scent stains and claw marks, a fixture of type "scent" (enrichment "scent"), a barrier to walk round (data/blocks.js), never a ledge. The Lion (zoocraft:lion, wild zoocraft:wild_lion in savannas by day, prides of 3 to 6 on grass, coarse dirt or dirt): habitat profile "pride" (220 blocks for the first, 60 more each, 4 high), Meat Rations, Reinforced Crate, social 2 to 8, enrichment scent, manipulate and forage, shade over a tenth of it (cover "shade"), ledges (a floor block 1 or 2 higher than the floor beside it, lionRules.js ledgesOf, measured into ex.ledgeCells: 2 for each grown lion, else comfort capped at 60, cause "ledge"), coat 0 a lioness and 1 a lion (sexByCoat), a light or dark mane (record mane, entity mark_variant, scale 0.5 or 0.575, cubs 0.24); a pride holds a coalition of 2 grown males at most (more: comfort capped at 45, cause "coalition", a warning, no breeding); litters of 1 to 3 cubs as room allows (species.breeding.litterMax), a cub's mane from one parent; cubs are no danger (never warn or charge, crated loose, no den). Crating (carriers.js, predatorRules.js crateBlock): a wild one only while it eats a thrown Meat Ration, a zoo one only from its den; released ones stay calm 30 seconds. The wild (lion.js huntStepLion, lionRules.js huntStep): a pride spots anyone in Survival or Adventure within 16 blocks by day and 24 at dusk, by night and at dawn (half that sneaking; never on Peaceful) with an action bar line, stalks low and slow with the lionesses fanning out 45 degrees apart round them (balance.lion.hunt.flankDeg) and a low growl within 12, charges from 8 blocks or after 10 seconds at 6 blocks a second, a male holding back roaring 4 seconds first, and bites and swipes for 8 damage (4 hearts) every 1.5 seconds within reach; it keeps after them till they're 8 blocks beyond its range, rests winded 15 seconds after 20 of charging, and a lion hurt by a player turns its pride on them for 30 seconds. A Meat Ration thrown within 16 blocks: the nearest wild one trots to it, eats it lying down (the item gone) and stays calm 15 seconds (predators.js tickWildBait, ateBait, isCalm); picked up first, it gives up. animals/lionRules.js (pure): ACT, PACE, TICKS, roleOf, wildRole, dayPart, pickMood, pilePlaces, ledgesOf, ledgesNeeded, prideWarnings, huntStep, wildLine. animals/predatorRules.js (pure): dangerStep, dangerLine, denServes, densNeeded, shiftPlan, allShutIn, servicePairs, interlockedBy, keeperBlock, fastDay, baitNear, crateBlock. animals/lion.js: every half second each pride near a player (an exhibit's lions, or wild lions within 16 blocks of each other) has a mood by the time of day (dawn, day, midday, dusk, night; balance.lion.moods): resting (the pride walks to a ledge, or a quiet spot, and lies down side by side touching, a grown male off the end, on their chests, then flat out on a side or now and then on the back, yawning; a lioness with cubs lies on her side to nurse), social (rubbing heads, licking, sitting together), patrol (a walk to the Scent Post or along the ground: sniffing, the flehmen grimace, spraying with the tail up, scraping with the hind feet, clawing the post), roar (each grown one stands and roars at dusk and before dawn, the cubs sit) and play (cubs crouch, wriggle, pounce and bat; a cub keeps within 6 blocks of its mother); zoo lions go to the feeders holding meat and the troughs. What predators.js asks comes first (a den call, a warning, a charge, backing off); walks, trots, gallops and the stalk glide a tick at a time (tickLionMoves) at the animations' speeds over the measured floor or open ground. What each lion does goes to the client through the int property zoocraft:act (0 to 25). Field Journal: the danger and wild lines in red, care notes (Meat Rations and fast days, shade, Holding Dens, the double gate, crating, ledges, the pride, the Scent Post); tasks spot, pile, scent, roar. Clipboard: keeper access (the double gate, dens for those needed, shut in, ledge blocks) and the danger line; the lion page shows the mane, lion or lioness, and "Now: shut in a Holding Den". gen/pred_world.py: the Meat Ration, its feeder looks, the Holding Den and the Scent Post. gen/lion.py: an original African lion (a lioness 1.07 m at the shoulder, a male 1.23 m) with jointed legs (shoulder, elbow, wrist, paw; hip, stifle, hock, paw), claws shown when it strikes, a jaw, tongue, lids and ears of their own, the male's mane, ruff and bib, a tufted tail; four skins (the lioness, a blond-maned and a dark-maned lion, the spotted cub). gen/lion_anim.py and gen/lion_poses.py: the walk, trot, rotary gallop and stalk from the gait solver, and from the pose solver the warning crouch, the swipe, the roar, lying on the chest, side and back and the ways between them, sitting, rubbing heads, licking, sniffing, the flehmen grimace, spraying, scraping, clawing a post, drinking, eating at a feeder and lying down with meat, nursing and suckling, yawning, and the cub's stalk, wriggle, pounce and bat, so paws, elbows and hocks stay where they touch the ground. 137 tests. Test plan docs/Lion_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.76 (2026-10-15, Big predators, second slice): the Tiger (spec 5.2 "tiger | Tiger | Solitary patrolling and swimming | Privacy and separate adult territories"; Austin's cards "Big predators" and "Warn, then swipe", and "Also make them very dangerous in the wild"). Research and design in research/predators_plan.md ("Tiger (0.1.76)") and research/predators_facts.md. It reuses 0.1.75's secure keeper access whole (Holding Dens, the double Keeper Gate, keepers who shift them first, Warn then swipe, fast days, crating only from a den or while a wild one eats), keyed off species flags; the new flags (solitary, privacy, patterns, sexLook, dens.cubsFollow) name no species either, and data/validate.js checks they go together (solitary needs dangerous, dens and cubsFollow and not prefersCompany; patterns a whole number with sexLook and no sexByCoat; privacy needs solitary). The Tiger (zoocraft:tiger, wild zoocraft:wild_tiger: Bengal tigers in jungles and Amur tigers in cold taigas, alone and rare, on grass, podzol, dirt or snow; the spawn rule's herd event zoocraft:wild_amur gives the taiga ones the Amur coat): habitat profile "tiger" (200 blocks for the first, 100 more each, 4 high), amphibious: a pool, 8% of the floor or more, with 6 spots 2 deep to swim and 4 spots 1 deep "to lie in" (exhibits/room.js and the Field Journal take the profile's shallowWhy and shallowUse); Meat Rations, Reinforced Crate, social 1 to 5 (happy alone; up to 5 in an exhibit, a pair and their cubs), enrichment scent, manipulate and swim, shade over a tenth of it; coat 0 Bengal and 1 Amur (minecraft:variant), the sex on the record and the mob (minecraft:mark_variant 1: a male, scale 0.572 against a tigress's 0.5, with a full white cheek ruff; a cub 0.225), and three stripe patterns a coat (record pattern, minecraft:skin_id; animals.js createAnimal picks one, spawnInExhibit and a release fire zoocraft:sex_N and zoocraft:pattern_N before zoocraft:juvenile, and a caught one keeps its own: carriers.js reads them off the wild mob); litters of 1 to 3 cubs, a cub's stripes its own. A Holding Den each (dens.spare 0), and a tigress's cubs go in and come out with her (dens.cubsFollow; animals/predators.js: the den record's `cubs`, cubsWith, hideInDen, outOfDen; denOf finds a cub in with its mother; keepInDen re-hides a mob respawned in a den and puts a newborn in with its mother when she's shut in; letCubOut lets a cub that grows up out of its mother's den; crating the tigress from her den lets her cubs out first: vacateDen(den, id)); callAnimalIn for one tiger by id; keeperBusy while a keeper is shifting or working. Living alone (animals/tigerTurns.js tickTurns every 2 seconds, tigerRules.js groupsOf, turnPlan, turnSteps): only one grown tiger is loose at a time (a tigress with her cubs counts as one; an orphan cub is no group), the others shut in their Holding Dens (marked `turn`), a different one out each park day: the ones out of turn are called into the nearest free den first, and only when they're all in is the one whose turn it is let out (only those the turns shut in: never one a player or a keeper shut in); one let out on the wrong day is called back in; nothing turns while a keeper is busy; on its own it's let out. The clipboard's turns line ("Turns: Raja is out today; Mira (out tomorrow) in a Holding Den"; "Mira and her cubs are out today"); the den's menu says "In for the turns" and names the cubs in with her. Two grown tigers loose together (but for a pair on their introduction day) fight (rearing up face to face and swatting, hissing, breaking off and going again): a care warning, comfort capped at 40 (balance.tiger.solitaryComfortCap, cause "solitary") and no breeding. Introductions: with breeding on, one park day in 4 (balance.tiger.introEvery) a grown male and a tigress without cubs are let out together instead of taking turns, the day and the two who met go on the exhibit (ex.turns.introDay, introIds), and for 2 park days after it (introWindow) only that pair can breed (animals/breeding.js: "Tigers meet only to mate ... The next one is in N park days", or "they're related: they won't pair"; nursery.js breedingStatus passes clash, intro, introEvery and introWindow). Privacy (exhibits/geometry.js summarize with opts.screenRead, a climb-aware block reader): a floor block is screened with 2 or more blocks of rock, earth, leaves or tree trunk round it at head height (tigerRules.js isScreened; glass, panes and fences don't hide them), measured into ex.screenCells and ex.screenSpots (the first 128); 6 for each grown tiger (balance.tiger.screenPerTiger), else comfort capped at 60 (privacyComfortCap, cause "privacy") and a care warning; the clipboard's Privacy line. animals/tigerRules.js (pure): TIGER_TYPES, ACT, DOWN_ACTS, TICKS, PACE, sizeOf, swimY, roleOf, wildRole, pickMood, groupsOf, introPair, turnPlan, daysToTurn, turnSteps, clash, turnsLine, screensNeeded, isScreened, tigerWarnings, looking, huntStep, wildLine. animals/tiger.js: every half second each tiger near a player (alone; a tigress shares her mood with her cubs) has a mood by the time of day (dawn, day, midday, dusk, night; balance.tiger.moods): rest (to screened floor out of sight, a ledge or a quiet spot, lying on its chest, flat out on its side asleep or now and then on its back, grooming, yawning; a tigress with cubs lies on her side to nurse and the cubs suckle), patrol (along the fence to the Scent Post or a tree, marking: the cheek rub, rearing up to claw it high, spraying it with the tail up, scraping with the hind feet, sniffing and the flehmen grimace, rolling on its back), swim (into the pool, across it with its head up held at the surface by its zoocraft:afloat group, no gravity, and out on a bank), soak (lying in the shallows up to its neck, most at midday), roar (rare, at dusk and dawn) and play (cubs stalk, pounce and bat); zoo tigers go to the feeders holding meat and drink at the pool's edge or a trough; they chuff (soft puffs through the nose) to a player standing still outside the fence within 6 blocks, to their cubs, and when let out of a den. Sounds: mob.cat.hiss sparring and warning, step.grass as a frozen tiger's grass rustles, mob.ravager.roar on the rush and the roar (pitch 0.5 a male, 0.58 a tigress), mob.horse.breathe for the chuff, mob.polarbear.idle and a cub's mob.cat.meow now and then. The wild (tigerRules.js huntStep, balance.tiger.hunt; never in Creative or Spectator, never on Peaceful): a wild tiger spots a player within 20 blocks by day and 28 at dusk, by night and at dawn (half that sneaking), with the action bar line "Something is watching you from cover: keep facing it and back away (a tiger creeps up only while your back is turned)"; it creeps up while their back is turned (the stalk, 0.5 blocks a second) and freezes flat when they look at it (within 50 degrees of their view; the grass rustles within 10 blocks); stared down for 8 seconds it slinks off 12 blocks and leaves them alone 30 seconds; within 10 blocks behind them, or 6 whatever they do, it rushes with a coughing roar at 7 blocks a second (faster than a sprint), leaps on them once from 4 blocks (10 damage, 5 hearts, and a hard shove) and bites for 8 (4 hearts) every 1.5 seconds within reach; a rush lasts 4 seconds, then it rests 15 panting; it gives up 10 blocks beyond its range; hurt by a player it comes at once and keeps at it 30 seconds; it swims after them. A Meat Ration thrown within 16 blocks: it eats it lying down and is calm (and crateable) while it eats, then 15 seconds. What each tiger does goes to the client through the int property zoocraft:act (0 to 32: the lion's numbers where a tiger does the same, its own from 26: swimming, soaking, the ambush, the pounce, chuffing, rolling, sparring). Field Journal: the danger and wild lines in red, care notes (living alone and the turns, introductions, privacy, the pool and shallows, swimming and chuffing, a tigress's cubs in the den with her), tasks spot, mark, swim and ambush (discovery.js tigerSight). Clipboard: keeper access, the turns, privacy and the danger line; the tiger page says tiger or tigress, and a cub "shut in a Holding Den with its mother". gen/tiger.py: an original tiger (a tigress 0.91 blocks at the shoulder, long and low, a male a seventh bigger with his cheek ruff) with jointed legs (shoulder, elbow, wrist, paw; hip, stifle, hock, paw), huge forepaws, claws shown when it strikes, a jaw, tongue, lids and ears of their own, small round ears black behind with a white spot, and a long tail ringed black to a black tip, hanging just clear of the ground; stripes painted in 3D (upright and doubled or forked on the body, meeting over the spine, denser toward the rear, across the legs and forehead, sweeping back on the cheeks) on six 256 x 128 skins: Bengal (deep orange, black stripes) and Amur (paler, longer fur, browner stripes spaced wider), three patterns each; cubs striped from birth. gen/tiger_anim.py and gen/tiger_poses.py: the walk, trot, rotary gallop and stalk from the gait solver, the swim (a dog paddle, the head up), and from the pose solver the warning crouch with the threat face (the ears twisted round so their white spots face forward), the swipe, the ambush freeze flat to the ground, the pounce (gathering, the leap with the forelegs reaching out either side of the jaw, landing), the roar, lying on the chest, side and back and the ways between them, soaking, sitting, the cheek rub, rearing up to claw high, sniffing, the flehmen grimace, spraying, scraping, rolling, drinking, eating at a feeder and lying down with meat, nursing and suckling, grooming, yawning, chuffing, sparring, and the cub's stalk, wriggle, pounce and bat, so paws, elbows and hocks stay where they touch the ground. Also for lions: a player swimming in a dangerous exhibit's pool counts as inside it (predators.js: the cell above the swimmer's feet), and a lion that walks to its resting place lies down from the tick it arrives, so its rest isn't cut short (lion.js restStep). A mock-world run of tiger.js, tigerTurns.js and predators.js (zoo, turns, cubs, danger, wild and bait scenarios, several seeds) found and fixed: walks into the shallows stopping a step short, no swimming where shallows ring the deep water, cubs gliding on after a den shut on them, the first bite landing before the pounce, back-to-back bites without a fresh swipe, a chase that couldn't reach the edge of the spotting range, the rush timer running while it got up, snap turns before marking, chuffing and sparring (now a turn on the spot first), and the turns line with nobody in a den. 140 tests. Test plan docs/Tiger_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.77 (2026-10-15, Big predators, third slice): the Crocodile (spec 5.2 "crocodile | Crocodile | Basking and submerged resting | Secure keeper access, shore clearance"; Austin's cards "Big predators" and "Warn, then swipe", and "Also make them very dangerous in the wild"). Research and design in research/predators_plan.md ("Crocodile (0.1.77)") and research/predators_facts.md. It reuses the secure keeper access of 0.1.75 whole (Holding Dens, the double Keeper Gate, keepers who shift them first, Warn then swipe, crating only from a den or while a wild one eats), keyed off species flags; the new flags (basks, submerges, shoreClear, meals, rank, sizes, sandNest, dangerMoves) name no species, and data/validate.js checks they go together (a bank and deep water need an amphibious profile; shore clearance a dangerous species; meals every 1 or more days below a satiety between 0 and 100; sizes a whole number with sexLook; a sand nest needs sites, 2 or more days expecting and no flamingo nest). The Crocodile (zoocraft:crocodile, wild zoocraft:wild_crocodile in swamps and mangrove swamps, one or two together, weight 2, at most 3 around): habitat profile "crocodile" (150 blocks for one, 45 more each, 2 high, amphibious, 40% water or more, 35% dry, 4 spots 2 deep), Meat Rations, Reinforced Crate, social 1 to 6 (happy alone), enrichment forage and swim, shade over a tenth of it; one coat (the Nile crocodile's), its sex on the record and the mob (minecraft:mark_variant 1: a male, scale 0.655 against a female's 0.5, with a broader head), its own size class (record size 0 to 4, the int property zoocraft:size: each 4% apart; animals.js createAnimal picks one, spawnInExhibit and a release fire zoocraft:size_N, and a caught one keeps its own: carriers.js reads the property) and three tail crests (minecraft:skin_id); hatchlings a tenth of a female's size that grow through five stages (zoocraft:grow, from juvenileDaysLeft). The measure (exhibits/exhibits.js, animals/crocRules.js measureCroc, with a croc block reader: data/blocks.js classifyBlock tells plants, zoo fixtures, sand, red sand and rough ground apart only when asked): the sunny basking bank (usable floor within 3 blocks of the water, open to the sky 12 up, not gravel, cobblestone, raw stone or logs: ex.sunBankCells, ex.sunBankSpots nearest the water first), the shore ring right beside the water and what clutters it (plants at foot or head height, a fixture at the edge: ex.shoreCells, ex.shoreBlocked, ex.shoreSpots), shade near the water (ex.shadeSpots), sand nest sites 2 to 5 blocks from the water (ex.sandSites, ex.sandSpots) and the inner Keeper Gate's distance from the water (ex.gateToWater). Basking: 4 sunny bank blocks for each grown one, taken in size order; the smallest go without when it's short: their comfort capped at 55 (cause "bank") and a care warning naming them. Deep water: 4 spots 2 deep for each grown one and 1 for each young one, else comfort capped at 50 (cause "submerge") and a warning. Shore clearance (predatorRules.js keeperBlock, predators.js keeperStop, crocRules.js shoreBlock): keepers go in only with the shore ring clear and the inner Keeper Gate 4 or more blocks from the water; the clipboard says what's wrong and where. Meals about once a week (animals.js, crocRules.js mealDrain, wantsMeal): a grown one's satiety drains at a seventh of the rate and it eats only when it's down to 75, then until it's full (about 2 Meat Rations a week); the young eat every day; the welfare loop goes biggest first, so with too little meat the smallest goes without; a female guarding her nest eats nothing; a meal's start (a.mealTick) is played for anyone near. Rank by size (crocRules.js sizeOrder, meet, rankLine): the clipboard's "Rank: Kubwa 4.2 m, Mara 3.1 m, and 2 young"; each crocodile's page gives its length. The sand nest (animals/nursery.js makeSandNest, syncSandNest; crocRules.js crocNestStage, pickSandSpot, hatchSex): breeding needs sand to nest in; a female that starts expecting gets a "nests" record (kind "sand") on the free site nearest her, the sand floor block swapped for the Crocodile Nest block (zoocraft:croc_nest, states zoocraft:stage 0 the hole with the eggs, 1 filled in flush, 2 hatched with the shells, and zoocraft:red for red sand) and back to sand two park days after the hatching; at the hatching the nest's warmth sets each hatchling's sex (open to the sky: about 4 in 5 males, Hutton 1987; shaded: all females) and the message says which; the hatchlings appear on the nest. animals/crocRules.js (pure): CROC_TYPES, ACT, DOWN_ACTS, WATER_ACTS, TICKS, scaleOf, lengthOf, bodyOf, growStage, paceOf, floatY, sizeOrder, meet, rankLine, pickMood, bankPlaces, bankNeeded, shortOfBank, deepNeeded, openSky, sunnyBank, measureCroc, gateToWater, shoreBlock, shoreLine, crocWarnings, mealDrain, wantsMeal, daysToMeal, mealsLine, crocNestStage, pickSandSpot, hatchSex, hatchLine, huntStepCroc, grabDamage, wildLine, SPOTTED_LINE. animals/croc.js: every half second each crocodile near a player has a mood by the time of day (balance.croc.moods): bask (to its own spot on the sunny bank in size order, the biggest nearest the water, lying with the jaws gaping), shade (gaping in the shade at midday), halfout (lying at the waterline, the back end in the water), float (eyes, nostrils and a line of scutes out, held at the surface by its zoocraft:afloat group, drifting now and then), submerge (sinking backward to the bottom, resting there, rising nose first), swim (cruising) and display (a grown male at dawn and dusk with a female about: the head lifted and the tail arched, a bellow with a bubble fountain, the jaw slap; with breeding on he swims to a female and they rub jaws); into the water by a belly slide, out in the high walk; rain (the weather changes, kept on the world) and nights keep them in the water; a smaller one meeting a bigger one lifts its snout and moves off, two males of a size face up and hiss. Meals: to the feeder, reared up snapping the meat off it, into the water for the death roll, then three head tosses to swallow. The nest: she digs it with her hind feet, lies guarding it (now and then in the water near it), and at the hatching carries each hatchling to the water in her jaws; the hatchlings keep near her, float, bask, chirp and gallop. Danger in the exhibit: the threat gape (reared up, puffed, the jaws wide, a long low hiss), the charge (a belly run, or a fast swim), the sideways snap. Sounds: mob.cat.hiss at pitch 0.35, mob.ravager.bite, mob.ravager.ambient at 0.45 and bubble.up for the bellow, random.splash, mob.frog.ambient at 1.9 for the chirps, block.turtle_egg.crack, mob.sniffer.digging, random.eat at 0.5 for the gulps. The wild (crocRules.js huntStepCroc, balance.croc.hunt; never in Creative or Spectator, never on Peaceful): a wild crocodile spots a player within 12 blocks by day and 18 at dusk, by night and at dawn (half that sneaking), with the action bar line "Something is watching you from the water: keep back from the edge (crocodiles lunge from the water and drag you in)"; it stalks under the water toward them, surfacing eyes-only to look; within 5 blocks of it and 3 of the water it lunges out at 8 blocks a second; within reach it bites (8), holds them in its jaws, drags them into the water and rolls (3 rolls, 3 damage each); hit, it lets go and backs off 10 seconds; a miss, 5 seconds; a swimmer it chases at 5 blocks a second (faster than a player swims); on land within 6 blocks it gapes and hisses, within 3 it lunges and snaps, further off it slides into the water; hurt on land it chases 3 seconds at 3.5 blocks a second, never more than 6 blocks from the water; hurt by a player it goes for them 30 seconds. A Meat Ration thrown near one: it gulps it down, calm (and crateable) while it eats, then 15 seconds. What each crocodile does goes to the client through the int property zoocraft:act (0 to 31). Field Journal: the danger and wild lines in red, care notes (the basking bank, under the water, shore clearance, meals, rank, nesting and the warmth that sets the sex, the display), "Hatchlings need no den" (the young's plural from the species), tasks spot, gape, roll and lurk (discovery.js crocSight). Clipboard: keeper access, shore clearance, the basking bank, deep water, meals, rank and the danger line (species.dangerMoves: "warn ... then charge and snap sideways"); a crocodile's page says male or female and its length; "Guarding her nest". gen/crocodile.py, croc_poses.py, croc_anim.py: an original Nile crocodile (a female 3.05 m, a male 4 m) with a three-part trunk, an 8-part tail, jointed legs, a jaw with interlocking teeth, lids and the third eyelid, the nuchal and keeled dorsal scutes, the double and single tail crest; the high walk, the belly run and a hatchling's gallop from the gait solver (each loop solved until its last frame meets its first, the remainder spread across the stride), and from the pose solver the bask, the threat gape, the snap, the lunge, the drag, the death roll, the swallow, the float, sinking and rising, the display, the snout lift, digging, guarding, carrying a hatchling, a hatchling being carried and chirping. A mock-world run of croc.js, predators.js and nursery.js (zoo, rain, nest, danger, wild, bait and comfort-cap scenarios, 4 seeds each) found and fixed: a carry cut short leaving the mother holding her hatchling, a meal stopping at the feeder (no death roll), a dive's continuation firing after a warning cut it short, young ones moving at a hatchling's size whatever their growth (and the clipboard's length), rain not sending a basking one into the water until its mood ran out, a hit on land never answered with a chase and a held player let go only after all the rolls, a respawned player pulled back to the jaws, and a croc dipping into the bank coming out of the water. 143 tests. Test plan docs/Crocodile_test_plan.md (full restart).
- 0.1.78 (2026-10-16, fix): the crocodile's drag and snap animations failed to load ("Precomputed cubic interpolation requires keyframes have constant data", and Austin was kicked from the world). A catmullrom key must hold plain numbers, but gen/croc_anim.py merge() wrote the drag's head shake (Molang) into the neck's catmullrom keys and joined two numbers as text ("-6 + -8") in the snap's. merge() now adds numbers as numbers and makes a key that picks up Molang linear (the keys are a frame apart, so the motion is the same); the drag's neck keys are linear and the snap's are numbers. A test now checks every catmullrom key in every animation file holds only numbers, and check-pack reports one as an error. 144 tests.
- 0.1.79 (2026-10-16, Treetop trio, first slice): the Red Panda (spec 5.2 "red_panda | Red panda | Elevated travel and resting | Continuous climbing routes, nest boxes"; Austin's card "Treetop trio", and "The details also does not need to be so high on the designs. This is minecraft remember" with "New ones only": a blocky model, one or two boxes a body part, the detail in the pixels; real average size, head and body 0.58 m). Research and design in research/treetop_plan.md and research/treetop_facts.md. The Red Panda (zoocraft:red_panda, wild zoocraft:wild_red_panda in bamboo jungles, one or two together, weight 12, at most 3 around): habitat profile "arboreal" (40 blocks for one, 16 more each, 5 high), Leafy Rations (bamboo), Small Carrier, social 1 to 2 (happy alone), enrichment climb and forage, one coat (the western red panda's); 2 or 3 cubs, born buff-grey (their own skins), scale 0.35, grown in 3 park days. It climbs like the raccoon (species.climbs: the exhibit has to be climb-proof; climbTree), and the new flags name no species: species.canopy (the climb measure, exhibits/geometry.js floodClimb with opts.full, goes on past a way out and counts the canopy: open blocks on top of leaves, a log or a Climbing Frame, 2 or more up, over the inside, reached by climbing; 6 for each grown one, else comfort is capped at 55), species.nestBox (the new Nest Box block, zoocraft:nest_box: planks and sticks at the Zoo Workshop, no collision, turned the way it's placed; one for each grown one, on the floor or up in a tree level with the canopy and right beside it, which the climb measure finds; else capped at 55) and species.heatShade (the biome at the exhibit's floor, platform/mc.js biomeAt: in a desert, savanna or badlands, shade over a third of the floor, else capped at 45 with "Too hot: red pandas suffer above about 25 C"). animals/redpanda.js (rules in animals/pandaRules.js; the raccoon's climbing legs, climbRules.js upLegs and downLegs, serve both): active at dawn, at dusk and for a spell around midnight (up a trunk and on up through the leaves onto the canopy, trips along it stopping to look about, back and down head first; sat up at the feeder chewing a stalk of bamboo; grooming; cubs play); through the day draped over a branch at the canopy's edge (sprawled panting in a hot biome); at night curled up with the tail over the face, on the canopy or in a Nest Box with the head peeking out of the hole; cubs stay in the Nest Box their first park day, twittering. A Survival player right beside one on the ground: it stands up on its hind legs, arms raised, and hisses (no harm). A wild one goes up a tree when someone comes near, and eats bamboo where it grows. Field Journal tasks: spot, headfirst, drape, tailwrap; care notes for the canopy, Nest Boxes, the heat and the food (the raccoon's Litter Bin note is only the raccoon's now). Exhibit Clipboard: canopy, Nest Boxes and heat lines, nest boxes in the equipment line. Test plan docs/Red_panda_test_plan.md (full restart: new entities, a new block and new textures). A mock-world run (zoo, hot, warn, wild, cubs and escape, 4 seeds each, whole days) found three things in animals/redpanda.js, fixed: a Nest Box entered from the canopy took the mood it climbed in and was left at once; a resting one lay down on the floor on its way to a tree or a Nest Box (now only when it got nowhere); one met again up in the trees (no player near for a while, a reload) or let go there was stranded on the canopy with gravity on (fromCanopy: it finds a trunk's route down, or comes down through the leaves). 150 tests.
- 0.1.80 (2026-10-17, Treetop trio, second slice): the Gorilla (spec 5.2 "gorilla | Gorilla | Social interaction and puzzle solving | Strong fixtures and climbing structures"; Austin's card "Treetop trio", the blocky style for new animals, real adult size: a silverback 1.25 blocks at the shoulder on all fours, a female 0.8 of him). Research and design in research/treetop_plan.md ("0.1.80 Gorilla") and research/treetop_facts.md. The Gorilla (zoocraft:gorilla, wild zoocraft:wild_gorilla in jungles, not bamboo jungles, 2 to 4 together) travels in a Reinforced Crate, eats Leafy and Fruit Rations (3 a park day) and is baited with a Fruit Ration; habitat profile "troop" (200 blocks for the first, 50 each more, 5 high). It's dangerous (Holding Dens, one each, an infant going in with its mother; the double Keeper Gate; "Warn, then swipe" inside the exhibit as a chest beat, a charge and a shove). The troop (species.troop): one silverback with the females; two with females are rivals (comfort capped at 45, no breeding; a young male growing up is told to move), a bachelor group of males is fine up to 4. Strong walls (species.ape): the ape measure, exhibits/geometry.js floodApe beside the raccoon's floodClimb (they now share the outline and the way out): a gorilla grips trunks, Climbing Frames, leaves and fences (a new "rail" category for the ape reader only: blocks.js RAIL_PATTERNS; stone walls, bars and the End Fence stay barriers), wades but never swims, and reaches up 3.5 blocks (BALANCE.gorilla.reach) onto a top beside it (a fence or wall block's top counting half a block more) or to a grip; walls 4 high of solid blocks or glass hold one, 3 don't, a frame or branches reaching the wall are a way out (hanging on by its hands it reaches only a block and a half more, so branches a block short are not). An exhibit a gorilla could get out of is unsafe and one won't move in (ex.apeClimb; the clipboard's "Strong walls" line); one living there gets out along the way out now and then (park members told where) and is loose: it bluffs like a wild one and is crated only while it eats a thrown Fruit Ration (crateBlock `loose`). No deep water (species.noSwim): water 2 or more deep caps comfort at 40 with a warning. The predators' bait is each species' own now (a Fruit Ration for gorillas, Meat Rations still for the cats and crocodiles), and only wild mobs get the hunt and calm events. A wild silverback bluffs (Austin's card, "Bluff"; gorillaRules.js bluffStep): within 10 blocks of someone in Survival or Adventure the display (hoots, chest beat, ground slap), within 6 a charge that stops 2.5 off, within 3 or after they hurt one of the troop a real charge and a shove for 3 hearts, then he lets them be. animals/gorilla.js: the troop's day (gorillaRules.js dayPart, BALANCE.gorilla.moods): feeding morning and afternoon (sat eating at a feeder, a Puzzle Feeder worked with a finger, browsing leaves beside the floor), travelling behind the silverback, a midday rest round him while the young play, grooming, the females and young climbing a trunk or frame to sit on top (up and down feet first), a night nest built at dusk and slept in; the silverback's display now and then; an infant rides on its mother's back its first 2 park days (zoocraft:ride, carried each tick). Field Journal tasks: spot, chestbeat, nightnest, picking; the wild line and care notes. A wild one only charges for real if the player steps in after the display starts, or hurts one. Checked in a mock world (a troop's day, escapes from weak pens, the bluff, baiting and crating, rival silverbacks); 157 tests. Test plan docs/Gorilla_test_plan.md (full restart: new entities and textures).
- 0.1.81 (2026-10-17, Treetop trio, third slice): the Owl, the Eurasian eagle-owl (spec 5.2 "owl | Owl | Nocturnal flight and perch watching | Enclosed airspace, nesting boxes", and the items list's "Perch"; the blocky style for new animals, real adult size: a female 0.72 blocks to the top of the head perched and 1.75 blocks wing tip to wing tip, a male 0.88 of her). Research and design in research/treetop_plan.md ("0.1.81 Owl") and research/treetop_facts.md. The Owl (zoocraft:owl, wild zoocraft:wild_owl in taiga, dark forest and windswept hills, alone or a pair) travels in a Small Carrier, eats Meat Rations (1 a park day) and is baited with one; habitat profile "aviary" (16 blocks of floor for the first, 8 more each, 4 blocks of air, roofed: the flight measure, exhibits/geometry.js floodAerial, as for the Void Ray). The Perch (zoocraft:perch, two from logs and sticks at the Zoo Workshop): a wooden T-perch placed on the floor, a log, a wall top or a ledge; its bar is the only part that collides; the flight measure sees it as a perch (blocks.js PERCH_PATTERNS) and the fixture "roost" serves wherever it stands with open air over it (Void Rays still use only Void Perches). An aviary needs 2 Perches; with no two of them 2 or more blocks apart in height, comfort is capped at 50 (AZA 2019: perches at varied heights). An empty exhibit with Perches in it is measured for flight at an owl's 4 blocks of air (ROOST_HEIGHT; a Void Perch still asks the ray's 14). Owls lay their eggs only in a Nest Box (breeding.nestBox; 2 or 3 eggs, 3 park days), the female sitting on them in the box; owlets (grey-buff down, 0.6 of a female) stay in the box their first 2 park days, their mother brooding them and at night fetching food from the feeder, they begging at her from dusk to dawn; then they hop and flutter about the floor and the low perches; grown in 6. animals/owl.js: by day it roosts on the highest free Perch, eyes half shut (open when someone comes within 5), the engine turning its head to follow people (look_at_target), preening now and then; at dusk a bout of hoots ("oo-HU", note.flute, the female's higher), its mate answering; at night it watches from a perch, then flies to another or swoops down to the feeder and mantles over the food; at dawn back to the roost and a pellet. Someone in Survival or Adventure within 3 blocks of one on the ground, on a low perch or in its Nest Box gets the threat display (wings spread, hissing, bill snapping; no harm). Flights go through the aviary's measured air (owlRules.js airWay: a search through the flight cells, never cutting a corner, then straightened), with a take-off, wingbeats and glides, and a landing feet first, pushed a tick at a time by animals/mover.js flyStep as the Void Ray is. In the wild it roosts in a treetop near where it was found, flies from tree to tree at night through open air it checks (up over what's in the way, or round it through the gaps: owlRules.js seekWay), pounces on the ground now and then; a Meat Ration held within 12 blocks (or an empty Small Carrier with one on you) draws it down to land 2 blocks away, where it can be crated once the Field Journal is done; come within 4 of one up a tree and it flies off, within 3 of one on the ground and it displays first. Field Journal tasks: spot, hoot, pounce, pellet. Fixed with it: a roofed flyer's exhibit counts all its floor as sheltered (the flight measure counted no cover, so a Void Ray could never breed: "a sheltered corner"); and six species whose group size left no room for a pair and their young never bred ("the group is full"): red pandas now up to 4, hedgehogs 5, Ember Foxes 5, Lava Rays 4, Rift Ferrets 5 and raccoons 5 (a test now checks every species). An owl met in mid-air (the world saved it mid-flight) flies off to land first, and one out of everyone's range finishes its flight; the note when the game ignores pushes names the mob, not always "a ray". Checked in a mock world (an aviary pair through two days and nights, eggs to grown owlets, wild owls in taiga with the flush, the display, the bait and the crate, and the step fallback when the game ignores pushes); 162 tests. Test plan docs/Owl_test_plan.md (full restart: new entities, a new block and textures).
- 0.1.82 (2026-10-18, fix): both owls (zoocraft:owl, zoocraft:wild_owl) failed to load ("minecraft:pushable: this component was found in the input, but is not present in the Schema"), so no owl appeared. Minecraft 1.26.10 split minecraft:pushable into minecraft:pushable_by_block and minecraft:pushable_by_entity and stopped reading the old one (Microsoft's 1.26.10 update notes). The owls now have pushable_by_block only (pistons can push one, players and mobs can't nudge it off a perch), like before. A test checks that no entity lists minecraft:pushable, and the pack checker now reports it as an error. 162 tests.
- 0.1.82b (2026-10-18, fix): a crocodile shut the world down ("[Watchdog] Stack overflow detected", an unhandled StackOverflow, from animals/croc.js stepOne in the wild-crocodile loop). With no place to lie out, lieOut sent it to float; with no water spot, floatStep sent it back to lie out in the shade; with neither (a wild one off in a forest, more than 4 blocks from any bank and 8 from deep water; or a zoo one in an exhibit with no water and no shade, or every spot taken) the two called each other until the stack overflowed, which a try/catch can't stop. The hand-back now carries fallback false and that call settles it where it is (on its feet; the engine strolls it). Reproduced in the crocodile mock world (new far and dry scenarios: RangeError at lieOut/floatStep on 0.1.82, none after); the other seven scenarios behave exactly as before. A test pins the guard. Scripts only: /reload or rejoin.
- 0.1.84 (2026-10-18, Nether trio, first slice): the Crimson Grazer (spec 5.3 "crimson_grazer | Stocky six-legged fungal herbivore | Herd browsing and fungus rubbing | Crimson ground, fungal feed, herd shelter", and the items list's "Fungal Ration"; Austin's card "Nether trio", the blocky style for new animals, real adult size on the musk ox: a bull 1.44 blocks to the top of the hump and 2.3 long, a cow 0.85 of him, a calf 0.4 of a cow). Research and design in research/nether_plan.md and research/nether_facts.md. The Crimson Grazer (zoocraft:crimson_grazer, wild zoocraft:wild_crimson_grazer in crimson forests, herds of 3 to 5, the first a bull) is a Nether animal: fire-immune, carried in a Thermal Carrier, needs heat (a fuelled Thermal Regulator), eats Fungal Rations (zoocraft:fungal_ration, two from a crimson fungus, a warped fungus and nether wart at the Zoo Workshop; 3 a park day; sold at the shop) and is baited with one (a crimson or warped fungus tempts it too but doesn't feed it, so striders keep their own food). Herd of 3 to 8, lonely below 3. Habitat profile "herd": 180 blocks of floor for the first, 40 more each, 4 blocks high; 10% of the floor under cover ("herd shelter"); 16 or more crimson nylium blocks in the floor (else comfort capped at 55) and a crimson stem or hyphae standing beside the floor to rub on (else capped at 60), both counted by the walking measure (blocks.js classifyBlock nylium and stem, only for a reader that asks; a climbing reader still sees a stem as a log). animals/grazer.js (rules in grazerRules.js): the oldest grown bull leads (tag zoocraft:herd_lead) and the rest follow him (behavior.follow_mob); mornings and afternoons they graze head-down on nylium and roots, eat at the feeder or wild fungus, and rub a flank on a crimson stem; round noon and through the night they lie down together near the lead, chewing the cud, a calf by its mother. Someone in Survival or Adventure within 8 blocks of a wild herd (6 inside an exhibit) makes it form a ring, the grown ones facing out round the calves, the bulls on the side toward them; come within 3 and the nearest grown one in the ring rushes and stops short, a bull before a cow (a bluff; with no room to lunge it stamps where it stands). Only someone who hurt one of the herd gets a real charge (4 damage and a shove; not in Peaceful) for a minute after. Field Journal tasks: spot one, see the ring, see one rub on a stem, see one chew the cud. The wild ones are found by type, as every script-steered mob. 167 tests; tsc clean (a Field Journal sight helper spread into the sight object needs one return type: a `{}` branch doubled TypeScript's union for that object and ran tsc out of room). New entities, items and textures: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.85 (2026-10-19, Nether trio, second slice): the Warped Gecko (spec 5.3 "warped_gecko | Blue-green luminous climber | Wall clinging and vertical hunting | Registered climbing surfaces and insect feed"; Austin's card "Nether trio", the blocky style for new animals, real adult size on the New Caledonian giant gecko: 43 cm nose to tail, males and females alike, a hatchling 0.35 of that). Research and design in research/nether_plan.md and research/nether_facts.md. The Warped Gecko (zoocraft:warped_gecko, wild zoocraft:wild_warped_gecko in warped forests on warped nylium, alone or in twos, the first a male) is a Nether animal: fire-immune, carried in a Thermal Carrier, needs heat, eats Insect Rations from the new Insect Feeder (zoocraft:insect_feeder: string, a glass pane and two planks at the Zoo Workshop; it holds Insect Rations only; any insect eater eats from it, hedgehogs, raccoons and flamingos too, and geckos eat from nothing else), 1 a park day, baited with one. Its spots and eyes glow (entity_emissive_alpha). One grown male to an exhibit, 1 to 4 together. Habitat profile "terrarium": 9 blocks of floor for the first, 4 more each, 4 blocks of air. The new wall measure (exhibits.js, for geckos, or an empty exhibit with an Insect Feeder in it): the flight flood over the exhibit's air, which must not lead out (geckos climb glass and walls: an open top makes the exhibit unsafe and a gecko may climb out at night and stay out until it's caught), the wall faces to cling to (16 each, else comfort capped at 55) and the cracks (a one-block gap in a wall open on one side only); a hide each by day, a crack or a Burrow Shelter (else capped at 60); two grown males cap comfort at 50. The gecko reader (blocks.js classifyBlock gecko) gives "partial" for blocks that aren't a whole cube (slabs, stairs, trapdoors, chests, zoo equipment): not climbable, no way through. animals/gecko.js (rules in geckoRules.js): it climbs by script on a graph of spots (an open block and the face it holds: the floor or one of four walls), along the floor, up, down and across walls, round inside and outside corners, over the top of a wall and off a ledge, a glide a tick at a time with its gravity off (zoocraft:climb), its pose (zoocraft:pose: floor, head up, head down, across left or right) and what it does (zoocraft:act) sent to the client. By day it sleeps tucked into a crack, else a Burrow Shelter, else high on a wall, each in a hide of its own; at dusk it calls (a cluck, then a growl); at night it climbs about (a grown male within 2 blocks of the floor, the rest higher), clings head down watching, licks its eyes, and hunts the moths a stocked Insect Feeder lets out onto the walls near it (a wild one, moths drawn to its glow): creeping up, a freeze, the snap. Two grown males within 3 blocks at night bob their heads and growl. Someone in Survival or Adventure within 2 blocks gets the threat and a hiss, then it runs off up the wall; no harm done. Someone holding an Insect Ration or an empty carrier within 8 draws it down to the floor. No two geckos end up in one spot; one met off its spot walks onto it rather than jumping. A nocturnal house turns its day round; in the Nether, where no daylight gets in anywhere, any dark exhibit is one (the clipboard says so, and that they glow, so guests see them in the dark). A Viewing Point doesn't count one tucked into its hide. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch one cling to a wall, see one hunt a moth, hear one call at dusk. Released on warped nylium. Found by type, as every script-steered mob. 174 tests; tsc clean; a mock world (zoo, Nether, rivals, open roof, wild and care scenes, two days each on four seeds) ran the real scripts with nothing flagged. New entities, a block, a particle and textures: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.86 (2026-10-19, fix): Austin's crocodile had its upper legs standing up like wings with the lower legs floating under them ("Croc texture is messed up. Legs dont connect", two screenshots). Cause: the game turns a part about z the opposite way to gen/render_pose.py and applies x, y and z in the opposite order (Blockbench's Bedrock codec, which the game matches), and it lays a box's skin out mirrored across the box with the top and bottom front to back compared with the 3D painters (Blockbench cube.js updateUV and three_custom.js setShape; Mojang's geometry.humanoid puts the right leg, wearing the skin's first strip, at -x). gen/gameconv.py rewrote the bind, cube and animated rotations of the nine animals drawn the old way (crocodile, lion, tiger, zebra, flamingo, hedgehog, raccoon, both rays) so the game shows the designs (renders of the game's maths match them pixel for pixel at rest); the red panda, gorilla, owl, grazer, gecko and the older animals were written in the game's signs and are untouched. gen/gameskin.py moved every texel of the 38 skins painted the old way (those nine animals and the chorus deer, ember fox, rift ferret, Blaze Bone and Wither Skull Ball) to where the game shows the point it was drawn for (an independent check against Blockbench's tables: 0 wrong), and fixed two slips in hand-drawn skins (the otter's nose and the gecko's toe pads were on the backs of their tops). Tests: 4 new (the croc's upper legs end at their joints and slope down; the turned and kept poses; tops meet their sides as the game folds them; the otter's nose and gecko pads), 178 in all. Test plan: docs/Skins_and_poses_0186_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.87 (2026-10-19, Nether trio, third slice): the Cinder Boar (spec 5.3 "cinder_boar | Ash-colored rooting animal | Digs and uses cooling mud pockets | Ash, thermal mud, strong barrier"; Austin's card "Nether trio", the blocky style for new animals, real adult size on the wild boar: a boar 0.8 blocks at the shoulder and 1.5 long, a sow 0.9 of him, a piglet 0.35 of a sow). Research and design in research/nether_plan.md and research/nether_facts.md. The Cinder Boar (zoocraft:cinder_boar, wild zoocraft:wild_cinder_boar in basalt deltas and nether wastes, in sounders of 3 to 6, two sows and their striped piglets that can't be caught, and now and then a lone grown boar that lives alone and never joins one) is a Nether animal: fire-immune, carried in a Thermal Carrier, needs heat, eats Ember or Fungal Rations from a Basic Feeder, 2 a park day, baited with an Ember Ration (a crimson fungus held up draws one over). One grown boar to an exhibit, 2 to 8 together. New block Thermal Mud (zoocraft:thermal_mud: 4 mud, a soul soil and a snowball make 4; mud sounds). Habitat profile "sounder": 120 blocks of floor for the first, 24 more each, 3 high. 8 Warm Ash in the floor to root in (species.ash), 4 Thermal Mud to wallow in (species.wallow, ex.thermalMudCells; else comfort capped at 50, "too hot without it", and a care warning). Strong walls (species.boarProof): a boar measure (geometry.js floodBoar) floods through wooden fences, gates and doors, and a way out through one makes the exhibit unsafe; the Clipboard says "Strong walls: yes" or where the wood is. animals/boar.js (rules in boarRules.js): the oldest sow leads the sounder, the rest follow and piglets keep by their mother; mornings and afternoons into the night it roots (in Warm Ash, a wild one in soul soil, gravel and netherrack) and eats; at noon it trots onto the mud and wallows, comes out muddy (zoocraft:muddy) and rubs it off against a rock or wall at its side, found by script, then lies down near the lead; late at night they sleep together. The cells under a boar's whole body count, so one that stops at the mud's edge still wallows. The sow's defence (Forestry England): someone in Survival or Adventure within 8 of a piglet (in an exhibit: inside it and within 7) and she trots between them and stands bristling; within 4 a charge that stops short; within 2, or at someone who hurt one, a real one, 3 damage (a boar's tusks 4) and a shove; nothing in Peaceful or for someone holding its food. Come round her piglets and she goes back between, and the piglets she stands up for stay hers while she guards (a wild piglet's mother is the nearest sow). Its trots and charges go round a basalt pillar in the way by one corner. A wild lone boar, or a sow without piglets, trots off from someone within 6 (straight away, or turned up to 90 degrees to get round a pillar) and again each 2 seconds they stay that near; hit, it charges back once for each hit, then keeps away. Its random stroll is in its walking body only, so standing still drops it and it doesn't stroll back toward whoever it trotted off from. Getting out: with a weak wall a grown one now and then goes to test the wood, butts it and, with mobGriefing on, breaks it (it drops) and trots 6 blocks out, with a red message; with it off the wood holds and a yellow message says so at most every 5 minutes. The outside watch now uses the last box the exhibit was measured enclosed in (penBox), so a boar out through a broken fence counts as outside. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch one root, find one wallowing, watch one rub the mud off. Released on basalt, blackstone, netherrack or soul soil. The Crimson Grazer's rub now leans toward the stem at its side. Found by type, as every script-steered mob. 185 tests; tsc clean; a mock world (zoo, wild, care, break-out, butting and holding scenes on four seeds) ran the real scripts with nothing flagged. New entities, a block and textures: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.88 (2026-10-19, the Nether pair, first): the Soul Moth (spec 5.3 "soul_moth | Pale blue moth | Gathers around soul light | Enclosed airspace, soul lights, nectar"; a fantasy hawk moth in the blocky style, drawn big on Austin's card "Big moth": 10 units from wing tip to wing tip, about twice the largest real moth, a male 0.92 of her with broad feathery antennae). Research and design in research/nether_pair_plan.md and research/nether_facts.md. The Soul Moth (zoocraft:soul_moth, wild zoocraft:wild_soul_moth in soul sand valleys, 2 to 4 together) is a Nether animal: fire-immune, carried in a Thermal Carrier, needs heat, glows faintly, 1 Nectar Ration a park day from a Nectar Feeder only, baited with a Nectar Ration. New item Nectar Ration (zoocraft:nectar_ration: 2 sugar and a honeycomb make 3, 2 sugar and sweet berries make 2). New block Nectar Feeder (zoocraft:nectar_feeder: a glass pane, two cornflowers and a stick at the Zoo Workshop; a glass dish of glowing nectar on a post, empty, low or full), which holds Nectar Rations only; Nectar Rations go in nothing else but a Hanging Food Basket (a new nectar look, food 12). Habitat profile "moth_house": the flight measure (roofed all over), 9 blocks of floor for the first and 2 more each under 4 blocks of air; an empty exhibit with a Nectar Feeder in it is measured for flight too, so moths can move in. The moth measure (mothRules.js measureMoth, ex.soul) finds the soul lights in the air or on its walls (a soul lantern hanging or standing, a lit soul campfire, soul fire, a soul torch standing or on a wall, read from the block states) and the host plants in the floor; without a soul light comfort stays under 40, with a care warning. It's a night animal (nightLife) whose own light counts as dark (species.houseLight 10, where the Nocturnal Lamps' limit is 7: houseFromReadings takes the limit), so a dark house turns its day round. animals/moth.js (rules in mothRules.js), steered through the house's measured air as the owl is: by day at rest flat on a wall, high and near a light first; at dusk to the feeder first, where it hovers at a side of the dish and sips; through the night by turns circling a soul light with its back to it (after Fabian et al. 2024: a circle 0.9 to 1.4 blocks out, either way round, several moths spread round one light all going the same way), a stall climbing toward a hanging light, a flip over one on the floor, sips and flits; back to a wall at dawn. Someone in Survival or Adventure right up to one at rest: the startle display (the hindwing eyespots), then off to a wall 3 or more blocks from them. In the wild it rests on the walls and pillars round home, finds the soul fire round home by a scan, and a Nectar Ration held within 10 blocks draws one to hover and sip in front of their hands. The young are caterpillars, laid only where 4 or more crimson or warped roots, nether sprouts or fungi grow in the floor (breeding.hostPlants): they hatch on the plants, crawl from plant to plant munching them (never the feeder: they take nothing from it), lie in the floor as a pupa for their last 2 park days, and the new moth pumps its wings out on the floor before it flies (emergedDay). Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch one circle a soul light, see one flip over a light and drop, watch one sip nectar. Released on soul soil or soul sand. The shop now names every ration (Fruit, Grain, Meat and Fungal Rations and Flamingo Pellets showed as ids). Found by type, as every script-steered mob. Run in a mock world (the real scripts on a modelled Nether: a moth house by day and night, a dark house, caterpillars to moths, an empty house, wild moths; 20 runs): fixed two moths sipping at one side of the dish, moths circling one light on top of each other (each now keeps 0.8 blocks behind the one ahead, and picks its way round on arrival), a drift after each circle, two flits ending in one block, caterpillars crawling over one another and two hatching on one plant, wild moths that never fled from high up a wall and shared rest spots, and every wild moth in reach coming to a held Nectar Ration (one comes). Moths drink from a Water Trough like every animal. New entities, a block, an item and textures: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.89 (2026-10-19, the Nether pair, second): the Quartz Beetle (spec 5.3 "quartz_beetle | Chunky beetle with quartz plates | Burrows through mineral substrate | Heated cave, mineral bed"; a fantasy rhinoceros beetle in the blocky style, drawn big on Austin's card "Big beetle": about 0.6 blocks long, a female 0.85 of a male and hornless). Research and design in research/nether_pair_plan.md and research/nether_facts.md. The Quartz Beetle (zoocraft:quartz_beetle, wild zoocraft:wild_quartz_beetle in the Nether wastes and basalt deltas, 1 to 3 together) is a Nether animal: fire-immune, carried in a Thermal Carrier, needs heat, 1 Mineral Pellet a park day from a Basic Feeder, baited with a Mineral Pellet or a nether quartz. New block Mineral Substrate (zoocraft:mineral_substrate: 4 gravel, a nether quartz and a bone meal make 4, at a crafting table or the Zoo Workshop; grey gravel with quartz chips, gravel's sounds, it doesn't fall). Habitat profile "cave": walking, 12 blocks of floor for the first and 3 more each, 2 high, with a roof over 90% of the floor (its cover, "a cave roof"). The walking measure counts the Mineral Substrate in the floor (the reader's "substrate", ex.substrateCells); with fewer than 6 comfort stays under 50, with a care warning. A night animal (nightLife): a dark cave turns its day round. animals/beetle.js (rules in beetleRules.js), walked by the engine with seek groups for the feeder and the bed, as the boar is, and moved by the script for the rest: by day buried in the substrate with only its quartz-plated back showing, now and then tunnelling 2 to 5 blocks through it; at dusk it digs out and goes to the feeder; through the night it walks and eats, and two grown males that meet wrestle (a male's horn is short, middling or long, zoocraft:horn: unmatched, the shorter backs off and is chased; matched, they shove and one pries the other up and flips him; he kicks, rights himself and walks off); at dawn it walks back to the bed and digs in. Someone in Survival or Adventure within 2 blocks of one on its feet: it rears and hisses, then digs in if it's on its bed or walks off. In the wild it buries itself in netherrack, soul soil, soul sand or gravel. The young are grubs, laid only where there are 6 or more Mineral Substrate blocks (breeding.substrate): they live buried in it and feed in it (never at the feeder), now and then curl up at the surface or tunnel a block or two, crawl back if they're out of it, lie in a pupal cell for their last 2 park days, and the new beetle digs up out of the substrate (emergedDay). Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch one dig itself in or out, watch two males wrestle, hear one hiss. Released on netherrack, soul soil, soul sand or gravel. Found by type, as every script-steered mob. Run in a mock world (the real scripts on a modelled Nether: a zoo cave by day and night with two males wrestling, the hiss, no bed, a dark cave, grubs to beetles, wild beetles, eight beetles on six bed cells; 28 runs): fixed a beetle standing two cells off the feeder with nothing to eat (the walk there now ends inside the ring of cells it eats from, goal_radius 1.2, and one stood still near it steps beside it), two males facing off across the feeder (a bout starts only with a level way for both), a loser or a hissed beetle against a wall going nowhere (it now turns up to 90 degrees either side), two beetles buried in one cell and grubs hatching in their mother's cell or off the bed (each bed cell is held by one, two to a cell only when there are more beetles than cells; grubs hatch in a free cell of the substrate), a tunnel clipping a block beside its way, tunnels never going to the cells just east or south, and the feeder's days left counting the grubs (and a moth's caterpillars). New entities, a block and textures: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.90 (2026-10-20, fixes from Austin's first look at the Nether pair): the Quartz Beetle's pupa state had an empty animation list, which the game rejects (his content log: "states | pupa | animations | Required child  not found"); it plays the buried pose now (the pupa is hidden, as before), gen/beetle.py makes it so, a test checks every controller state, and check-pack.mjs flags an empty list as an error. A wild Soul Moth by day with no wall near it (his superflat world: "the wings flap but nothing happens") hovered on the spot all day, wings going (its 24 random looks for a wall found none, and it waited to look again). Now, when the random looks find none, it looks at every other column round home at every height; with no wall at all it flies off up to 3 times, to 10 to 16 blocks from where it was met, looking again, and failing that lands on the ground below it (a block clear of the others) and rests there flat, wings swept back, till dusk; walked up to there, it flashes and flies off as from a wall; at dusk it goes back round where it was met for the night. A baited wild moth hovers in front of someone's hands toward it, or now further round them when a pillar or a wall beside them is in the way (it didn't follow someone who stepped up to a basalt pillar). Run in the moth mock world on a flat field by day, by night and the next morning (it hovered there on 0.1.89, as Austin saw), and the zoo, dark, young, empty and wild scenes again: a first try that gave up on walls the random looks had missed sent valley moths away from their soul fire, so the full look comes first. No new files.
- 0.1.91 (2026-10-22, the End trio, first): the End Hopper (spec 5.4 "end_hopper | Small creature with long angular legs | High jumps between platforms | Jump clearance, roof, landing pads"; built on the springhare, life-size, about a rabbit sitting up, with a jerboa's long angular hind legs, in the simpler blocky style). Research and design in research/end_plan.md and research/end_facts.md. The End Hopper (zoocraft:end_hopper, wild zoocraft:wild_end_hopper on the end stone of the outer End islands, 1 to 3 together) is an End animal: carried in a Stabilized Carrier, 8 or more end stone in the floor, 1 Chorus Browse a park day from a Basic Feeder, baited with Chorus Browse. Coats from minecraft:variant (Pale, the common one, and Purpur); big dark eyes with a violet shine and faint violet patches that glow (a springhare's fur fluoresces in patches). New block Landing Pad (zoocraft:landing_pad: 3 purple wool over 3 end stone make 3 at the Zoo Workshop; end-stone-brick sides, a quilted purple cushion on top, wool sounds), a fixture. Habitat profile "hopper": walking, 36 blocks of floor for the first and 8 more each, 4 blocks of air over it. It leaps 3 blocks high, so it gets the jump measure (the flight measure's flood through the air): an exhibit whose air leads out is unsafe with hoppers and they won't move in. Its pads (species.pads): 3 or more that count, one more for each hopper over 3, at least one 2 or more blocks over the floor; a pad counts with 3 blocks of air over it, within 3 up and 5 across of the floor or another pad that counts. Too few, or none high: comfort stays at 50 or below, with a care warning that says which. animals/hopper.js (rules in hopperRules.js): the End has no day or night, so it keeps to bouts, 40 to 90 seconds out and about, then a nap of 20 to 40 standing asleep. The engine walks it on the ground (keeping off the pads, navigation blocks_to_avoid); every leap is the script's, a push a tick along an arc checked clear for its body, flown closed loop on where it is, carried by teleport if the game doesn't move it, settling exactly where it was going. In a zoo it runs from pad to pad (crouch, leap, land, sit up, 2 to 5 in a run, sometimes back down), one hopper to a pad; it forages at the feeder, sits up to look round, and listens with its head and ears on the ground. In the wild it leaps up onto blocks 1 to 3 higher and down off them and across flat ground, never onto a slab, stairs, a fence top or a chorus stalk, never out over the void (ground within 8 blocks under every point of the arc). Someone in Survival or Adventure, not sneaking or holding its food, within 6 of a wild one (3 inside a zoo exhibit): it gets away in quick low zigzag darts, each turned 20 to 60 degrees off the way away from where they are then, left and right by turns, doubling back past them when boxed in; it keeps going until they're 5 off (up to 12 darts), then sits bolt upright watching, and goes again if they come a block nearer.
