In a zoo it leaps to the pad farthest from them instead. A held Chorus Browse draws one over to sit up and sniff it. One pup, which keeps to the floor by its mother and never leaps or runs. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch one leap, watch one zigzag away, watch one listen. Run in a mock world (the real scripts against a modelled game with velocity physics in four models: the game's order, drag first, half-strength pushes, pushes ignored; a roofed zoo with pads at three heights, too few pads, an open roof, a chase, a held Chorus Browse, a mother and pup, End islands over the void and a superflat field; 108 runs): fixed a jump of up to 0.8 of a block as it settled at the end of a drop (it now settles the tick after the last push), the zigzag task ticked off by ordinary landings (now only a dart counts), leaps landing on chorus stalks, darts kept on the way away from where someone was when the zigzag began (one could take it back at them) and too slow to get away (someone walking caught up; darts are now 6 to 9 ticks a tick apart, so it draws away from a walker and about holds its distance from a sprinter), short hops along an island's edge, sitting up within reach of someone still coming, a leap off a rock whose body caught the rock's corner on the way down (the arc check now follows the game's move: down first, then across), and one that strolled up onto a pad another sat on. One brush of a rock's edge by 0.03 of a block remains in the harshest model (drag before the move, in sub-steps), once in 108 runs; the game's own order flies it clean. New entities, a block and textures: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.92 (2026-10-22, the End trio, second): the Shimmer Snail (spec 5.4 "shimmer_snail | Pale body, pearlescent spiral shell | Leaves a fading luminous trail | Mineral bed, shade, crevices"; built on the giant African land snail, the rock snails and the glowing Quantula striata, drawn big, three quarters of a block crawling, in the simpler blocky style). Research and design in research/end_plan.md and research/end_facts.md. The Shimmer Snail (zoocraft:shimmer_snail, wild zoocraft:wild_shimmer_snail on the end stone of the outer End islands, 2 to 4 together) is an End animal: carried in a Stabilized Carrier, 4 or more end stone in the floor, 1 Mineral Pellet a park day from a Basic Feeder, baited with Mineral Pellets. Coats from minecraft:variant (Pearl, the common one, and Opal); the front of its foot glows green-aqua and its shell's sheen glows faintly. Habitat profile "snail": walking, 9 blocks of floor for the first and 3 more each, 2 blocks of air, shade over a quarter of the floor. Its crevices (species.crevices): the crevice measure counts the one-block holes at the foot of the walls that open onto the floor (open, solid under, over and on three sides), one for each grown snail; its bed (species.mineralBed): 4 or more Mineral Substrate in the floor, counted as the beetles' is. Too few of either: comfort stays at 50 or below, with a care warning that says how to make one. A hermaphrodite (species.hermaphrodite): no sexes, any two grown ones that aren't related pair and one lays 2 or 3 eggs in the Mineral Substrate (4 or more blocks); the hatchlings come up on it near their parent. animals/snail.js (rules in snailRules.js): the End has no day or night, so it keeps to bouts, 90 to 150 seconds out, then 40 to 80 sealed in its shell (a white seal over the mouth) at its resting place: the crevice it rested in last, else the nearest free one, else a wall's foot beside where another rests, else any wall's foot, else where it is (a superflat field). Out, it crawls about, grazes the Mineral Substrate (the end stone in the wild), eats at the feeder, looks about with its eye stalks up, and follows another's fresh trail, touching tentacles when it catches up. Two never end up on top of each other: its ways go round the others that are still and where crawling ones will stop, and one that comes up to another waits a moment, then goes round it. All its moving is the script's: a teleport a tick along a way through the floor (or over standable ground in the wild: never over the void, a slab, a fence top or a chorus stalk), gravity off while it glides, 0.2 blocks a second (a hatchling 0.12), climbing a solid one-block step's face and easing down off one. As it crawls it lays a glowing trail, a mark every fifth of a block that fades over 45 seconds (zoocraft:snail_trail, unlit, lying flat along its way), dimmer marks where it grazes or eats. Someone in Survival or Adventure, not sneaking or holding its food, within 3 blocks of a wild one (2 inside a zoo exhibit) and coming nearer, or hitting it: it pulls into its shell 4 to 8 seconds; each time again within a minute it stays in 0.6 of the time before, and after 4 it stops pulling in until they've left it alone a minute. A held Mineral Pellet draws one over (off a crawl too) to reach up at it. Hatchlings crawl after their parent, toward where it's going, and seal themselves in beside it when it rests. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch its trail, watch one pull in, find one sealed in. Run in a mock world (the real scripts against a modelled game: a zoo with a shelf of crevices, a rock and a step, too few crevices, a startle with habituation, a held pellet, a mother with hatchlings, End islands over the void with a rock crevice, a chorus stalk, a slab and a fence, and a superflat field; 224 runs): fixed trail marks over a step's edge or pressed into a feeder (a mark now lies only on a block's top, and only those are followed), followers crawling into the snail ahead or over the feeder's corner (a follow stops short of the other, and its way to the trail and along it keeps its body clear), two crawling onto each other or sealing in one cell (giving way, going round, a step aside when they meet in one block), a startle stay that could come out longer than the one before, zoo snails resting at a wall with crevices free farther off (any crevice of the exhibit now), hatchlings hatching off the substrate, falling behind their parent and missing its rests, a crawling snail ignoring a held pellet until its crawl was done, and a mismatch between the crevice measure and the snail over leaves and chorus. New entities, textures and a particle: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.93 (2026-10-22, the End trio, third): the Obsidian Crab (spec 5.4 "Low armored black shell | Scrapes minerals and digs | Rocky cave, mineral feeder, substrate"; built on the coconut crab for its size and life, drawn as a true crab with its low wide shell and sideways walk, with the rock crabs' scraping and the sand crabs' digging in backwards, in the simpler blocky style, life-size: the average grown male coconut crab, the shell 5 units wide and the legs 14.7 across). Research and design in research/end_plan.md and research/end_facts.md. The Obsidian Crab (zoocraft:obsidian_crab, wild zoocraft:wild_obsidian_crab on the end stone of the outer End islands, alone or 2 together) is an End animal: carried in a Stabilized Carrier, 4 or more end stone in the floor, alone by choice (1 to 4, an exhibit of its own), baited with Mineral Pellets smelled from 10 blocks. Coats from minecraft:variant (Obsidian, the common one, and Crying, flecked with glowing violet); its eyes glow violet. Sexes from minecraft:mark_variant: a female 0.7 of a male, a crablet 0.35 of him. A new block, the Mineral Feeder (zoocraft:mineral_feeder, 5 polished blackstone in a U at the Zoo Workshop; a basin whose look shows how full it is): it holds Mineral Pellets only; crabs (species.mineralFeeder) eat from nothing else, and the other mineral eaters (Quartz Beetle, Shimmer Snail, Basalt Tortoise) eat from it as well as from a Basic Feeder. Habitat profile "crab": walking, 12 blocks of floor for the first and 6 more each, 2 blocks of air, a cave roof over 9 in 10 of the floor. Its rock (species.rockFaces): the rock measure counts the sides of rock blocks facing onto the floor at its level (a wall of rock, a boulder on the floor: obsidian, end stone, stone, deepslate, blackstone, basalt and the like), 4 for the first crab and 2 more each; its burrows (species.burrows): 2 Mineral Substrate a crab in the floor, 4 at least. Too few of either: comfort stays at 50 or below, with a care warning. Breeding: she carries 2 or 3 eggs under her tail 3 park days, then the crablets come out from under her, each with a little spiral shell on its back, keep near her and dig in when she rests; grown in 5. animals/crab.js (rules in crabRules.js): the End has no day or night, so it keeps to bouts, 2 to 3 minutes out, then dug in backwards 30 to 60 seconds with only the eyes and the big claw showing, the claw across the hole: its own burrow while it's free (a wild one's when it's within 8 blocks), else a free Mineral Substrate block beside a rock first (a wild one in diggable ground at a rock's foot, else where it is), else pressed flat by a rock, eyes folded in. Out, it walks sideways about (forward and slower for the last block to something), scrapes a rock face with its claw tips (the ground with none: a superflat field) with grit flying, eats at the Mineral Feeder facing it, sifts the substrate, and looks about up on its legs. Two grown ones within 1.5 blocks face off with their claws up, and the smaller sidles off; not the same two again for 30 seconds. Two never dig into one block or end up on top of each other (it gives way as the snail does). All its moving is the script's, gliding a tick at a time with gravity off along a way over the floor (never over the void, a slab, a fence top or a chorus stalk), up a solid one-block step's face and down off one. Someone in Survival or Adventure, not sneaking or holding its food, coming at a wild one: within 4 blocks it rears with its claws up, clacking; within 2, or hit, it bolts sideways 4 to 8 blocks at 3 blocks a second and digs in 10 to 20 seconds (inside a zoo exhibit 3 and 1.5). A held Mineral Pellet draws one over, forward, antennae flicking, to reach up at it. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch it scrape a rock, watch it dig itself in, see it rear with its claws up. Run in a mock world (the real scripts against a modelled game: a roofed zoo with an obsidian wall, a boulder and a step of end stone, too little rock and substrate, a keeper walking up, sneaking, holding pellets, punching and rushing, held pellets, face-offs, a mother with crablets, End islands over the void with obsidian and crying obsidian rocks, a chorus stalk, a slab and a fence, and a superflat field; 48 runs and two long zoo runs, all clean). New entities, a block, textures and a particle: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.94 (2026-10-22, the End pair, first): the Chorus Moth (spec 5.4 "chorus_moth | Purple angular wings | Hovers around chorus flowers | Flight space, chorus nectar, low light"; a fantasy moth built on the elephant hawk-moth, in the simpler blocky style, drawn big: 8 units, half a block, from wing tip to wing tip, a little smaller than the big Soul Moth, until the End pair's size card is answered). Research and design in research/end_pair_plan.md and research/end_pair_facts.md. The Chorus Moth (zoocraft:chorus_moth, wild zoocraft:wild_chorus_moth on the end stone of the outer End islands, 2 to 4 together, a Wild Chorus Moth Spawn Egg) is an End animal: carried in a Stabilized Carrier, 4 or more end stone in the floor, 1 Nectar Ration a park day from a Nectar Feeder only, baited with a Nectar Ration held within 10 blocks (it flies over and hovers to sip from it). Chorus nectar: a new recipe, 2 sugar and a chorus fruit make 2 Nectar Rations (crafting table or Zoo Workshop); the Basic Feeder's refusal now names Chorus Moths too. Coats from minecraft:variant: Chorus (deep purple forewings with lilac-pink bars, pink hindwings, the common one) and Popped (pale lilac); the sexes look alike; narrow forewings with cut-off tips that fold back over the hindwings into an arrowhead at rest. The Soul Moth's moth house (aerial, 9 blocks of floor for the first and 2 more each, 4 high, roofed), with chorus flowers inside or on its walls (one for each moth, 2 at least, any age, on end stone or a chorus plant) and low light (the nocturnal house's test: no daylight, nothing brighter than the Nocturnal Lamps); too few flowers, or a bright house (where they fly only at night), and comfort stays at 50 or below, with a care warning. The Clipboard shows the chorus flowers, the Nectar Feeders, the low light and the chorus on the floor. The tops of chorus low under the roof (a flower on the floor of a 4-high house) are no longer counted as low floor. Breeding: 3 to 6 eggs laid only where 4 or more chorus plants or flowers stand on the floor; the caterpillars (grey-purple, two big lilac-ringed eyespots, a horn at the tail) crawl from plant to plant and munch at their foot, facing it, never at the feeder, and rear to make the eyespots stare at someone close; their last 2 park days they lie in the floor as a pupa; the moth crawls out, pumps its wings out and flies; grown in 5. animals/chorusMoth.js (rules in chorusMothRules.js): out in bouts at any hour in a dim house (2 to 3 minutes flying, 40 to 80 seconds at rest on a wall, high first, never on a chorus stalk; with no wall, on the ground), a 3 to 5 second shiver to warm its wings before every flight from rest. Out, it works round the chorus flowers, the nearest it hasn't just been at first, never straight back to the one it left, hovering at an open side or over the top with its proboscis in the flower, swaying, 3 to 8 seconds, one moth to a flower; sips at the Nectar Feeder's dish now and then; flits between. Someone in Survival or Adventure, not sneaking, within 1.5 blocks of their body startles one at rest: a 1-second shiver, then off to rest 3 or more blocks from them. Wild ones find the chorus flowers within 10 blocks of home (looked for again each minute, so placed or broken flowers are seen), never fly out over the void, and on a flat field with no flowers or walls flit about and rest on the ground. Two never end up in one block. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch it hover at a chorus flower and sip, watch it shiver its wings warm, see it sip nectar. Run in a mock world (the real scripts against a modelled game: a dim moth house with chorus, too few flowers, a glass-roofed house by day and night, an empty house with a Nectar Feeder, caterpillars to grown moths, the End's islands over the void with chorus, a startle and a held ration, a superflat field; 28 runs, all clean). New entities, textures and a recipe: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.95 (2026-10-22, the End pair, second, the last custom animal): the Starback Tortoise (spec 5.4 "Dark shell with star-like markings | Slow browsing and resting | End vegetation, warm pad, large retreat"; a fantasy tortoise built on the Indian star tortoise, in the simpler blocky style, drawn big: a female's shell about 0.8 blocks long, a male 0.64 of her, until the End pair's size card is answered; research/end_pair_plan.md and research/end_pair_facts.md). The Starback Tortoise (zoocraft:starback_tortoise, wild zoocraft:wild_starback_tortoise on the end stone of the outer End islands, alone or 2 together, a Wild Starback Tortoise Spawn Egg) is an End animal: carried in a Stabilized Carrier, 4 or more end stone in the floor, 1 Chorus Browse a park day from a Basic Feeder, baited with Chorus Browse or a chorus fruit held within 8 blocks (it plods over to about a block off and nibbles at it, leaving off basking once it's warm enough to eat, browsing or grazing; it stops when it's put away, and heads for you again if you move off). A black, high-domed shell in steps with a yellow star on every scute (a faintly glowing centre), pale olive-yellow legs, neck, head and tail; males clearly smaller with a longer, thicker tail; one coat. The Warm Pad (zoocraft:warm_pad): a new whole block, end stone bricks with glowing orange seams, light 3, harmless to stand on, made at the Zoo Workshop from 3 end stone bricks over a magma block; it drops itself, and a gecko holds it like stone. The exhibit wants chorus standing on the floor (2 plants or flowers for each tortoise, 3 at least), Warm Pads in the floor (one for every 2) and a retreat (2 blocks of floor for each, 4 at least, under a low roof 2 blocks over the floor: a lean-to, a hut or a low cave); too little of any and comfort stays at 50 or below, with a care warning. The Clipboard shows the chorus, the Warm Pads, the retreat and the Mineral Substrate. animals/starback.js (rules in starbackRules.js): the End has no day or night, so it keeps to bouts. It rests 1.5 to 2.5 minutes under the roof, drawn halfway into its shell, back to the same spot each time while it's free (with no roof, against a wall or a chorus stalk, else where it is); then out 3 to 5 minutes: to a free Warm Pad to bask, legs splayed and neck stretched along the ground, till it's warm (about half a minute on a pad, slower and never as warm in the open); then it browses the chorus, the nearest it hasn't just browsed, at the foot of the plant facing it, neck stretched up, biting and tugging, 10 to 20 seconds, one tortoise to a plant, now and then at the feeder's side; once it has cooled (about 2 minutes) it goes back to a pad. With no chorus it grazes the ground, plodding on 2 to 4 blocks between bites. It plods at about a quarter of a block a second, never climbs anything a block high (it goes round) and steps down off one; its ways run along the straight line, not round two sides. Someone in Survival or Adventure, not sneaking, coming on within 3 blocks of a wild one (1.5 in a zoo), or a hit: it pulls everything into its shell with a hiss and a shuffle and sits 6 to 12 seconds (hit: 15 to 25), then the head comes slowly out, it looks about and goes on (back to its rest if it was resting); it never runs; sneaking, standing still or holding its food, it stays out. Breeding: with 2 or more Mineral Substrate she digs a nest there with her hind feet (about 20 seconds) and pats it flat; the male comes over to her once, grunting; 2 to 4 hatchlings dig out at the nest 3 park days later (an egg's crack as each appears), black with yellow blotches and no stars yet, 0.3 of a female, and go their own way; grown in 6. Wild ones bask in the open, browse the chorus within 8 blocks, rest under an overhang or a chorus tree, and never walk off an island's edge. Field Journal tasks: spot one, watch one bask, watch one browse, see one pull into its shell. Run in a mock world (the real scripts against a modelled game: a zoo exhibit with Warm Pads, a lean-to, chorus and a feeder; one with none of them, then all; startles and a punch; a held browse; a nest and hatchlings; the End's islands over the void with chorus trees and an overhang; a superflat field; 35 runs, all clean). New entities, a block, textures and a recipe: a full Minecraft restart.
- 0.1.96 (2026-10-22, the performance check, the first of the remaining modules; spec 21: "Measure frame behavior, simulation step time, save size, and recovery time"): a benchmark and lighter script loops. `/zoocraft:benchmark build` (cheats on, in a world with no park) builds a full park round the player (bench/parkPlan.js: 20 exhibits with 100 animals of the 20 script-steered species, the paths, 28 Viewing Points, 24 guest services, 5 Staff Rooms and the entrance), hires 10 keepers and opens it to 40 visible guests (the benchmark park lifts the usual caps of 6); `/zoocraft:benchmark run [seconds]` (60 unless told) measures any park and prints the readout (ticks per second and the slowest second, script time per tick on average, at the 95th percentile and at worst, what the park holds, the saved data's size, the world's start-up script time, and the 8 slowest loops; the whole table goes in the content log); `/zoocraft:benchmark report` prints the last one again. Every loop was profiled with that park in a modelled game (the real scripts on QuickJS, the game's script engine, and on Node's profiler) and the slowest were made lighter without changing what they do: the exhibit re-checks are spread over their two minutes, each block is read once a measurement (a block read is one getBlock and one to three property reads, was seven native calls) and the results go in in a step of their own; guest route searches queue in one job (one job a guest took a slice each every tick, however full the tick) and run a third quicker; the path network's rebuild and the guests' routes index their loops; Viewing Points are checked each at a second of its own over the 30-second cycle, stop looking once the nearest exhibit settles the choice, and read the asleep property only of the animals that have one; the guest services are checked at their own second too; Staff Room and Gift Shop re-measures run in one job, a slice of the walk at a time (placing and using still answer at once); the 10-second care step goes round the exhibits a slice each (each exhibit's care in one slice, every animal once a step, nothing skipped or hurried); animal ways (zebras, lions, tigers and the other script-steered walkers) are planned by A* instead of a breadth-first walk (the same length of way, 2 to 6 times cheaper; an unreachable goal is known at once); the lions' ledges are worked out once a measurement; Clear Lava's burn check leaves out the fire-proof mobs. Measured on the model (6000 ticks of the full park, QuickJS): script time a tick 8.6 to 7.4 ms on average, 46 to 28 ms at the 99th percentile, 693 to 88 ms at worst (the worst single runs before: an exhibit measurement step 253 ms, the clock with the care step and the Viewing Points 240 ms, the Staff Room check 127 ms); the same end state (rating 91 and 4.5 stars, 5 Staff Rooms working, 27 of 28 Viewing Points viewed, 24 services working). Only scripts changed: leave the world and join again. The test plan is docs/Performance_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.97 (2026-10-23, the guests module, first of four builds; ideas backlog 1, "Austin writes his own lines"): the guests' lines move into one data file, data/guestLines.js, grouped by situation (each complaint, on the way somewhere, a happy animal, a glum animal, no animals yet) with lines for every guest and room for lines for one kind of visitor (the Nether and End visitors of 0.1.98), placeholders for the animal, its species, the exhibit and where the guest is going, and two new situations: a greeting in a guest's first 40 seconds and a goodbye on the way out; a test checks every complaint has lines and every placeholder belongs to its situation. The benchmark build (0.1.96) is fixed: it gave the Litter Bin a facing the block doesn't have, which the game refused ("Failed to resolve block"), so the build stopped at the first bin with the park claimed; its 30-second wait for the ground counted job steps, not ticks, so it gave up in a moment ("the park's ground didn't load"); and it took the ground from where the player's feet were, so a flying player got "isn't level". Now each service is placed with the states its block has, every block of the plan is checked with the game before anything changes, the wait is 30 seconds by the tick with two ticking areas (one by command, kept with the world, and one from the script API), the ground is the top block under the player, and a world where a build stopped half-way says to make a new one. A test checks every state the plan sets against the block files. Only scripts changed: leave the world and join again. The test plan is docs/Guests_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.98 (2026-10-23, the guests module, second of four builds; ideas backlog 2 and spec section 12, "dimensional explorers ... behavior profiles rather than separate complicated economies"): Nether and End visitors. Each new visible guest is an everyday guest or a themed visitor (visitors/visitorMix.js): the share of Nether visitors is the share of the species on show (every species in an exhibit with animals, as the rating counts them) whose home is the Nether (the vanilla strider included), the same for the End, the two together capped at 60% (BALANCE.guests.themedCap) in proportion; no Nether or End animals, no themed visitors. Six new guest skins in the simple style (gen/themed_guests.py, the everyday guests' painter): Nether visitors in orange heat suits with goggles, gloves, boots and a pack (looks 6 to 8), End visitors in violet research coats with a green badge (looks 9 to 11); zoocraft:look grows to 0..11. When a themed visitor picks where to walk, an exhibit with an animal from its dimension counts double (BALANCE.guests.themedPull; routes.js pickDestination takes the favoured exhibits). They have their own lines in every situation in data/guestLines.js (their happy and glum ones only about their own dimension's animals), and talking to one says "Nether visitor:" or "End visitor:". The record keeps the type, so a ghost comes back the same; tour followers and Expedition riders are drawn from the same mix and are known by their look. The Park Ledger shows a Visitors line with the shares and why. Nothing else changes: they pay, spend and rate the park like everyone else. Model run (the benchmark park, 20 species, 5 Nether and 6 End): mix 25% Nether and 30% End; of the trips to exhibits, End visitors went to End exhibits 54% of the time against 28% for everyday guests, Nether visitors to Nether exhibits 39% against 30%; no errors. New textures: a full Minecraft restart. The test plan is docs/Guests_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.99 (2026-10-23, the guests module, third of four builds; ideas backlog 4 and spec section 14, the Cleaner's "paths and bins"): bins and benches by how much path they cover, the well-furnished park, and keepers empty the bins. Coverage (visitors/serviceRules.js coverageSteps, worked out as a job in services.js whenever the path network or the working bins and benches change, a bin or bench a slice: under 1 ms a slice in the model): the share of the reached path blocks with a working Litter Bin within 12 blocks sideways (a circle) and 4 up or down of the ground it stands on (Keep America Beautiful's 2009 study: litterers were on average 9 m from the nearest bin), or a Bench within 25 (the UK's Inclusive Mobility 4.5: seats no more than 50 m apart on a busy footway). The bins catch 50% of each step's litter plus half their coverage (all of it with the paths covered), in turn while they have room; the rest lands on the paths (before the first count, all of it to the bins as before). The seating part counts 70% of itself plus 30% of the bench coverage. Bins near 90% of the paths with enough of them for a day's litter (BALANCE.litter.minBins at least) bring 2.5% more guests a day, benches near 90% with the benches needed the same; the amenity bonuses together are capped at 10% (BALANCE.amenities) and multiply the day's demand. The Park Ledger's Finances page shows each with its count, what's needed, its coverage, what it does and what would bring the bonus. Keepers: a working bin three-quarters full (BALANCE.staff.binEmptyAt) is a park-wide job (staff/rules.js binJobs; pickJob takes park-wide jobs for any keeper), priority 1 (2 when full), after the exhibits' urgent jobs; in view the keeper appears on a path block 6 blocks off the bin and walks to it (a new zoocraft:go_bin group on the keeper), out of sight it takes the usual time away; the status names the bin by its position. Model run (the benchmark park): bins near 28% of the paths catch 64% of the litter, benches near 33% count 80%; 8 bins filled to 28 were all emptied by 8 keepers (2 in view) within 600 ticks; no errors on Node or QuickJS. Only scripts and the keeper's behaviour file changed: leave the world and join again. The test plan is docs/Guests_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.100 (2026-10-23, the guests module, last of four builds; spec section 12, "seating, bins, and restrooms"): Restrooms, Water Fountains, Path Lamps and Planters, made at the Zoo Workshop and placed beside a path like the other guest services (zoocraft:guest_service). The Restroom (planks, blue dye, a bucket) and the Path Lamp (a glass pane, iron nuggets, a torch, an oak fence; makes 2) are two blocks high (equipment/multiblock.js LAYOUTS: a clear space 2 blocks high, the top a part block; breaking either half takes both down and drops one item); the Water Fountain (stone bricks, iron nuggets, a bucket) and the Planter (planks, bone meal, dirt; makes 2) are one block. Simple vanilla-style art (gen/amenities100.py). Services: two new parts, restrooms (1 per 30 guests a day, BALANCE.restrooms) and drinking water (1 per 100, BALANCE.drinks: the plumbing code asks for about one fountain for every 20 toilets; research/guests_facts.md), each 50 with none (guests make do, so a park built before 0.1.100 shows 85 instead of 100) up to 100 with all that's needed (serviceRules.js facilityPart); weights food 30%, seating 20%, tidy paths 20%, restrooms 15%, drinks 15%; new complaints and guest lines; guests walk to restrooms and fountains like stands and benches (services.js WALK_TO). Path Lamps: the lantern on the top half gives light 15; a path block within 8 blocks sideways and 2 up or down of a lamp is covered and stays at light 1 or more in the open (taxicab), so no monsters spawn on it (Bedrock since 1.18: block light 0 only); lamps lighting 80% of the paths bring 2.5% more guests a day. Planters: near half the paths, the same. Lamps and planters count only by coverage (the coverage job, now four kinds); guests see through both (data/blocks.js). The amenity bonuses together stay capped at 10%. The Park Ledger's Finances page lists Restrooms and Water Fountains (how many, how many needed, what they count) and Path Lamps and Planters (how much of the paths). Model run (the benchmark park, which now places 4 Restrooms, 4 Water Fountains, 8 Path Lamps and 8 Planters): restrooms and fountains count 100, lamps light 22% of the paths, planters are near 21%; guests walk to the restrooms ("Off to the restrooms, over the paths."); no errors on Node or QuickJS; per tick the same as 0.1.99 within run-to-run noise. QuickJS note: inside a generator's try with a finally, `x.k = yield* g()` doesn't compile ("unconsistent stack size"); a test now checks no such form is used. New blocks: close Minecraft completely and start it again. The test plan is docs/Guests_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.101 (2026-10-25, module 3, the fenced park area; ideas backlog 12, "a 1-block-tall fence all the way round makes every X and Z position inside it, at any Y, workable zoo space"): the Aluminum Fence and the Aluminum Fence Gate, made at the Zoo Workshop (4 copper ingots and 2 iron nuggets make 16 fences, the yield of vanilla iron bars; 2 copper ingots and 4 iron nuggets make a gate). Silver metal in the simple style (gen/fence101.py), the vanilla fence shape, collision a block and a half high (boxes up to 24 px, stable since 1.26.0); the fence sets its own four link states from the script like the End Fence (equipment/endFence.js: to Aluminum Fences, gates at their ends, walls, other fences and solid blocks), the gate opens and shuts when used (state zoocraft:open, a door sound) and faces the player. The park's area (park/fence.js, park/fenceRules.js): every Aluminum Fence, Aluminum Fence Gate and Park Entrance Gate block is kept in a saved list by the blocks' place and break events (no world scans); three seconds after the last change (BALANCE.park.fenceSettleTicks) a job fills over the fence's box plus a margin, 4,000 cells a slice: everything reached from the edge without crossing a fence column, sideways and diagonally (a diagonal step between two fences is a gap), is outside; if the Park Deed's spot isn't reached the fence is closed and the park is every column joined to the spot that isn't outside (the fence and any pens inside it included), at every height, saved as runs of X a row; fence more than 500 blocks from the spot (BALANCE.park.fenceReach, Austin's answer) doesn't count. A fence with a gap: the park keeps its last closed edge and every member is told roughly where the gap is (the first squeeze between fence columns on the way out). Required (Austin's answer): a park claimed from 0.1.101 has no ground until its fence closes (park.needsFence), the claim says so and the tutorial's step after the Zoo Workshop is "Fence in your park"; a park claimed before keeps its 500-block circle until it's fenced, its fence step marked done. Everything that asks whether a spot is in the park asks the fence's area (exhibits, which get an "Outside the park" problem when the edge leaves them out, Viewing Points, guest services, the Gift Shop, Tour Desks, Expedition Platforms, the entrance gate, path sweeping and the guests' path network); when a fence closes, a Park Entrance Gate in it or inside it becomes the entrance, and an entrance left outside closes the park. Conservation releases in a fenced park: at least 100 blocks beyond the fence (BALANCE.conservation.beyondFence). The Park Ledger has a Park edge line. The benchmark park (BENCH_VERSION 2) puts an Aluminum Fence round itself with a gate on the entrance side. Measured in QuickJS: a fence 701 across (the largest square within the reach) takes 209 ms of work in all, never more than 7 ms a slice; in the model park (13,455 columns inside) the fence loop costs nothing measurable, script time a tick 5.54 and 5.86 ms against 5.58 and 5.71 for 0.1.100 (run-to-run noise), the same end state (rating 92, 4.5 stars), no errors on Node or QuickJS. New blocks: close Minecraft completely and start it again. The test plan is docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.102 (2026-10-25, module 5, the Bank; ideas backlog 3, "swap Zoo Coins for any block at the bank"; Austin's answer on selling back: "Only sell"): the Zoo Bank, made at the Zoo Workshop (1 gold ingot, 1 chest, 5 stone bricks), a plain cube in the simple style (gen/bank102.py: pale stone with a gold coin on each side, a gold rim on top). It works inside the park (economy/bank.js; placing or using one outside says so). Using it opens its menu (ui/bankMenu.js): the park's coins, search by name, each player's last 6 purchases ("bought lately", collection bank) and 13 groups to browse, 40 blocks to a page (BALANCE.bank); a block, then how many from a list with each choice's cost (1, 2, 4 ... a stack, 2 stacks ... up to what fits in the inventory and what the park can pay), then Buy. Each purchase is one ledger entry with its own operation id ("Bank: 64 Oak Planks"); the coins are taken first, the save flushed, then the blocks given, and what no longer fits drops at the player's feet. Only owners and managers buy (the spend permission); keepers and visitors can look. Sandbox: free, the entry written at 0. It never buys back. Prices (data/bankPrices.js, made by gen102/prices.py from vanilla-data 1.26.52 ids and Mojang's bedrock-samples 1.26.50.4 recipes and English names): 1,066 blocks, whole coins each, at least 1; a common block 1 (a mason pays an emerald for 20 stone, so an emerald is about 20 coins), crafted blocks what their cheapest recipe's ingredients cost (a smelt adds a quarter coin), ores at what villagers pay (4 iron ingots, 3 gold ingots or 15 coal an emerald), set prices for the valuable and rare (diamond 120, emerald 60, ancient debris 400, heart of the sea 1,500, nether star 4,000 ...); packed ice 4 as at the supply shop. Never sold: the 50 creative-only, unbreakable and technical blocks (bedrock, barriers, command and structure blocks, jigsaw, light blocks, spawners and vaults, End portal frames, reinforced deepslate, the dragon egg, budding amethyst, infested blocks ...); blocks the game doesn't have (newer or experimental ones) are hidden by ItemTypes.get. Search matches every word of the name or id, plurals and "grey" too, the name itself first. Tried in the full-park mock world (every path above, 48 checks, all passed); in the model park the Bank costs nothing a tick: script time 5.91 and 5.43 ms a tick in QuickJS against 5.86 and 5.54 for 0.1.101 (run-to-run noise), the same end state (rating 92, 4.5 stars), no errors on Node or QuickJS. New block: close Minecraft completely and start it again. The test plan is docs/Bank_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.103 (2026-10-25, Austin, with a photo of a black ornamental aluminium picket fence: "The alumium fence can be a block, and it should look like this but silver"): the Aluminum Fence is a full block of silver pickets (gen/fence103.py; research/fence_plan.md, the 0.1.103 section). Each of its four sides is a flat see-through panel: a 1 px post at each edge with a flat grey cap (two blocks side by side make a 2 px post, a corner a post on both outer faces), a double top rail, a bottom rail a pixel above the ground and four pickets 2 px apart, drawn from outside only (alpha_test_single_sided) so the far side never shows through the gaps, with a thin solid top rail behind each panel so the fence shows from above. Culling rules (RP/block_culling/aluminum_fence.json, condition same_block, the default against full blocks kept; culling_layer is still experimental, so not used) hide a side against another Aluminum Fence or a full block, so a run reads as one fence with no inner faces seen through the gaps at an angle, and a fence against a wall doesn't flicker. No link states, arms or permutations any more: full-block collision a block and a half high (24 px, as before) and a full selection box, so a row places like any block and nothing jumps it; park/fence.js no longer sets a permutation on placing, and equipment/endFence.js sets arms on End Fences only (they still reach out to an Aluminum Fence as to any solid block). The Aluminum Fence Gate matches: two square posts in the fence's post column and a 12 px picket panel, one panel shut (both faces drawn) and two halves swung out toward the front when open; its swing, collision and placement are unchanged. The 0.1.101 textures aluminum and aluminum_dark stay in the pack unused. The park's fence list, fill, messages and tutorial are untouched (a fence from before loads as the new block). 266 tests; model park: no errors on Node or QuickJS, the same end state (rating 92, 4.5 stars, 13,455 fence columns), QuickJS 5.77 ms of script a tick against 5.91 and 5.43 for 0.1.102 (noise). Replaced block files and a new culling file: close Minecraft completely and start it again. Test plan: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (the 0.1.103 section).
- 0.1.104 (2026-10-25, Austin: "can you make a zoocrsft build command with the aluminum fences and a 100 x 100 zoo so I can test easier", then "Make an exhibit for all of our current species in it, and make sure the animals are in it as well"): `/zoocraft:build [size]` (cheats on; a new Superflat world, or a park claimed and still empty) builds a test zoo in one go: the park claimed where you stand if there's none (an empty park is built round its deed's spot), an exhibit for every one of the 34 species with its animals in it (147 in all), each built the way its test plan says a good exhibit is built (bench/parkPlan.js: the benchmark's 20 blueprints and 14 new ones: rabbits, otters, capybaras, deer under an oak canopy, penguins with a Cooling Unit, giraffes with Elevated Browse Feeders, elephants with a mud wallow, hedgehogs in a nocturnal house lit by Nocturnal Lamps, Basalt Tortoises and Ember Foxes with a Thermal Regulator and a Lava Trough, Lava Rays in a lava pool with a Thermal Feeder set in the bank, Striders on lava with magma on the bank, Chorus Deer on end stone, Rift Ferrets with a Containment Anchor and Rift Tunnels), packed in rows either side of an avenue (packRows: the deepest first, each on the first side with room, so a ring holds them in the fewest rows), paths, Viewing Points, guest services, 5 Staff Rooms with keepers, and round it all an Aluminum Fence ring with a gate in the middle of the north, east and west sides and the Park Entrance Gate in the middle of the south side (bench/zooPlan.js ringPlan); the park's area comes from that fence and the park opens. The ring is `size` columns a side (100 unless told, 10 to 250) or the smallest that holds every exhibit: 34 take 142 x 142, and the build says when it grew. The benchmark's build job does the work (bench/benchmark.js runBuild with a mode: the benchmark keeps its records and caps; the zoo has the usual caps, 6 keepers and 6 guests in view, and the same 100,000-coin grant for wages), and refuses a park with exhibits or the benchmark's world; the benchmark park itself is unchanged (BENCH_VERSION 2, the same 922 blocks). A lesson kept in the blueprints: a fixture against a 2-high wall is a step over it (the otters' Water Filter, the penguins' Cooling Unit, the Striders' feeder and the Rift Ferrets' anchor let the measure out of their pens in the mock world until the walls were 3 high or the fixture stood back). Tried in the full-park mock world (the default, a 160 ring, and a park claimed first: all 34 occupied, 147 animals live in their exhibits, the ring closed with the entrance as the park's, guests and keepers at work, no errors; a second run refused); the model park unchanged (rating 92, 4.5 stars, no errors, per tick the same as 0.1.103). 267 tests. Scripts only: leave the world and join again (0.1.103's fence block needs its full restart first). The test plan is docs/Test_zoo_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.105 (2026-10-26, Austin: "An error", with the build's refusal "the game won't take minecraft:snow_block"): the penguins' bank in the test zoo was floored with minecraft:snow_block, the Java Edition name; Bedrock's full snow block is minecraft:snow (as the penguin rules and the Cooling Unit already use), so the build's check refused the plan before placing anything, as it should. Fixed in bench/parkPlan.js; a test now holds every vanilla block id the zoo and benchmark plans place against Bedrock's names (@minecraft/vanilla-data 1.26.52), with the look-alike Java names noted. 268 tests; the mock world's test zoo builds as before. Scripts only: leave the world and join again, then `/zoocraft:build` in the same world (it changed nothing).
- 0.1.106 (2026-10-26, Austin: "We also should change the 500 block rule. The zoo should just be where aluminum fences enclose. That way we are not scanning 500 blocks if its just 100 x 100"): the park is exactly the ground its Aluminum Fence closes round, for every park. The 500-block circle a park from before 0.1.101 kept until it was fenced is gone (its members hear so once, the first time they join; exhibits outside a fence still work for their animals, and guests reach them again once the fence goes round them), and so is the 500-block reach on the fence: every fence column counts, however far out, and the only ground ever worked over is the fence's own box plus a one-block margin (a 100 x 100 fence is a 102 x 102 fill). The tutorial's fence step now waits for every park. The claim, the Park Ledger's Park edge line and the fence messages say nothing about 500 blocks any more; releases from a park with no fence closed yet still have to be 600 blocks from the Park Deed's spot (it has no edge to measure from). Scripts only: leave the world and join again. 268 tests; model park unchanged. Test plan: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (the 0.1.106 section).
- 0.1.107 (2026-10-26, Austin, from the laptop's content log and a screenshot: Molang errors "particles/water_splash.json ... variable.direction.x/.y/.z", "hiring keeper 7: The Staff Rooms house 6 keepers", "the aluminum fences should only be half a block visually" and "I also do not want the zoocraft park tab to be perminant on the screen, it should be togglable"): four small fixes. The flamingo's and raccoon's water splash now passes the direction vanilla's water_splash particle reads (platform/mc.js splashVars), so the content log stays clean when they splash. `/zoocraft:build` hires as many keepers as the Staff Rooms house (6) instead of the benchmark's count, so the "keeper 7" refusal is gone. The Aluminum Fence and its gate are drawn half a block tall (gen/fence107.py: the pickets, posts, rails and gate in the bottom half of the block, with the outline to match) while the collision stays a block and a half, so animals still can't jump it. The ZooCraft Park sidebar is a Park setting (Park Deed, Settings: "ZooCraft Park sidebar on screen"), on unless the owner switches it off; a scoreboard sidebar is one per world, so the switch is for everyone in the park. New block files: a full restart of Minecraft. 269 tests; model park unchanged. Test plan: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (the 0.1.107 section).
- 0.1.108 (2026-10-26, Austin: "We also need to undo the super detailed animals in this mod (Croc, Tiger, Lion, Raccoon, Zebra)"; the 2026-10-16 art rule "The details also does not need to be so high on the designs. This is minecraft remember"): the crocodile, tiger, lion, raccoon and zebra redrawn in the simple vanilla style (gen107/simple107.py). Every bone, pivot, rotation and name is kept, so all their animations, controllers, part visibility (mane, ruff, bib, lids, claws, nictitating lids) and scripts run as before; only the cubes and skins are new: the crocodile 240 cubes to 30, the lion 76 to 32, the tiger 73 to 30, the zebra 53 to 29, the raccoon 36 to 17, all at one texel a unit on one sheet each (crocodile 128 x 128, lion/tiger/zebra 128 x 256, raccoon 64 x 128). Sizes unchanged (life-size: the lineup in previews/0.1.108/size_lineup.png). All 21 coats redrawn: crocodile adult 0-2 and young 0-2, tiger Bengal 0-2 and Amur 0-2, lion, lion dark, lioness, cub (spotted), raccoon grey and cinnamon, zebra Burchell's (shadow stripes), Grant's and foal. Previews: previews/0.1.108 (before_after.png and a coats sheet an animal). Resource pack only, but the skins are new sizes: a full restart of Minecraft. 269 tests (the Game skin layout and Game rotation checks pass on the new sheets). Test plan: docs/Simple_animals_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.109 (2026-10-26, module 5b, the first of four: Austin, "People should be able to have their own zoos on a world, or work together"; plan research/multipark_plan.md): room for several parks in one world, with nothing changing yet. The "park" collection holds one record a park (the first is "main", number 1, named "<owner>'s Zoo"; later ones "p2", "p3"...); `allParks()`, `parkAt(dimension, pos)` (the park whose fence holds that column), `parkOf(player)` (the park you stand in, else your own, else one you belong to) and `forEachPark()` (the clock loop runs once a park, and `getPark()` answers that park while its events run; with one park in the world it answers as it always did). Each park has its own fence area record ("area" for the first park, "area:<id>" after), each exhibit says which park it stands in, every ledger entry carries its park (the Finances page and the pruning are per park), and schema 2 marks a world from before: the park gets its number and name, exhibits and ledger entries get "main". A world still takes one park until 0.1.110. Scripts only: leave the world and join again. 270 tests; model park and test zoo unchanged.
- 0.1.110 (2026-10-26, Austin, on 0.1.107 and 0.1.108: "The aluminum fence should be a block and a half tall, but I meant that it should be half a block wide" and "The problem with the animals wasnt the details in the texture. It was them having to many voxels or squares... The details of the textures were just needed to be a little simplified"): two corrections. The Aluminum Fence is a thin panel of silver pickets, half a block wide and a block and a half tall (gen/fence110.py): a post 8 x 24 x 8 in the middle of the block and an arm 8 px thick out to each side that has a fence, a gate end or a solid block beside it (the zoocraft:n/e/s/w states, set as the End Fence's arms are, equipment/endFence.js; a placed fence lands already joined), pickets on every long face so a run reads as one fence, no lids; the gate matches (24 tall, 8 thick, posts 4 x 24 x 8). Collision and selection follow the post and arms. The five simple animals keep their 0.1.108 shapes and get their textures painted at two texels a unit with a soft fur grain, a lit top and a shaded underside, and rows of scales on the crocodile (gen107/simple110.py). Block files and skins changed: a full restart of Minecraft. 270 tests. Test plans: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (0.1.110) and docs/Simple_animals_test_plan.md (0.1.110).
- 0.1.111 (2026-10-26, Austin, on 0.1.110: "The 5 animals still look bad. I want you to get rid of every previous model/design you have made for them and start fresh with creating them how we have made the last few"): the crocodile, tiger, lion, raccoon and zebra built again from nothing, the way the red panda, gorilla and the Nether and End animals were (gen111/animlib.py, gen111/lion.py also run as the tiger, croc.py, zebra.py, raccoon.py): small rigs of one box a part (the lion 16 bones, the tiger 15, the crocodile 13, the zebra 15, the raccoon 16), skins painted in 3D at a texel to a unit in the game's own box layout (every coat kept by name: 4 lion, 6 tiger, 6 crocodile, 3 zebra, 2 raccoon), and every animation the controllers and scripts use written again in Molang for the new rigs (45 lion, 51 tiger, 39 crocodile, 43 zebra, 12 raccoon; held poses solved on the model so feet, bellies and chins meet the ground, the one-shots between them keyed so nothing sinks). Controllers, client scripts, act numbers, server entities, sizes (life-size) and collision boxes are unchanged; the render controllers lost the parts that no longer exist (ruff, bib, claws, the croc's third eyelid). Models and skins changed: a full restart of Minecraft. 270 tests (the zebra's and the crocodile's rig tests rewritten for the new bones). Test plan: docs/Simple_animals_test_plan.md (0.1.111).
- 0.1.112 (2026-10-26, Austin's content log on 0.1.111: the Aluminum Fence and its gate "minecraft:selection_box: Invalid box size ... must be inside the following limit: Vec3(-8,0,-8) and Vec3(8,16,8)", then both recipes "missing or invalid" and blocks.json "does not exist in the registry"): since 0.1.110 their selection boxes were 24 px tall, and the game refuses a whole block whose selection box leaves the block, so neither loaded. The selection boxes are 16 tall now (every permutation of the fence, both states of the gate); the collision boxes stay 24, a block and a half. The fence tests had pinned the wrong height and are corrected; a new test checks every block's selection boxes against the limit, and the plugin's pack checker now errors on it. Block files changed: a full restart. 271 tests. Test plan: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (0.1.112).
- 0.1.113 (2026-10-26, Austin on 0.1.111 with photos of a crocodile and a zebra: "Eyes are messed up here. Zebras also look bad. Look at how the red panda is made, that is a perfect example of how to go from real life to minecraft"): the five painted the red panda's way (scratchpad gen113/): flat colours set out by rule, marks a texel or two across, faces drawn texel by texel, only a little jitter (the 3D noise mottling is gone). The crocodile's eyes are two 2x2x3 bumps on top of the head with a yellow eye and black slit on the outer side, the eyelids the bumps' own skin a shade over them (they were yellow dashes painted flat on the head, like goggles); its bands clean, its teeth a sparse row. The zebra's stripes are even bands two texels black and two white (upright on the barrel and chest, level on the rump, haunch and legs, across neck, mane and face), a white belly, dark brown muzzle, drawn eyes and nostrils; Burchell's keep pale shadow stripes on the haunch. The lion and tiger heads have a 6x4x3 snout box with the nose and mouth drawn on it and amber eyes with pupils, a darker spine line, the mane tufted; tiger stripes are clean bands that stop at lengths of their own (three patterns a subspecies), bars on the legs, tail rings. The raccoon is flat grey (or cinnamon) with pale flecks, clean tail rings, and a glint for each eye in the mask. The cats' and the zebra's legs are 5 across (were 4). Animations re-solved on the new boxes (same names and lengths); controllers, scripts, sizes and collision unchanged. The "Game skin layout" test now checks the raccoon in the lion's place (a flat coat can't tell the two layouts apart; the tiger shares the lion's painter). Models and skins changed: a full restart. 271 tests. Test plan: docs/Simple_animals_test_plan.md (0.1.113).
- 0.1.114 (2026-10-26, module 5b, the second of four: Austin, "People should be able to have their own zoos on a world, or work together"; plan research/multipark_plan.md): the second park. A Park Deed claims a park of your own anywhere outside every park's Aluminum Fence and 16 blocks or more from another park's Deed spot, one park an owner (you can still be a manager or keeper of any number of others); the Deed inside someone's park says so, and a fence that closes round another park's Deed spot too is refused ("overlap": the members are told, the park keeps its last closed edge). Each park has its own name ("<owner>'s Zoo" until the Park Ledger's settings rename it; a name is one park's), fence, entrance gate and Ticket Booth, path network and guests (a guest cap each), coins, day, rating, clock (it runs while one of its members is online), keepers, guides, rides, research, quests, releases and ledger; the Park Ledger opens the park you stand in if you belong to it, else your own, else one you belong to, and names it when the world has several (a "park 2 of 3" row, and "Claim a park of your own here" when you have none); every block's menu (the gate, the booth, the Snack Stand, the Gift Shop, the Staff Room, the Tour Desk, the Expedition Platform, the Research Desk, the Zoo Bank) is the menu of the park the block stands in. The sidebar shows the park with the most members online (ties: the lowest number) with its name on the top row; everyone reads their own park's figures in the Ledger. `/zoocraft:skip` and `/zoocraft:grant` act on the runner's park; `/zoocraft:build` builds the runner's own park (claiming one where they stand if they have none); `/zoocraft:status` lists every park. How: the park in scope is lexical (park/park.js): a block's handler runs in the park its block stands in, an item's in the player's, a loop's slice in its park (`forEachPark`, `inPark`, `scopedJob`), a form's answer in the park the menu opened for (`withPark`), and `runLater`/`runTimeoutLater` carry the scope across system.run; `getPark()` with several parks and no scope answers nothing (the mock world checks that never happens). Scripts only: leave the world and join again. 272 tests; mock world parkh/scene_two.mjs (two full test zoos and a third claim); model park, test zoo and bank scenes unchanged. Next: 0.1.115, each player's own Field Journal.
- 0.1.115 (2026-10-26, Austin, on 0.1.113 and 0.1.110 in the game, with three photos: "Zebras have no eyes and their head look weird. You can also see one that when it rolls over, the body goes underground and you can only see the legs. Tiger fix is easy, it just isnt orange, it looks great though. The fence visually is what we are going for, it just doesnt connect. The texture should be a full block wide, that is an easy fix. and remember I want the block to pretty much be a verticle slab. The fence should not be one pixel wide."): three fixes. The zebra's eyes were painted six texels back from the face (the two side faces of the game's box layout run opposite ways, and the painter had them swapped), where the face's stripes hid them; they sit two texels from the face now in a pale patch, with a glint and a rim, under the blinking lids. Its head was an upright 7 x 12 x 9 block hanging under the neck; it is a longer, narrower skull (6 x 10 x 7) sloping forward and down from the neck's top with a 5 x 5 x 6 muzzle and the ears at the poll, the way a horse's sits (gen115/zebra.py; every animation re-solved on it). The roll's back pose kept the neck's forward lean, so upside down the head hung below the back and the root was lifted to it: the zebra stood on its head with its body in the air (and, as the game drew it, sunk); the neck now swings down to lie along the ground, solved so the head's lowest corner meets the back's level, and the body rolls over on the ground. The tiger's Amur coat was a sandy tan that read as white in the game; it is a paler orange than the Bengal now. The Aluminum Fence is one vertical slab a block (gen115/fence115.py): 16 wide, 24 tall, 8 thick, along x when a fence, gate end or solid block stands east or west (or nothing beside it at all), along z when one stands north or south, both crossed at a corner (the zoocraft:n/e/s/w states as before); the picket texture is a full block wide, four pickets two texels wide every four, so the pickets run on from one block to the next; a slab's end is a solid post, culled where another Aluminum Fence carries on, and its top shows the pickets' tops. The ring `/zoocraft:build` puts up and the benchmark park's edge land with their link states set from the plan (bench/zooPlan.js linkStates): a block the script places is never relinked, so the 0.1.110 ring stood as lone posts with no arms between them, which is what the photo showed. Models, skins and block files changed: a full restart of Minecraft. 272 tests. Test plans: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (0.1.115) and docs/Simple_animals_test_plan.md (0.1.115).
- 0.1.116 (2026-10-26, Austin, on 0.1.115 in a world whose ring `/zoocraft:build` had put up before it, with a photo: "The fence problem is they only work facing a certain direction"): a fence a script placed before 0.1.115 has no link states, so its slab ran along x whichever way its row went (the east and west sides of the old ring stood as slabs across the line). The fence area job (park/fence.js work) now sets every listed Aluminum Fence's states from its neighbours as it checks the list, once after the world loads and after every fence change, and says how many it joined in the log; a fence already right is left alone (equipment/endFence.js relinkBlock). Scripts only: leave the world and join again, and the old ring joins up within a few seconds of loading. 272 tests; the test zoo mock scene strips three fences of their states and sees them joined again. Test plan: docs/Park_fence_test_plan.md (0.1.116).
- 0.1.117 (2026-10-26, module 5b, the third of four: Austin, "Field journals should be individual unlocks"; plan research/multipark_plan.md): the Field Journal is each player's own. Watching an animal fills in the watcher's page and tells the watcher alone ("Field Journal: You spotted one: Zebra. Now: Observed. Next: ..."); the journal goes with the player to any park. Catching a wild animal, releasing one to the wild (the care-notes rule) and the Research Desk's locks read the player's own journal (the research itself stays the park's, paid with its coins); the park's End expedition and conservation programme read what any of its members knows. On your first join after the update, every species in a park you belong to is filled in already (a member made later watches the animals like anyone else); a world whose journal was the park's gives those pages to every member of every park once, at load, and the old records go (both in the content log). How: one record a player and species in the "discovery" collection, "<player id>:<species id>"; discovery.js levelFor/levels/canMove/doneTasks take the player, parkLevels(park) the park. Scripts only: leave the world and join again. 273 tests; mock world parkh/scene_journal.mjs (the migration, the first join, a keeper's own page, the locks) and scene_two.mjs unchanged. Test plan: docs/Multipark_test_plan.md (0.1.117). Next: multipark polish (a two-park benchmark run, docs), then module 6 aquariums and fish.
- 0.1.118 (2026-10-26, module 5b, the last of four: the polish; plan research/multipark_plan.md): the benchmark's two-park run. `/zoocraft:benchmark build 2` builds the benchmark park round you and a second one 300 blocks east (owned by nobody who plays, "Benchmark 2's Zoo", with you as its manager so its clock runs while you're on; its own ticking area; you stay at the first park's viewpoint), says "park 1 of 2 built" between them and "Benchmark park built" after the second with both parks' counts; `/zoocraft:benchmark run` then measures every park of the world and the readout's park line says "2 parks: 40 exhibits ...". `build` alone is the one park as before; "build 3" is refused (1 or 2). How: park/park.js claimParkAs(owner, dimension, spot) (claimPark is the player's case of it), bench/benchmark.js nextPark after the first build's job ends (runLater), parkFacts over allParks. Scripts only: leave the world and join again. 274 tests; the benchmark mock scene with PARKS=2 builds and measures both. Test plan: docs/Multipark_test_plan.md (0.1.118). Module 5b done; next module 6, aquariums and fish (idea 9).
- 0.1.119 (2026-10-26, module 6, the first of two: tanks and the first four fish; Austin's idea 9 "Add fish species so zoos can have an aquarium. Players can gather fish in large numbers, and fish need less watching than the animals"; plan research/aquarium_plan.md, facts research/aquarium_facts.md): Cod, Salmon, Tropical Fish and Pufferfish as zoo animals (the game's own fish models and skins), kept in SCHOOLS. A Water Transport Tank (4 glass, 2 iron ingots, a water bucket) used on a wild fish takes it and its kind within 6 blocks, up to 16, no bait, as many as some tank of yours has room for; the school is one animal record (named, with its welfare) and the tank's lore says "10 Cod". Used on the bank of an aquarium it lets the school into the water. An aquarium is an exhibit with a tank in it (walls of glass if you like), measured with the Habitat Marker on the ground beside the water: the fish's space is the water itself (16 cells for a school and 1 more a fish), at least 60% of the exhibit water, 2 deep in 4 spots. The Aquarium Feeder (3 glass panes, an iron ingot, a stick) hangs over the water or stands in it and holds 96 units of Fish Flakes (a dried kelp and wheat seeds make 4); every fish eats from it and from nothing else, an eighth of a unit a park day (a school of 16 eats 2). At most 6 of a school are shown as mobs (one wears the name); killed ones come back; a fish killed makes the school one fewer. A vanilla fish bucket emptied into a tank joins the tank's school of its kind (or starts one). The tank on a shown fish picks the whole school up. Fish never fall ill, want no enrichment, don't breed, can't go back to the wild from the clipboard (move them in a tank). All four share the Reef community. A tropical school keeps the look (shape, pattern, two colours) of the fish it was caught from. The clipboard lists a school as "11 Cod · Welfare 85" with its own page; the keepers feed Aquarium Feeders by the fish. The test zoo has a tank a fish (a school of 12 each, 38 exhibits now); the benchmark too. How: animals/schoolRules.js (pure), animals/animals.js spawnSchool/trackFish/refillSchools/fishFromBucket, transport/carriers.js packSchool, exhibits/room.js aquaticProblem, profiles.aquarium, equipment/feeder.js capacityOf, BALANCE.aquarium. New entity, block, item and texture files: a full Minecraft restart. 275 tests; the aquarium mock scene (68 checks). Test plan: docs/Aquarium_test_plan.md. Next 0.1.120: squid and glow squid, variety bonus, moving schools, bucketing fish out.
- 0.1.120 (2026-10-27, Austin: the content log full of Molang errors on the four zoo fish, "unhandled request for unknown variable 'variable.animationamountprev'" and the swim and flop animations' animationamountblend): the vanilla fish client files blend variable.AnimationAmount with its previous value, but the game sets those two only for its own fish types (as it does variable.TropicalFish.Base), so on a zoo fish they were unknown. The four client entity files now set variable.AnimationAmountBlend themselves (a steady idle wag from query.life_time plus a faster one for the distance swum, query.modified_distance_moved); the vanilla animations and controller are unchanged. Resource files: a full Minecraft restart. 275 tests. The squid, variety bonus and the rest move to 0.1.121.
- 0.1.121 (2026-10-27, module 6, the second of two): the Squid and the Glow Squid as zoo animals (the game's own model, skins and swimming), kept in schools of up to 8 with 4 shown; caught with the Water Transport Tank like the fish and fed Fish Flakes from the Aquarium Feeder (a quarter unit a squid a park day); they share the Reef community with the four fish, so one tank can hold all six kinds. A water bucket used on a fish in a tank takes it out in the game's own fish bucket (a cod bucket, a salmon bucket, a tropical fish bucket of a look of its own, a pufferfish bucket): the school is one fewer, the last fish out ends the school; squid can't be bucketed. The variety bonus: a tank with more than one kind in it counts for more in the guests' viewing score (a quarter more a kind beyond the first, double at most). The benchmark readout counts the fish apart ("6 of them schools of 60 fish in 6 tanks"). The test zoo gets a Squid Tank and a Glow Squid Tank (40 exhibits now). The tropical fish drawn right (Austin, 0.1.120 in game: "The tropical fish are just purple and one pixel wide"): the game paints its own tropical fish with a two-texture material whose two dye colours it feeds only for its own fish, so a zoo copy came out as the missing-texture purple; now the 22 named looks (Clownfish, Blue Tang, Queen Angelfish, ...) are baked as plain textures and a zoo tropical fish wears one of them, a caught shoal the look nearest its colours (the clipboard's school page says which). Moving a school from tank to tank (pick it up with an empty Water Transport Tank, let it out on the other tank's bank) was in 0.1.119 already and is in the test plan now. How: gen121/tropical121.py (the baked looks, zoocraft:look 0-21, one render controller, entity_alphatest), animals/schoolRules.js TROPICAL_LOOKS/lookIndex/lookName, gen121/squid121.py (the squid set the Molang variables the game gives only its own squid: variable.squid.tentacle_angle, swim_rotation, glow_alpha), animals.js bucketFor/bucketOut, main.js the water bucket branch, demand.js varietyFactor, visitors.js isTank, BALANCE.aquarium.varietyBonus/varietyMax, bench/report.js. New entity files: a full Minecraft restart. 276 tests; the aquarium mock scene (79 checks). Test plan: docs/Aquarium_test_plan.md steps 16 to 21.
- 0.1.123 (2026-10-27, Austin's content log from the first 0.1.121 game: `[AI][error]-zoocraft:zoo_squid | minecraft:behavior.squid_idle | ... | Can't use SquidIdleGoal unless Mob is a Squid mob`, and the same for squid_dive, squid_move_away_from_ground and squid_out_of_water on both zoo squid): the game's four squid goals are locked to its own squid class, and the vanilla squid has no navigation that swims (those goals push it about themselves), so a zoo copy keeping them never moved and logged an error a goal. The zoo Squid and Glow Squid now swim as the zoo fish and the dolphin do: `navigation.generic` (can_swim), `underwater_movement` 0.2, `random_swim` (12 across, 4 up and down), `swim_wander`, `swim_idle`; `navigation.walk` and `jump.static` gone (gen123/squid123.py). Entity files only: a rejoin is enough. 276 tests. Test plan step 22. (The laptop read 0.1.122 before this: a second build.cmd run bumped it with no change.)
- 0.1.124 (2026-10-27, module 6b part 1, Austin: "next module", the trench row with "whale sharks are passive"): the Whale Shark, the first big fish: a single named animal in a giant tank, not a school. The giant tank habitat profile needs 600 water cells for the first (about 30 by 20) and 150 more each, 400 of them 2 deep, 3 high. It is caught one at a time in the Large Water Tank (5 glass, a Water Transport Tank, a water bucket and 2 iron ingots at the Zoo Workshop), which the Marine Husbandry research project opens (after Veterinary Care and a Field Journal page on cod; 220 and 8 glass, 4 prismarine shards, a book); the tank is used on the bank beside the water like the fish tanks. It filter-feeds Fish Flakes from the Aquarium Feeder, 8 units a park day, never breeds, never falls ill in a tank, is harmless, and takes Fish Flakes as bait. Every 25 to 45 s it slows to half speed with its mouth open for 10 to 16 s (the entity's own timers set zoocraft:feeding), which the Field Journal's new task "Watch one filter feeding" sees; the guests count its tank as a tank. Wild ones spawn alone near the surface of warm and lukewarm deep oceans (height 40 to 64, weight 2, one at a time). The model is our own 15-box body with the spotted back and white belly at 4x scale (12 m), swimming with the generic swimming navigation as the zoo fish do. The test zoo gets a Whale Shark Tank, 32 by 22 (41 exhibits now). How: gen124/whale124.py (model, skin, animations and controllers, entities, spawn rule, items, recipe, lang), gen124/wire124.py (profiles.js giant_tank, items.js CARRIERS.marine, research.js marine_husbandry, species.js, validate.js the big-fish rule, carriers.js lets an aquatic animal into the water, benchmark.js and visitors.js count it as a tank, discovery/rules.js gulp, discovery.js bigFishSight, parkPlan.js). New entity, item and texture files: a full Minecraft restart. 277 tests; the four mock scenes with 41 species. Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 1 to 9. Next: 0.1.125 the Great White and the Hammerhead (dangerous, in the water: the validate rule for dangerous species is relaxed then).
- 0.1.125 (2026-10-27, module 6b part 2, Austin: "great whites, hammerheads ... They should be dangerous"): the Great White Shark and the Hammerhead Shark, the first dangerous big fish. Each is one named animal in a large tank (256 water cells for the first and 64 more each, 128 of them 2 deep), eating Fish Rations from the Aquarium Feeder (which now holds Fish Rations as well as Fish Flakes; 3 units a park day for a great white, 2 for a hammerhead), never breeding. The danger is the predators' warn-then-charge with water moves: a loose shark circles anyone swimming in its tank in Survival or Adventure (the warn on the action bar), then rushes and bites (3 hearts and a shove) and backs off; never across the glass, never in Creative. A shark's tank needs no Holding Den or double Keeper Gate: keepers fill the Aquarium Feeder from the bank and never go in, and the clipboard says so. Wild great whites (alone, deep oceans) and hammerheads (ones and threes, warm and lukewarm oceans) hunt swimmers with the game's own targeting (3 hearts a bite). A Fish Ration thrown into the water calms one, wild or in a tank: it swims to it and eats, and only then the Large Water Tank (Marine Husbandry) takes it. The journal: the great white surges to the surface now and then ("Watch one surge up to the surface, jaws open"), the hammerhead sweeps the floor with its wide head. Models in the game's style at 2x (4.5 m and 4 m). The test zoo gets a Great White Tank (20 by 16, one) and a Hammerhead Tank (22 by 16, two): 43 exhibits. How: gen125/sharks125.py (models, skins, animations, the act controller, entities with the swim goals in zoocraft:free and the show timers in zoocraft:cruise/showing, the wild hunting and calm groups, spawn rules, lang), animals/sharkRules.js (pure: the acts, the circle, the back-off, the step, the heading) and animals/shark.js (holds a shark with zoocraft:hold while it steers it a step a tick through water, by the predators' intents; frees it after), gen125/wire125.py (species, profiles.js large_tank, validate.js the shark rule, feeder.js, predators.js keeperStop/shiftForKeeper/stepDanger/the tank bait, predatorRules.js crateBlock/dangerLine, carriers.js, animals.js, clipboard.js, research.js, discovery, main.js loops, parkPlan.js, BALANCE.shark). New entity and texture files: a full Minecraft restart. 278 tests; the four mock scenes with 43 species and a shark scene (the warn, the rush and bite, the calm, the bait, the tank, the clipboard, a park day of keepers). Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 12 to 20. Next: 0.1.126 the Goblin Shark and the Anglerfish (the dark trench tank).
- 0.1.126 (2026-10-27, module 6b part 3, Austin: goblin sharks among the dangerous ones; research/trench_plan.md): the Goblin Shark and the Anglerfish, the first animals of the dark trench tanks. The goblin shark is a slow shark (half the others' steps: it drifts up to a swimmer, then the rush and the bite as the great white's; a thrown Fish Ration calms it, the Large Water Tank takes it then), 2 Fish Rations a park day, with the jaw lunge as its show (the jaws slung forward to the snout tip, the journal's "Watch one shoot its jaws out"). The anglerfish is a harmless big fish drawn giant at 1 m (the real one is 15 cm): it hangs in the water barely moving, its lure bulb glows (an emissive texture) and now and then the mouth gapes with the rod twitching (zoocraft:luring, the entity's timers; the journal's "Watch one gape with its lure"), 1 Fish Ration a park day; the Large Water Tank takes it as it is. Both live in the dark: the trench tank (200 water cells for a goblin shark, 100 of them 2 deep, 50 more each) and the dark tank (64 for an anglerfish, 32 deep, 16 more each) are measured for light as a nocturnal house is, on the water and the bank: no daylight and nothing brighter than the Nocturnal Lamps' 7 (glow lichen and a sea pickle or two are vanilla ways to light one). The clipboard's "Dark tank: yes/no" line, a care warning that names the fix, the Field Journal's line, and comfort capped at 50 in daylight (the Chorus Moths' dim cap). Wild ones spawn alone low in deep oceans (height 48 and under). The test zoo gets a Goblin Shark Tank (18 by 14, stone walls, a tinted front, a stone roof, seven lamps) and an Anglerfish Tank (10 by 9): 45 exhibits. How: gen126/trench126.py (models, skins, animations, controllers, entities, spawn rules, lang), gen126/wire126.py (species, profiles.js trench_tank and dark_tank, validate.js sharkPace and lure, sharkRules.js lunge and paceFor's scale, shark.js PACE, light.js measureHouse's kinds, exhibits.js, nightRules.js darkTankLine, welfare.js, animals.js the warning, clipboard.js, fieldJournal.js, discovery, parkPlan.js). The mock harness's light now stops at walls and tinted glass as the game's does. Also fixed: `/zoocraft:build` set a school's record in its tank and waited for the tank's second measure before spawning its shown fish, and the refill that runs every 100 ticks could land in that wait and spawn the school first, so a tank showed 12 fish with two named (seen in the aquarium scene, timing-dependent); the mobs now spawn before the second measure. New entity and texture files: a full Minecraft restart. 279 tests; the five mock scenes with 45 species and a trench scene (both tanks dark, the slow circle and rush, the roof off and the warning, the anglerfish in and out of the Large Water Tank). Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 21 to 28. Next: 0.1.127 the Giant Stingray and the Jellyfish.
- 0.1.127 (2026-10-27, module 6b part 4, Austin: "giant stingrays ... at the bottom" and "big glowing beautiful jelly fish", "keep them harmless", "they should drift very slowly"; research/trench_plan.md): the Giant Stingray and the Jellyfish. The stingray is drawn as the smooth stingray, the largest stingray of the sea (2 m across, 4 m long with its tail), rather than the plan's manta, because it lives and buries itself on the sea floor: a harmless big fish, one named animal in a ray tank (144 water cells, 72 of them 2 deep, 48 more each), 2 Fish Rations a park day from the Aquarium Feeder, taken by the Large Water Tank as it is. It wants a sand floor: the measure counts, down each column of the tank's water, the water blocks with sand (sand, red sand or suspicious sand) at the foot of the water, and sand under half the water (32 blocks at least) is enough; the clipboard's "Sand floor: yes/no" line, a care warning that names the fix, the Field Journal's line, and comfort capped at 50 without it. Every minute or so (40 to 90 seconds) a stingray near a player glides down to one of the measured sand spots (in the wild, the sand under it or round it) and lies buried there, its body sunk into the sand with only the eyes above (zoocraft:act 2; the journal's "Watch one lie buried in the sand"), for 15 to 30 seconds or until someone comes within 2.5 blocks, then bursts out and swims on; out of every player's range it is let go, and one left buried when the world was saved swims again when it is next seen. The jellyfish is a harmless school (8 at most, 4 shown) in a jelly tank (48 water cells, 32 deep, 6 more each) on Fish Flakes: big glowing bells at 1.25x on the emissive material, in crimson, sapphire and violet shown by turns (a school's colours now go by turns whenever its species has more than one), drifting at a tenth of a cod's speed with nothing that tempts, chases or flees, the bell pulsing and the tentacles swaying; one flashes a ring of light round its bell when someone swims within 2.5 blocks, and now and then by itself (zoocraft:flashing, the entity's timers; the journal's "Watch one flash"). Wild stingrays spawn alone in warm and lukewarm oceans, wild jellyfish in twos to fours in deep oceans, each a colour at random. The test zoo gets a Stingray Tank (16 by 13, water 2 deep over sand) and a Jellyfish Tank (12 by 10, water 3 deep, a school of 6): 47 exhibits. Also fixed: the anglerfish's teeth gaps were drawn see-through, which the glowing material shows as black, not as gaps; they are dark mouth now. How: gen127/rayjelly127.py (models, skins, animations, the ray's act controller, the jellies' render controller with the colour array and the flash shown only while flashing, entities, spawn rules, lang), animals/rayRules.js (pure: the acts, the sand needed, the clipboard line and warning, the bed under a column of water, the spot to settle on, the burst), animals/ray.js (the burying steered a step a tick through water, by zoocraft:hold, zoocraft:burst and zoocraft:free) and animals/jelly.js (the flash when someone swims close), gen127/wire127.py (species, profiles.js ray_tank and jelly_tank, validate.js sandBed and flashes, balance.js, blocks.js "sand" for readers that ask, mc.js, geometry.js the sand count, exhibits.js, welfare.js, animals.js, clipboard.js, fieldJournal.js, discovery, main.js loops, parkPlan.js). New entity and texture files: a full Minecraft restart. 280 tests; the six mock scenes with 47 species (the shark scene now starts its great white in the middle of its water, as it could spawn by the bank where its circle waits on the wall) and a ray scene (the sand measured, the burying and the burst, the sand taken up and the warning, the jellies' colours and flash, a wild stingray in a little sea). Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 29 to 35. Next: 0.1.128 the sharks' faces with rows of sharp teeth and a more detailed anglerfish (Austin 2026-10-27), then the Catfish and the Manatee.
- 0.1.128 (2026-10-28, Austin after 0.1.126 in the game: "The shapes of the sharks look good, but the faces look bad. There definitely has to be some sharp teeth on the sharks. The angler fish also needs more detail."): the faces redrawn, the bodies kept.
