The great white has a stepped pointed snout over an underslung mouth with a row of big triangular upper teeth and narrower lower ones (each tooth a box turned 45 degrees about its own centre on the gum line, so a sharp point shows), red gums, a dark throat that shows when the jaw drops, a lip line, black eyes with a glint, nostrils and five gill slits; the hammerhead the notched hammer with its nostrils and a smaller arched mouth of teeth under the head; the goblin shark long nail-like front teeth jutting out of both jaws and its upper jaw as its own bone ("maw"), slung forward with the lower one in the rush, the lunge and the eat. The mouths hang a little open at rest (12 degrees, the goblin shark 6). The anglerfish (Austin's ask outweighs the low-detail art rule for it) is rebuilt as the humpback anglerfish: a stepped round black body with velvety skin, spinules and rows of lateral-line papillae, a huge mouth 18 degrees open at rest with long thin fangs in both jaws leaning in and the lower jaw jutting, small pale eyes, the rod in two parts arching forward (0.1.126's leaned back) to the glowing lure with its sheath, crest and filaments, fan-shaped pectoral fins, the second dorsal and anal fins far back and a rayed tail fan; every texel it shows is opaque but the lure's (an alpha of 0 under entity_emissive_alpha glows black). The whale shark's feeding jaw now drops 22 degrees (0.1.124 turned it -28, up into its head, so its mouth never showed open), with filter pads inside, a mouth slit with its rows of tiny teeth, nostrils, small flatter eyes and five gill slits. The skins of these five are painted in the game's own box-UV layout at two texels a model unit (twice the size the models declare; the game scales the UVs), and the giant stingray's is repainted in the game's layout: gen124 to gen127 painted the design layout (each box mirrored in x, its top front to back), which put the stingray's spots by its tail and its eyes' dark look inward. Test 281. Next: 0.1.129 the Catfish and the Manatee.
- 0.1.129 (2026-10-28, module 6b part 5, idea 14 "catfish" and "manatees"; research/trench_plan.md): the Catfish and the Manatee. The catfish (the channel catfish, 0.6 m, at 0.75x) is a harmless school (8 at most, 4 shown) in an aquarium on Fish Flakes, caught by the Water Transport Tank: every 30 to 70 seconds one near a player glides down to the floor under it and creeps along it nose down for 8 to 16 seconds, its heading wandering slowly and turning round on the spot at a wall, a drop or the water's edge, then swims again (animals/catfish.js; the act drives the head-down whisker sweep; the journal's "Watch one creep along the floor nose down, sweeping its whiskers"). The manatee (the West Indian manatee, 3 m, drawn at half size and scaled 2x) is one named animal in the new lagoon (160 water cells for the first and 60 more each, 80 of them 2 deep), a mammal of the water (species.aquaticMammal): it breeds (one calf after 5 park days, born in the water beside its mother, a lagoon needs no sheltered corner), the Large Water Tank (Marine Husbandry) takes it and a Leafy Ration tempts it, harmless. It eats Leafy Rations from an Aquarium Feeder (which takes them now) and grazes the seagrass and kelp growing in its water: the measure counts the water columns with seagrass or kelp in them for a lagoon's reader (blocks.js "seagrass" for readers that ask, water to the flood and deep water under the surface), 6 for each (a calf half); with enough, 40% of its food comes from grazing and its enrichment holds, without it comfort can't rise above 55 (the clipboard's "Seagrass: yes/no" line and a warning that names the fix). Near a player it goes down to a measured seagrass spot and grazes head down, and every 3 to 5 minutes rises to just under the surface and holds its nostrils out a few seconds with the blowhole's puff (animals/manatee.js; the journal's "Watch one rise and put its nostrils out of the water to breathe"); its glides go all the way to the spot. A guest favourite: its exhibit's view counts a quarter more. Wild catfish spawn 2 to 4 in rivers, swamps and mangrove swamps; wild manatees alone or in twos in rivers, mangrove swamps and warm and lukewarm ocean shallows. The test zoo gets a Catfish Tank and a Manatee Lagoon (20 by 15, water 3 deep over sand, 32 columns of seagrass): 49 exhibits. Also fixed: a big fish's enrichment (the sharks, the stingray, the anglerfish) ran down 15 a park day with nothing in a tank to restore it, to critical in about four park days and then illness; it is held now, as a school's. How: gen129/river129.py (models, skins, animations, controllers, entities, spawn rules, lang), animals/catfishRules.js and catfish.js, animals/manateeRules.js and manatee.js, gen129/wire129.py (species, profiles.js lagoon, validate.js bottomFeeder, aquaticMammal and seagrass, balance.js, blocks.js, mc.js, geometry.js the seagrass count, exhibits.js, welfare.js, animals.js, breeding.js and nursery.js the calf in the water, feeder.js, carriers.js, research.js, visitors.js, benchmark.js, clipboard.js, fieldJournal.js, discovery, main.js loops, parkPlan.js). New entity and texture files: a full Minecraft restart. 282 tests; the mock scenes with 49 species and a river scene (the catfish down to the gravel and creeping, turning slowly at a wall; the lagoon's 32 seagrass columns and the clipboard; grazing and breathing at the surface; breeding on and a calf born in the water; the Large Water Tank; the seagrass taken out and the cap; wild ones foraging and grazing; the big fish's enrichment held). Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 42 to 51. Next: 0.1.130 the Deep Trench biome.
- 0.1.130 (2026-10-28, module 6b part 6, idea 14 "deep ocean trench"; research/trench_plan.md, trench_biome_facts.md): the Deep Trench. A custom biome `zoocraft:deep_trench` (BP biomes/deep_trench.biome.json, format 1.26.30, no experiment since 1.21.110) replaces 35% of the deep ocean and the deep cold ocean in big patches (replace_biomes, noise_frequency_scale 8): a deepslate sea floor, the vanilla ocean tags kept (deep, ocean, overworld, monster, high_seas, fast_fishing) and our tag zoocraft_trench. Its look (RP biomes/deep_trench.client_biome.json, fogs/deep_trench_fog_setting.json): nearly black navy water, underwater fog closing in at 18 blocks, a grey sea mist above, the deep ocean's underwater sounds and music. A biome can't reshape the ground, so the trenches are carved by script (world/trenchRules.js pure, world/trench.js): winding canyons on a fixed pattern of x and z (points on a jittered 100-block grid joined east and south by chance, bent by two layers of noise), about 24 wide with a flat bottom near y -48, carved a chunk at a time in a background job when a player comes within a circle of 2 chunks (its 8 neighbours loaded): only columns in the biome whose floor is natural ground at or under y 50, only natural blocks and sea plants turned to water, air and lava under each column and on its four sides sealed with stone (deepslate below 0) first; each carved chunk recorded in the "trench" collection (a bitmap a region of 32 by 32 chunks) and never carved again; a chunk that unloads part way is tried again (three times a session). The goblin shark and anglerfish spawn only in the Deep Trench below y 40 in the dark, the great white in its upper water, the jellyfish from y -40 to 56. The Field Journal has a Places page (world/places.js: found the first time a player is in the biome, a message and the spot; before that the Park Guidebook says where to look), the species' Where lines name the trench, /zoocraft:status has a deep trench line. How: gen130/biome130.py (biome, client biome, fog, spawn rules), gen130/wire130.py (balance.js trench numbers, main.js loops, commands.js, fieldJournal.js Places, research.js, species.js, db.js). New biome and fog files: a full Minecraft restart, and a new world (or new chunks) to see it. 283 tests; a mock-ocean scene (every trench column water from its bottom to the floor and nothing else under the floor, a cave and lava at the walls sealed, a shipwreck, a seamount and the plain deep ocean left alone, kelp and ore in a trench carved, every chunk recorded, blocks placed afterwards kept and a chunk with its record taken away carved again, the journal's message and Places page; with chunks loading, leaving part way leaks nothing and the chunks are finished on coming back). Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 52 to 59. Module 6b is done once Austin has seen it in the game. Next: module 6c (the NPCs) when Austin's list comes.
- 0.1.131 (2026-10-28, module 6b part 7): the Deep Trench loads. Austin's content log on 0.1.130 (game 26.60): "minecraft:replace_biomes | No biome found with name: 'deep_ocean'", "targets[0]: Missing referenced asset deep_ocean" (and deep_cold_ocean), then "Loaded client biome but no biome with that name exists: zoocraft:deep_trench"; `/locate biome` had no zoocraft biome. The replace_biomes targets were bare names, as the docs say ("Target biomes must not contain namespaces": Microsoft Learn, bedrock.dev, Mojang's schema text and the wikis all still say it). The game is right and the text is stale: since 1.26.40 the targets are a "Biome Reference" (Mojang's json_schemas, client_server/common/legacy/Reference 3.json; it was a plain "Reference" string in 1.26.30), the same type vanilla biomes fill with full ids (forest's "hills_transformation": "minecraft:forest_hills"), and vanilla biome ids have been "minecraft:deep_ocean" since at least 1.21.100. The biome (BP biomes/deep_trench.biome.json) now targets "minecraft:deep_ocean" and "minecraft:deep_cold_ocean"; nothing else changed. How: gen131/biome131.py. 284 tests: the 0.1.130 test expects the full ids, and a new test checks every custom biome's targets against the vanilla biome list (@minecraft/vanilla-data 1.26.52). A full Minecraft restart and a new world. Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 60 to 62, then 52 to 59. Module 6b is done once Austin has seen the Deep Trench in the game. Next: module 6c (the NPCs) when Austin's list comes.
- 0.1.132 (2026-10-28, module 6b part 8): the Deep Trench's abyss. Austin, having seen 0.1.131's biome: "If you research the bloop addon for bedrock, I want the ocean to be similar to that", then "The depth is the most important thing. The oceans go very deep in the bloop addon, we can have our own look so it is not straight up copying". A biome can't reshape the ground, so the add-on digs it (world/trench.js, the rules in world/abyssRules.js, pure): each column in the biome down to about y -40 (abyss -40, give or take 8 over 90 blocks and 2 over 12), the old trench lines (trenchRules.js) canyons to about -56; within 16 blocks of the biome's edge (the nearest 4 by 4 biome cell outside, from the chunk and the chunks round it) the floor rises to a cliff in wandering ledges with a rough face, starting above the old floor so the edge itself isn't dug. Seals (air and lava to stone or deepslate) first, then water, natural blocks only, as in 0.1.130. Then the life on the new floor, all from hashes of x and z so chunks meet without seams: spire clusters (64-block cells, dark or calcite, amethyst flecks, a few lanterns, capped at y 48), coral reefs (mounds or canopies, fans on top), kelp (thin in the deep), sea grass, and in the dark sea lanterns, sea pickles and glow lichen, given water with Block.setWaterlogged (only so do they glow); amethyst veins in the bared walls. Dug chunks go in a new record ("abyss"), so the chunks 0.1.130 and 0.1.131 carved are dug again; chunks within 3 of a player are looked at, each dug once the chunks round it are loaded. The look: water #17A9B9 at 0.6, water fog teal #0E8396 to 64 blocks with the vanilla eyes-adjusting fog, no air or weather fog, the default colour grading. Every block and state checked against Mojang's block data (gen132/ids132.mjs). Mock ocean (parkh/scene_abyss.mjs, also with chunks unloading at simulation distances 4 and 6): every block as an order-free model says, no leaks, about 300 fills and 13,000 blocks a chunk, worst job step 14 ms in the mock. 285 tests. How: gen132/abyss132.py, tests132.py, docs132.py. A full Minecraft restart; the 0.1.131 world works. Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 63 to 70. Module 6b is done once Austin has seen the abyss in the game. Next: module 6c (the NPCs) when Austin's list comes.
- 0.1.133 (2026-10-28, module 6b part 9): the abyss made with the world. Austin, on 0.1.132 in game: "This just looks like a bunch of chunks deleted ... you need to find a way to have everything load naturally as a biome instead of you putting in one chunk at a time. Research how to do it and get it done." The 0.1.132 job's own fault: each chunk's first row and column of blocks were left standing (seen in a scan, x = 912 "water*23,stone*40,deepslate*60" between dug columns). Now the abyss is world generation: the BP's feature_rules/trench_abyss_rule (first pass, every ocean chunk) runs one scatter over the chunk's 16 by 16 grid (257 iterations: 256 never reached the last column), each column's floor a Molang program (a share of 18 biome samples round it gives the cliff, 0.1.132's numbers: abyss -40 give or take 8 and 2, the cliff from 46 in four ledges with a rough face, ridged-noise canyons to -56, spires in 12-block cells with a cluster noise, left as natural rock up to y 28), then water from the floor to y 61. Glow lichen the game hangs on a chunk's rock from the chunk next door before that chunk is dug was left floating along the chunk lines (595 in a 192 by 192 scan, every one on a chunk's side): each side column now also clears glow lichen in the neighbouring column over the height it digs. After the surface pass: sea lanterns, waterlogged sea pickles and glow lichen on the deep floor, calcite and amethyst patches, coral heaps with fans (one in about 45 chunks of 100). The game's own kelp and sea grass find the new floor; its pickle and coral features can't be placed from a pack's scatter (nothing placed). main.js no longer runs the digging job (world/trench.js kept, retired); /zoocraft:status says the abyss is made with the world. Tried on Mojang's Bedrock Dedicated Server 1.26.60 in the cloud (seed 12345): 400 chunks with half of them Deep Trench made in about 7 s against 5.8 s without it; scans show one smooth abyss, no seams. tests/molang.mjs reads the features' Molang so the test checks the floor in a made-up ocean. 286 tests. How: gen133/features133.py, abyss133.py, tests133.py, docs133.py. A full Minecraft restart and a new world (only chunks made after it). Test plan: docs/Trench_test_plan.md steps 71 to 78. Module 6b is done once Austin has seen the abyss in the game. Next: the mob work Austin picks.
- 0.1.134 (2026-10-29, after module 6b): wild spawning checked for every animal. Austin: "start the generation of all of our mobs", then the card's pick "Wild spawning". Each wild_* spawn rule was tried on Mojang's Bedrock Dedicated Server 1.26.60 in the cloud (seed 12345, bench in the scratchpad's bdstools: a simulated player held at a site with a ticking area round it), by day and by night, over the Overworld, the Nether and the End, first at the shipped weights and then with weights raised to 100 (can it spawn at all?). With 0.1.133's rules 32 of the 41 spawned; 9 never did at any weight: the catfish, manatee, giant stingray, jellyfish, anglerfish, goblin shark, whale shark, great white and Basalt Tortoise. Found on the server: (1) a rule with spawns_underwater and neither spawns_on_surface nor spawns_underground never spawns (0 of the six at weight 100); with spawns_on_surface the game places a water animal on the sea floor (the first water block over it), as vanilla's fish. (2) Under water the light drops about one a block, so a deep floor is dark (light 0), and animals appear 24 to 44 blocks from the player (all 150 wild spawns measured were): the whale shark (y 40 to 64, light 7 or more) and the great white (y 36 to 64, light 4 or more, in the Deep Trench whose floor is the abyss) never fitted a floor, and the hammerhead (y 36 to 64, light 4 or more) only fitted shallow ones. (3) Every spawn in the basalt deltas (51 of 51: magma cubes, striders, skeletons, Cinder Boars, a ghast) stood on netherrack, none on basalt or blackstone, and the Basalt Tortoise's block filter left netherrack out. Fixes: the six get spawns_on_surface; the whale shark and hammerhead take y 0 to 64 and the great white y -64 to 64, with no light floor (as vanilla's dolphin); the anglerfish and goblin shark keep their dark filter (light 0 to 4, y -60 to 40); the Basalt Tortoise may stand on netherrack. After the fixes all 41 spawned on the server. Then each animal was looked for at its shipped weight (stays of two to five minutes where it lives; glow squid, weight 10 with no light limit below y 30, share the abyss's dark water, and only about a quarter of the abyss floor is dark since 0.1.133's glowing life): the anglerfish and goblin shark (none in 13 minutes in the abyss), the whale shark (none in 12 minutes of deep lukewarm ocean) and the Basalt Tortoise (its 1.3-block shell fits fewer spots than the Cinder Boar's) were too rare to find, so their weights went up: anglerfish 3 to 8, goblin shark 2 to 5, whale shark 2 to 5, Basalt Tortoise 6 to 12 (with them: 4 anglerfish and a goblin shark in 10 minutes, 3 whale sharks and 2 tortoises in 15). Also checked on the server: the Lava Ray (lava spawns land in the lava at the lava sea's floor; made common, it and two test copies, one narrower and one with the strider's rule, all spawned over Nether lava seas; at its real weight 3 rose from a basalt deltas lava sea in 5 minutes beside 3 striders), the Flamingo's warm-beach branch and the tiger's snowy-taiga branch (variant 1, the Amur coat). With 0.1.134's weights every one of the 41 was seen in its own biome. 287 tests (the new one checks every water rule has a placement flag, the sea animals' heights and light, and the tortoise's netherrack). How: gen134/searules.py, landrules.py, weights134.py, tests134.py, docs134.py. A full Minecraft restart; any world. Test plan: docs/Wild_spawning_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.135 (2026-10-29): wild animals easy to find and the Deep Trench about 4 times as common. Austin: "The biome looks great, it is way too rare, I only found one within 2000 X 2000 blocks of spawn. Improve the spawn rate 4X. I also did not see a single custom animal in the wild". The laptop's content log was clean (every rule loaded). Found on Mojang's Bedrock Dedicated Server 1.26.60 in the cloud (seed 12345): the wild animals spawned, but Bedrock caps the "animal" population at about 4 in each 9 x 9 chunks and ours shared it with vanilla cows, sheep, pigs and chickens; at the old weights they took about 1 in 10 in forests, taigas and jungles and 1 in 5 in the savanna (Mojang's spawn rules weigh savanna's vanilla animals 64 and forest's 49, against our 15 and 6), and plains had none. Two minutes by day in each of 14 land biomes (bdstools w37A, w43A): 12 of our land animals still around to 54 vanilla before (a zoo animal in 7 of the 14 places), 29 to 27 after (11 of 14). Weights now: zebra 24, giraffe 16, elephant 12, lion 6, deer 30, owl 8, tiger 3 and 3, gorilla 24, capybara 20, red panda 36, flamingo 18, otter 18, crocodile 6, penguin 12, hammerhead 8, giant stingray 15, manatee 12, whale shark 12, jellyfish 12, great white 5 (predators less than prey; the Nether and End animals, the river catfish, the night raccoon and hedgehog and the dark-floor trench animals keep theirs); so ours are about half the animals in the savanna and swamps, about 4 in 10 in forests, taigas and plains, most of them in meadows, mangroves and snowy plains, and about a quarter in jungles (parrots, ocelots and pandas weigh 80 to 110 there). Deer also graze plains and sunflower plains (Field Journal text too). More checks, four minutes a spot: deer at each of four more forest and taiga spots (at the flower forest, in the plains beside it), 20 of the 60 animals that spawned, about the third the weights give (bdstools w46F); a sunflower plains 7 deer of 14 animals (w44S); three jungle spots 18 of our capybaras, gorillas and tigers to 43 vanilla (w47J). The sea, two and a half minutes at each of five spots (w37B, w44S): 15 of our sea animals spawned before, 32 after (lukewarm seas 5 to 12 with a whale shark; over the trench's cliff 0 to 7, at its top 0 to 4). The trench: a locate grid every 100 blocks over 8000 x 8000 (bdstools/census135, gen135/census.py) found the old setting (amount 0.35 of deep and deep cold ocean) on 2.2% of the map with 1 patch in the 2000 x 2000 round spawn; deep and deep cold oceans are only about 5% of the map (all of them, amount 1: 4.7%, fewer merged patches; adding deep lukewarm and deep frozen: 7.5%), so the biome now also takes ocean and cold ocean at amount 0.4: 9.2% (4.1x), 3.7x the patches reaching 2 or more grid points and 4.4x those reaching 5 or more, 12 patches round spawn, the median distance to the nearest one 736 blocks (was 1,248), a trench within 1,000 blocks of 61% of places (was 40%). Frozen and warm seas untouched. A new patch next to a beach, generated and scanned on the server: the abyss stops short of the shore (maxFloor 50). 288 tests (the new one pins every wild weight, the deer's plains and the trench setting). How: gen135/weights135.py, plains135.py, trench135.py, tests135.py, docs135.py. A full Minecraft restart; the animals in any world, the trench's new patches in a NEW world. - 0.1.136 (2026-10-29): the Diving Helmet. Austin: "We need to create a helmet that let's you see in water easily"; on the card, "Sight only" (no water breathing). Why water is hard to see in (Mojang's vanilla fogs): the water fog ends 60 blocks out and, each time the head goes under, its transition_fog starts at 0.01 blocks and clears over 30 seconds (60% at 5); deep water and night are dark. Worn, the helmet's script (equipment/divingHelmet.js, rules in divingRules.js, a 10-tick loop in main.js) puts its own water fog on the wearer's fog stack with `/fog @s push <fog> zoocraft_diving` (removed with `/fog @s remove zoocraft_diving` before any push and on taking it off, so the stack never holds more than one; the first look after the scripts load clears any left from before, since the stack is saved with the player). The game searches the stack top-down for each fog kind separately (Microsoft's fog docs), so only the water fog changes. RP fogs/diving_helmet_fog_setting.json (#44AFF5) and diving_helmet_trench_fog_setting.json (the Deep Trench's #0E8396, used there): water only, fog_start 16, fog_end 128, fixed, no transition_fog. Night vision while the head is under the water (water, flowing water, bubble columns, seagrass, kelp or anything waterlogged at the head), topped up to 300 ticks every 10 so it never blinks, taken off on coming up or taking the helmet off unless it has more left than that (a potion's). The item: slot.armor.head, protection 2, 275 durability (the turtle shell's), repaired with copper ingots, enchantable as a helmet, recipe 5 copper ingots round a glass pane (crafting table and Zoo Workshop). The worn model: an attachable shaped as Mojang's vanilla helmets (armor materials, helmet_layer_visible 0, controller.render.armor) on a "head" bone, a copper dome with a round window cut through the faceplate, glass side ports, a valve and a brass collar; its 16 x 16 icon. Bench (BDS 1.26.60, w53H, a simulated Survival player in a glass box of water, bdstools/helm_bp): dry 0 night vision, under 293 then 292, out 0, back 293, helmet off under 0, a 3600-tick potion kept on coming up (3560). The fog can't be seen on a server. 289 tests (the new one pins the rules, the item, recipe, attachable, geometry, icon, name, both fogs and the loop). How: gen136/helmet136.py, tests136.py, docs136.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Diving_helmet_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.137 (2026-10-29): the Lush Okapi and Cave Expeditions (module 7, the cave wing; research/cave_plan.md and cave_facts.md). A real okapi at real size (1.5 m at the shoulder, collision 0.9 x 1.9), vanilla-level detail, 128 x 64 skins (male, female, calf; the male's ossicones from minecraft:mark_variant 1), a pacing walk, a long tongue bone, swivelling ears. zoocraft:okapi (zoo) and zoocraft:wild_okapi (wild, with a spawn egg). Species: leafy diet from either feeder (browsesEither), lushGround (8 moss, 2 lush plants for each grown one, 2 at least, up to 30% of the food: browseShare), clayLick (1), cover 10%, oneMale, sexLook, hiddenCalf, breeding 1 calf (4, 6, 8 park days, scale 0.45), species.research "cave_expeditions" (the Reinforced Crate's gate, checked in transport/carriers.js on wild and zoo animals). The okapi measure (okapiRules.js measureOkapi, in exhibits.js): moss, lush plants beside the floor (at its level up to 2 above), clay, trunk faces, and the best-covered spots for a calf (ex.okapi.hideSpots). Care: comfort caps 50 (lush ground) and 60 (clay), warnings, the clipboard lines, the Field Journal's care notes and tasks (spot, tongue, eyewash, clay). Behaviour (animals/okapi.js, a 10-tick loop near players): script-steered as the grazer and the tortoise: zoocraft:seek_browse / feeder / clay / rub groups (move_to_block, the clay with target_offset [0, 1, 0] and goal 1.0) and zoocraft:moving / still bodies, zoocraft:act 0 to 9 (browse, lick, groom, rub, flehmen, alert, hide, nurse, rest); out by day, lying down at night; a held Leafy Ration interrupts anything but a startled look; a wild one startles (anyone in Survival within 10 blocks, not sneaking or baiting; its avoid_mob_type has ignore_visibility so it hears someone standing still) and its look is cut short within 5 blocks; in a zoo someone sprinting within 6. Its wild stroll, tempt and avoid are in the moving group (in the base they held their targets through still spells). The calf: lies hidden its first park day (still from the moment it is spawned), now and then gets up and walks to its mother (zoocraft:to_mother, a follow_mob filtered to the tag zoocraft_okapi_mother), who leaves off what she is doing (getting up if lying) and waits, nurses while she licks it, and lies down again; then follow_parent. Wild spawn rule: lush_caves tag, on moss, clay, rooted dirt or grass, herd 1, density underground 2, weight 40. Cave Expeditions (3 species ready; 2 lanterns, 4 copper ingots, a compass, 200 coins). Places: Lush Caves (found by standing in minecraft:lush_caves, kept with its height), and after Cave Expeditions the way to the nearest from Dimension.calculateClosestBiomeFromSeed (stable 2.10.0; 5 to 9 ms on the bench; only when the page opens, cached a minute a player or 10 seconds after a 64-block walk). The test zoo gets a 13 x 12 okapi exhibit (50 exhibits). Bench (BDS 1.26.60, bdstools/okapi_bp, engine-only copies of the BP): spawn rule 1 wild okapi in a lush cave in 2 minutes; avoid from a still player 4 to 7 s, a walking one 1.4 s; sneaking ignored; tempt to 2.1 blocks; browse 1.28, rub 1.27; clay onto the block in 3 s (from 4 blocks) and 7 s (from 11), tryLick's check true there; calf to its tagged mother 1.35 to 1.58, not to an untagged adult; lush cave columns found. Park simulator (parkh/scene_okapi.mjs, 51 checks, all pass): it found a lying mother staying down while her calf nursed and a newborn calf strolling off its spot before the script laid it down; both fixed. 290 tests. How: gen137/okapi137.py (art), ents137.py, wire137.py, okapi.js, okapiRules.js, tests137.py, docs137.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Lush_okapi_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.138 (2026-10-29): the Earth Molebear and Burrow Soil (module 7, the cave wing; research/cave_plan.md). A fantasy animal: a black bear's body at its real size (back 0.875 m, nose to tail about 1.56 blocks, collision 0.9 x 0.9 so it fits a tunnel a block high) with a mole's spade hands and velvet fur and the star-nosed mole's pink star (its own bone, flickering), vanilla-level detail, skins Slate, Loam and cub. zoocraft:molebear (zoo) and zoocraft:wild_molebear (wild, with a spawn egg). Burrow Soil (zoocraft:burrow_soil, a full block, gravel sound, Nature tab; 2 dirt, a gravel and a clay ball make 4). Species: insect diet from a Basic or Insect Feeder, burrowSoil (8 blocks beside the floor; with a face for each grown one the grubs it digs give 25% of its food: grubShare), comfort cap 50 without it, social 1 to 4, breeding 2 cubs (3, 6, 8 park days, scale 0.4), research cave_expeditions. The molebear measure (molebearRules.js measureMolebear, in exhibits.js): Burrow Soil under, beside or a block above the floor (each once) and the faces (blocks a tunnel can start into). Behaviour (animals/molebear.js, a 10-tick loop near players): script-steered: seek groups (move_to_block to Burrow Soil with search_height 0, to either feeder) and moving / still bodies, zoocraft:act 0 to 7 (dig, sniff, snap, stand, rest, buried, crawl); out by day, curled up at night. Tunnels (zoo only): from the floor beside a face it shuffles to the cell's centre, digs (setType air, the only block change it makes), snaps a grub, crawls in, and digs on to 4 blocks while the next block can be dug (canDig: Burrow Soil, horizontal at floor level, within a block of the exhibit's box, its floor, top, far side and both sides Burrow Soil or solid and not falling), so every tunnel is a dead end closed in all round; then the den (rest), up, and back out along the same cells; 4 blocks a park day each (a.dug), cubs don't dig; carried off (moved over 1.2 blocks) it leaves off; the exhibit is measured again after. A wild one: sinks into the cave floor (act buried, cosmetic, nothing changed; only on natural stone, deepslate, tuff, gravel or dirt) from a Survival player within 5 blocks who isn't sneaking or holding an Insect Ration, stays in while they're there, and climbs out after; stands to sniff at anyone within 10 (in a zoo someone sprinting within 6); comes to an Insect Ration from 8; sinks in for its rest. Wild spawn rule: below y 0 in the Overworld, not lush caves or the deep dark, on stone, deepslate, tuff, cobbled deepslate, andesite, diorite, granite, gravel or dirt, herd 1, density underground 2, weight 20. Places: the Deep Caves (below y 0 out of the sky's light, Dimension.getSkyLightLevel, not lush caves or the deep dark). Journal tasks spot, spade, starnose, rearup. The test zoo gets a 13 x 12 molebear exhibit with a 7 x 3 x 2 Burrow Soil bank (51 exhibits). Bench (BDS 1.26.60, bdstools/mole_bp, an engine-only copy of the BP): the spawn rule put 7 in an ordinary deep cave in 3 minutes, 2 at a time, none in a lush cave or the deep dark; the soil seek stopped beside a face; setType air on Burrow Soil; a teleport into a block-high tunnel held, unhurt. Park simulator (parkh/scene_molebear.mjs, 31 checks, all pass). 291 tests. How: gen138/molebear138.py (art), ents138.py, wire138.py, molebear.js, molebearRules.js, tests138.py, docs138.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Earth_molebear_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.139 (2026-10-29): the Skulker (module 7, the cave wing; research/cave_plan.md). A fantasy animal of the deep dark: the warden's shape and senses, gentle, 1.9 blocks to the top of its head (collision 0.7 x 1.9, a sculkling 0.35 x 0.95), vanilla-level detail, one coat (Sculk); its ribs, heart and tendrils are two glow layers drawn with the warden's own material (warden_bioluminescent_layer, ignore_lighting) whose strength is a Molang alpha: the heart beats slowly, fast while excited (the zoocraft:excited property); the tendrils brighten while it listens. zoocraft:skulker (zoo) and zoocraft:wild_skulker (wild, with a spawn egg). Species: insect diet from a Basic or Insect Feeder, lowLight (the nocturnal house's dark test, comfort cap 50), sculkFloor (8 blocks of sculk under the floor, cap 50 without), no shrieker in or beside the floor (cap 30), social 1 to 3, breeding 1 sculkling (4, 8, 10 park days, scale 0.5), research cave_expeditions. The skulker measure (skulkerRules.js measureSkulker, in exhibits.js): sculk under the floor and shriekers under, in or beside it (each once). Behaviour (animals/skulker.js, a 10-tick loop near players): bodies moving / still / visit, zoocraft:act 0 to 6 (listen, sniff, feed, sit, buried, emerge); up by day, at night dug into the sculk (or a sculk vein) or else sitting. Hearing: a player who moved over 0.3 blocks since the last look (half a second), not sneaking or a spectator, within 8 blocks: it gets up or rises out of the sculk first, listens 2 seconds facing them (clicking, its heart racing), then walks over (follow_mob filtered on a zoocraft_heard tag the script puts on that player only), meets within 2.5 blocks (or stuck within 5), sniffs, and pays no heed for 20 seconds; gives up past 16 blocks or 15 seconds. Ancient Cities (animals/ancientCityRules.js, a 20-tick loop, each player every 5 seconds): a zoo member in the deep dark below y -30 where 24 city blocks (deepslate tiles, bricks and polished deepslate, gray wool and carpet, soul lanterns) are in a sample of 324 cells round them: the place is found, and the first time at a city (cities 160 blocks apart) a Wild Skulker is put resting 12 to 24 blocks off out of their sight (getBlockFromRay), spawned with initialPersistence, with a heartbeat. Wild spawn rule: the deep dark only (biome tag deep_dark), underground, y -64 to 0, on sculk, deepslate or cobbled deepslate, herd 1, density underground 1, weight 4. Places: the Deep Dark (its biome; directions after Cave Expeditions) and Ancient Cities (found by the city check only). Journal tasks spot, tendrils, meet, emerge. The test zoo gets a 12 x 12 dark house with a sculk floor, a sculk catalyst (light 6) and a sculk sensor (52 exhibits). validateData now also requires every species to name its coats (the Skulker first came without, and the park simulator's test zoo build stopped there). Bench (BDS 1.26.60): follow_mob after the tag walked 14 blocks to a simulated player; the city check scored 76 to 95 in real cities and 0 elsewhere in 4 to 5 ms; hidden places found 14 to 22 blocks off; one Wild Skulker in 3 minutes in the plain deep dark, none in cities or an ordinary cave; light: closed room 0, sculk catalyst 6, soul lantern 10, torch 14. Park simulator (parkh/scene_skulker.mjs, 33 checks, all pass). 292 tests. How: gen139/skulker139.py (art), ents139.py, wire139.py, skulker.js, skulkerRules.js, ancientCityRules.js, tests139.py, docs139.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Skulker_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.140 (2026-10-29): Austin's two asks. The manatee's face redrawn (gen140/manatee140.py; the same bones and animations): a rounded muzzle with the upper lip in two lobes either side of a cleft, a chin, sparse bristle dots, nostrils on top, eyes with a glint and wrinkles; no algae or dark blotches on the head (the old random dark marks read as dirt in the game, which floors the size of each box to lay out its skin; renders now do the same, rbed/render_floor.py). The Earth Molebear twice the size: the client draws it at 2 (a cub 0.8), collision 1.8 x 1.8 (a cub 0.72), visible bounds 5; its walk to the soil or feeder ends 2.2 blocks off. Its tunnels are lanes 2 wide and 2 high (molebearRules.js: laneSides, laneColumns, laneCentre, bodyIn; canDig on a lane: every block of the slice Burrow Soil, firm under, over, ahead and both sides; measureMolebear and tunnelStart take the size and the 3 slices of open floor it stands on, and tunnelStart picks the face it has least far to shuffle to), each slice dug at once, its body drawn back 1.5 from a slice's middle so its nose (2.1 ahead) just meets the soil, up to 3 deep and 3 a park day (balance tunnelWide, tunnelHigh, bodyBack, tunnelRoom, startReach). When the engine's walk to the soil stalls (it does with a body this big), after 5 seconds it creeps over to the nearest face the measure kept (ex.molebear.faces, up to 16), in a straight line over open floor (lineClear), up to 12 blocks (approachTicks, approachRange, approachStep). The measure counts the tunnels' floor (2 high under Burrow Soil) and exhibits.js takes it off the low-roof count. Species: habitatProfile big_burrower (the large animals' 256 and 80, the old 4 of headroom; Austin's card "Large-animal room"), burrowSoil min 12; the bank 3 high and 4 wide in the advice, the warnings and the journal. Test zoo: a 20 x 19 exhibit, a bank 8 x 4 x 3 (42 beside the floor, 9 faces). Bench (BDS 1.26.60): a 1.8 body moved into 2 by 2 slices held exactly, unhurt, 3 deep and back out; its engine walk reached a face from 3 to 5 blocks, not reliably from 7 to 10 (hence the creep over); the wild spawn rule still put 2 and 4 in 3 minutes at two deep caves (2 near at a time, the cap), none in a lush cave or the deep dark. Park simulator (parkh/scene_molebear140.mjs, 36 checks, all pass). 293 tests. How: gen140/manatee140.py, wire140.py, molebearRules.js, molebear.js, tests140.py, docs140.py. A full Minecraft restart. - 0.1.141 (2026-10-30): the Lava Falls (module 8 biomes, research/biomes_plan.md; Austin's card: real lava). A Nether biome, zoocraft:lava_falls, replacing part of the Nether wastes (minecraft:hell) and basalt deltas (amount 0.25, noise 2: 13.3% of the Nether on the bench census): warped nylium over netherrack, a magma sea floor under lava seas, the warped_forest tag for the warped forest's fungi, roots, sprouts and vines, glowstone snapped to the roof (features/lava_falls_glow*), and the warped forest's client look (fog, atmospherics, nether lighting, blue spores, sounds, no music). Lava falls: zoocraft:lava_spring (looks like netherrack, ticks) placed in the roof (snapped up from y 32-44, 4 a chunk) and in netherrack cliffs (4 a chunk); world/lavaFalls.js turns it into lava and sets its open side to netherrack and back to air (a block update: lava set by script doesn't flow without one), or into netherrack where the lava wouldn't fall 8 or more blocks into the lava sea (world/lavaFallRules.js). Lava Ray spawn rule: a second condition, weight 40 in the biome. Places: lava_falls (Heat Systems; the way to the nearest from the Nether: nearPlaces now works in a place's own dimension). Heat Systems text, Lava Ray and Warped Gecko "where". Test plan docs/Lava_falls_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.142 (2026-10-30): the Chorus Meadow (module 8 biomes, research/biomes_plan.md). The End takes no custom biome, so meadows are patches made with the world on the outer islands (r > 800), by feature rules on the_end: a Molang field (query.noise, 170-block waves, threshold 0.3, edge frayed per column) turns the top end stone into zoocraft:chorus_turf column by column (surface_pass, chorus_meadow_ground) with zoocraft:end_grass on some; a pond a chunk where it holds (before_surface_pass, chorus_meadow_ponds: the middle 5-10 blocks in, radius 2.6-4.2, level = lowest of 8 points a block past the edge); whitewood trees (tall, young, old/fancy) on the turf and chorus stalks with a branch (after_surface_pass). Bench census: 26.7% of outer island land. Blocks: chorus_turf, whitewood_log (block_face pillar), whitewood_planks (+ recipe, sticks), whitewood_leaves (minecraft:leaves tag, no decay), end_grass (cross, passable). Script world/meadow.js (rules meadowRules.js): every 20 ticks, turf under or 4 blocks round a player in the End past 780 blocks = in a meadow (out after 3 looks without): fog push zoocraft:fog_chorus_meadow (tag zoocraft_meadow), motes zoocraft:meadow_mote, chimes, a title on walking in (again after 2 minutes out), Places chorus_meadow (byScript, Dimensional Preparation), endermen on meadow ground near the player sent away; entitySpawn removes endermen spawned on meadow ground (not within 16 blocks of a player). Chorus Deer spawn rule: a second condition on chorus_turf, weight 40. Chorus Turf counts as end stone (END_PATTERNS, release ground); End Grass passable. Test plan docs/Chorus_meadow_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.143 (2026-10-30): the Rift Willows (module 8 biomes, research/biomes_plan.md). End patches as the Chorus Meadows (0.1.142), on the same Molang field's other side (under -0.3, the meadows over +0.3, so they never meet): zoocraft:rift_turf on the top end stone (surface_pass, rift_willows_ground) with zoocraft:rift_grass on 25-70%; white willows (after_surface_pass, white_willow_rule, 2 tries a chunk at 1/2): ancient (acacia_trunk 2 wide, trunk 14-19, branches with cherry crowns, a cherry crown 15 across), great (2 wide, trunk 12-16) and small (cherry_trunk), each then hung with strands as the pale oak's hanging moss (scatter, snap_to_surface ceiling on willow leaves, growing_plant_feature DOWN, zoocraft:willow_strands with a zoocraft:tip state). Blocks: rift_turf, rift_grass (cross, passable), white_willow_log (makes 4 whitewood_planks), white_willow_leaves (minecraft:leaves tag), willow_strands (cross, passable, held from above by leaves, a log or a strand, from the side by leaves or a log). world/meadowRules.js and meadow.js now serve both patches (PATCHES: turf, name, colour, fog and its tag, particle; patchOf, meadowStep by patch id, fog commands by patch, ambience by patch; endermen only sent from meadows): fog zoocraft:fog_rift_willows (tag zoocraft_willows), sparks zoocraft:rift_spark, beacon hum and high chimes, Places rift_willows (byScript, Dimensional Preparation). Rift Ferret spawn rule: a second condition on rift_turf, weight 40. Rift Turf counts as end stone (END_PATTERNS, release ground). Test plan docs/Rift_willows_test_plan.md.

Selectors per biome with vanilla's data features: optional_beehive_feature, scatter_leaf_litter_feature, optional_fallen_oak/birch/super_birch_tree_feature, select_roofed_tree_feature, scatter_tall_grass_around_forest_foliage_feature. Every crown's share of leaves over 4 from a log (they decay once disturbed) kept within vanilla's own range (its big oak 15%). Spawn rules untouched (biome tags unchanged). - 0.1.146 (2026-10-30): Overworld trees, part 3: jungle, savanna, swamp (module 8 biomes, research/biomes_plan.md; Austin's card: replace vanilla's trees in new chunks). feature_rules/<vanilla name>.json with the vanilla identifier take over six more vanilla tree rules (jungle_surface_trees_feature, jungle_edge_surface_trees_feature, bamboo_jungle_surface_trees_feature, savanna_surface_trees_feature, savanna_mutated_surface_trees_feature, swamp_surface_swamp_foliage_feature: the last is what places the swamp's oaks and their vines), their biome filters as vanilla 1.26.60 has them; the jungle and savanna rules place at query.heightmap (stops at the water surface, so no tree grows up out of a lake), the swamp's at query.above_top_solid (the floor under shallow water); tries per chunk tuned on the bench against vanilla's tree counts. Trees (features/): jungle_giant, jungle_great, jungle_emergent (2 wide), jungle_tall, jungle_mid (jungle wood, mega_trunk with branch crowns; jungle_giant_cocoa etc. are sequence_features [tree, vanilla optional_jungle_tree_cocoa_feature] that put pods on them), jungle_bush (jungle log, oak leaves; unlike vanilla's jungle_bush_feature it may replace grass and ferns, which vanilla's can't, so it grows on the jungle floor); acacia_umbrella, acacia_wide (acacia_trunk), acacia_tiered, acacia_great, baobab (mega_trunk with acacia_canopy pads); swamp_oak_tall, swamp_oak_big, swamp_cypress (classic trunk, can_be_submerged 2, NO may_grow_through: with both the game never places a classic trunk), swamp_cypress_giant, swamp_live_oak (mega_trunk; grows in shallow water with water in may_replace). Selectors per biome mix in vanilla's select_jungle_tree_feature, savanna_tree_feature, oak_tree_feature, swamp_tree_feature and 0.1.144's oak_round. Crowns kept small (wide ones lose leaves over 4 from a log once disturbed). Mangrove swamps unchanged (game code). Spawn rules untouched (biome tags unchanged). Test plan docs/Tropical_trees_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.147 (2026-10-30): Mythical Glade, module 9's first version (research/mythical_plan.md; Austin's idea 10 and "massive fantasy looking trees"). biomes/mythical_glade.biome.json replaces part of flower_forest, meadow, cherry_grove, birch_forest and forest (full minecraft: ids; amount 0.25, noise 2: 4.1% of the land census, the nearest a median 684 blocks away), tags overworld and zoocraft_mythic only (no monster: no vanilla hostiles; no animal: no farm animals). Client biome, fog zoocraft:fog_mythical_glade, particle zoocraft:glade_mote. Ten blocks (Mythwood log/wood/planks/leaves, Moonwillow leaves, Willow Veil, Willowlight, Moonbloom, Starpetal, Wisp Fern), recipes, loot. Trees: 16 .mcstructure files (structures/zoocraft/, written by gen147/trees147.py + world147.py), placed by structure_template_feature 1.26.10 (ground_level 4, rotate_around_center, block_intersection allowlist: air, plants, leaves, ground, stone and ore; never logs, water or lava). The world generator writes a structure only into the 3 x 3 chunks round its point, so the 7 giants (up to 41 across, 94 tall) sit near the middle of chunk 0 of every 4 each way (mythic_heart: the giant, else a mid tree) and the mid and young trees grow only in the chunks between (chunk x or z 2 of 4), never in a giant's room. Each tree is guarded by a one-block feature that needs grass or dirt under it. Floor: flower drifts, grass, amethyst vegetation patches with buds. - 0.1.148 (2026-10-30): the Mythical Glade's water, module 9's second version (research/mythical_plan.md). Six glowing pools (structures/zoocraft/glade_pool_*.mcstructure, gen148/world148.py): water 1 to 2 deep in a sealed calcite basin (every water block walled in on four sides and below, so it never flows), sea lanterns in the floor, a calcite and amethyst rim flush with the turf, lily pads, amethyst clusters. Placed in the surface pass (before the trees, which never grow into water), only in the chunks between the giants' rooms, point 5 to 10 into the chunk so the pool stays in it, only on turf (the trees' guard at its point: on the bench the structure's block check let pools onto ponds and rivers), with no lake or river round its edge (the rule's y: at 8 points round it query.heightmap no higher than query.above_top_solid, which goes under water, and that at most 1 under its own, else y -60 where the guard fails; with the point alone a quarter ran their rim into a lake, and 8 scatter probes in the aggregate stopped none) and only on level ground (height_difference_filter_feature, 1.26.30: within 5 the ground at most 1 lower and 3 higher; the structure's own grounded and leveled checks let pools onto slopes on the bench). Springs: block zoocraft:glade_spring placed in cliff faces (height difference filter: the next column at least 5 lower), turned into water by world/gladeFalls.js (custom component, looping tick) when the fall below its open side is 4 or more onto anything but lava, else dirt or stone; the open side is set to stone and back to air so the water flows. Glow lichen (multiface) on the cliffs. Test plan docs/Mythical_glade_water_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.149 (2026-10-30): the Unicorn (module 9, the Mythical Glade; research/mythical_plan.md). The classic unicorn on a horse's frame at a horse's size (collision 1.2 x 2.0, a foal 0.72 x 1.2), vanilla-level detail; coats from minecraft:variant (0 Moonlit, 1 Dawn; each wild branch of entity_spawned removes the other coat's group) and a foal skin; the horn glows faintly; a horn_sparkle particle. zoocraft:unicorn (zoo) and zoocraft:wild_unicorn (wild, with a spawn egg). Species: grain diet from a Basic Feeder or a Hay Rack, carrier livestock, habitat grassland, grazing 30 grass blocks each up to 40%, gladeFlowers (Moonbloom, Starpetal or Wisp Fern on the floor: 3 each, 4 at least; comfort cap 50 without, the welfare cause "bloom"), social 2 to 6, breeding 1 foal (4, 5, 6 park days, scale 0.6), no research. The unicorn measure (unicornRules.js measureUnicorn, in exhibits.js): glowing flowers on the floor and dip spots (floor cells with water or a Water Trough on a side or a corner, level or one down). Behaviour (animals/unicorn.js, a 10-tick loop near players): bodies moving / still / seek_flowers / seek_water (the engine's move_to_block), zoocraft:act 0 to 6 (graze 1, dip 2, rear 3, alert 4, rest 5, flowers 6); the horn dip faces the water and puts sparkles over it with chime.amethyst_block; flowers and water count on a side or a corner (the engine's walk stops corner-on); a night rest is renewed till morning and cut to 1 to 10 s at sunrise (wakeTicks). Zoo ones: a keeper sprinting within 6 blocks: head up and a snort. Wild ones: a player within 10 blocks not sneaking or holding a Grain Ration: it rears (mob.horse.angry) facing them, then avoid_mob_type (max_dist 10) moves it off; tempt by a Grain Ration within 8. Navigation avoid_water false (as the vanilla camel): with it true, move_to_block towards water never started on BDS. Spawn rule: biome tag zoocraft_mythic, on grass_block, surface, light 7+, herd 1 to 3, density 4, weight 20. Journal tasks spot, moonflowers, horndip, rearing; the glade's Places text mentions Unicorns. The test zoo gets a 16 x 15 grass exhibit with 8 glowing flowers, a Hay Rack, a Basic Feeder, a Hanging Food Basket and a trough (53 exhibits; the ring grew to 172, so the "bigger ring" test asks for 200). Bench (BDS 1.26.60): 4 wild unicorns in a pen: 13 grass grazes, 10 flower grazes and 4 horn dips in 4 minutes (5 dips by three of them in the run); all reared at a keeper walking up and moved off; a Grain Ration brought one from 8.5 to 2.3 blocks; down at midnight within 30 s, up at noon within 20 s; no script errors. Spawn bench (six places in and round two glades, 3 minutes each): 6 Wild Unicorns, all in the Mythical Glade on grass_block, coats 3 and 3, none outside it. Park simulator (parkh/scene_unicorn.mjs, 39 checks, all pass). 302 tests. How: gen149/unicorn149.py (art), sheet149.py, ents149.py, wire149.py, unicorn.js, unicornRules.js, tests149.py, test149.mjs, docs149.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Unicorn_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.150 (2026-10-31): the Dragon (module 9, the Mythical Glade; research/mythical_plan.md). A movie-style dragon at the "Big" size (about 7 blocks nose to tail, an 11 to 12-block span; collision 2.4 x 2.2, a hatchling 0.9 x 0.8 at the client's 0.35 scale), vanilla-level detail; coats from minecraft:variant (0 Emerald, 1 Ember, 2 Frost), glowing eyes; the wings are cut-out planes; a dragon_fire particle coloured by the coat. zoocraft:dragon (zoo) and zoocraft:wild_dragon (wild, with a spawn egg). Species: meat diet (4 a park day, a Basic Feeder), carrier large (a Reinforced Crate; a Meat Ration in the inventory coaxes a wild one in), habitat great_aerial (aerial, lands: roofed over 16 blocks of air, 900 blocks of floor for the first and 300 for each more, no perches), warmth (a lit campfire or soul campfire on the floor: comfort cap 50 without, the welfare cause "warmth"), social 1 to 3, breeding 1 hatchling (6, 8, 10 park days, scale 0.35), no research. The dragon measure (dragonRules.js measureDragon, in exhibits.js): the lit campfires on the floor or beside it and where the first 8 are; a big empty exhibit with a lit campfire is measured for flight at the hall's height. Behaviour (animals/dragon.js: a 10-tick brain and a 1-tick mover near players, RANGE 64): zoocraft:act 0 fly, 1 stand, 2 bask, 3 roar, 4 fire, 5 eat, 6 sleep, 7 hover; flight by the mover's flyStep with a glide model (dragonRules.js glideStep); landings on the measure's land cells (never beside a campfire), bask and sleep by a campfire, eat by the feeder; the fire is particles only (mob.ghast.fireball and fire.fire), lights nothing. Whole-body flight (the server bench's fault): mover.js flyStep takes the body box (boxOpen: every cell the box moves into must be passable: air or the plants of data/blocks.js PASSABLE_PATTERNS, not liquid, cobweb, berry bush or powder snow); dragonRules.js roomToLand checks the whole footprint, the body's height and the wing margin; the wild landing (roomy) also needs ground that isn't a tree's crown and open sky over the body (block raycasts); the wild air spot needs a clear straight way (clearFlight: 9 raycasts from the box); a crown ahead in the air turns it up and over or, after a second, to somewhere else; never pushed up or down into a block; touchDown gives up a landing more than half a block off the floor instead of teleporting. Wild ones: a Survival player within 14 not sneaking or holding a Meat Ration: it roars facing them and takes off, and for a minute flies and lands only 21+ blocks from where they stood (its air spots 23 to 33 off, on its own side); a Meat Ration within 16 brings one in the air down beside them (the ground all round them tried, a great tree can stand on one side), facing them, while they hold it; flights and landings kept within about 24 blocks of where it was met. A hatchling follows one grown one (kept while it is about, not the nearest each time) and flies 1.4 times as fast when more than 6 blocks from its spot beside it. Exhibits: a re-check never cuts off a measurement under way (the sweep superseded the crate release's measure). Spawn rule: biome tag zoocraft_mythic, on grass_block, surface, light 7+, herd 1, density 1, weight 8 (at 3 the spawn bench saw none in 30 minutes). Journal tasks spot, dragonfire, wingbask, dragonroar; the glade's Places text mentions Dragons. The test zoo gets a 38 x 38 hall, walls 16 high under a glass roof, two campfires, a Basic Feeder and a Hanging Food Basket of Meat Rations and a trough (54 exhibits). Bench (BDS 1.26.60): run 1 found the fault (a raised grass block under its side, a false "ignored the pushes", short steps into leaves; confirmed by a block dump); run 2, 88 s: path 741, up to 31 over the ground, bask, fire, hover, glides, no ignored pushes, its box in a block only where it was summoned on an amethyst bud; a roar within 0.25 s at a Survival player 8 off; run 3 (the final steering): the roar within 0.3 s, then 31 blocks off 12 s later; sneaking to 7 blocks, no roar in 20 s; a Meat Ration held 4 blocks from it in the air brought it down 3.1 blocks off within 4 s, facing the player, staying while held; no ignored pushes. Spawn bench (six places in and round two glades, 5 minutes each, as the Unicorn's): at weight 3 no dragon in 30 minutes (8 unicorns); at weight 8, 2 (both in the glade on grass_block) and 6 unicorns; at weight 300 (bench only) 5 in 12 minutes, coats 0, 1 and 2. Park simulator (parkh/scene_dragon.mjs, 54 checks, all pass on seeds 1 and 2; scene_dragonwild.mjs on seeds 1 to 6; the mock now moves a flier's whole box and records blocked pushes). 303 tests. How: gen150/dragon150.py (art), sheet150.py, ents150.py, wire150.py, dragon.js, dragonRules.js, tests150.py, test150.mjs, docs150.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Dragon_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.151 (2026-10-31): the Griffin (module 9, the Mythical Glade; research/mythical_plan.md). The classic griffin at the "Big" size (about 4.5 blocks nose to tail, an 8-block span; collision 1.6 x 1.8, a chick 0.65 x 0.7 at the client's 0.4 scale), vanilla-level detail; coats from minecraft:variant (0 Golden, 1 Snowy, 2 Storm; wild weights 2/1/1). zoocraft:griffin (zoo, takes no damage) and zoocraft:wild_griffin (wild, with a spawn egg, no fall damage). Species: meat diet (3 a park day, a Basic Feeder), carrier large, habitat great_aerial (the dragons' hall), hoard (species.hoard: a block of gold or raw gold on the floor or in it; comfort cap 50 without, the welfare cause "hoard"), social 1 to 4 (prefersCompany false), breeding 1 chick (5, 6, 8 park days, scale 0.4), no research. The griffin measure (griffinRules.js measureGriffin, in exhibits.js): the gold blocks under the floor or beside it and where the first 8 are; a big empty exhibit with gold on its floor is measured for flight at the hall's height; the dragon measure's lowTops take gold and raw gold as things on the floor (FLOOR_THINGS). Behaviour (animals/griffin.js: a 10-tick brain and a 1-tick mover near players, RANGE 64; the dragon's flight and landing rules in dragonRules.js with GRIFFIN_FLIGHT's numbers, speed 0.25, turn 4.5): zoocraft:act 0 fly, 1 stand, 2 guard, 3 screech, 4 preen, 5 eat, 6 sleep, 7 hover, 8 dive. The dive (DIVE_FLIGHT, speed 0.4, maxRise 0.34): now and then (diveChance 0.25, a grown one by day) it flies to a spot within 3 blocks of the top of the hall's air and 8+ over the floor (pickDiveTop; a wild one 16 over the ground), circles up till it is within 1.5 blocks of its height (atDiveTop), then stoops at a spot 2 or 3 over the floor ahead of it (within 50 degrees, 9 to 22 blocks off and at least DIVE_SLOPE 1.25 times the drop, so it gets all the way down; a wild one 3.5 over the ground ahead) and sweeps up; broken off when scared. Ground acts by pickGroundAct (sleep at night; guard weighted 1.5x with gold, 0.5x without; eat with a feeder; screech for a grown one, never twice in a row; preen; stand); to guard or sleep a grown one more than 5 blocks from its gold flies over to it; byGold (within 5 blocks of a gold block, set where it lands and at each new act) plays the clink (armor.equip_gold). Sounds (vanilla): mob.enderdragon.flap pitched up, mob.phantom.idle (screech), mob.phantom.swoop (dive), mob.parrot.fly (preen), mob.ravager.bite (eat). Wild: perches on the giant trees' leaves as well as grass (never a trunk or log); a Survival player within 12 not sneaking or holding a Meat Ration: it screeches facing them and takes off, flying 20+ blocks from where they stood; a Meat Ration within 16 brings one in the air down beside them while they hold it. Spawn rule: biome tag zoocraft_mythic, on grass_block, surface, herd 1 to 2, density 2, weight 10. Journal tasks spot, guard, screech, dive; the glade's Places text mentions Griffins. The test zoo gets a 38 x 38 hall, walls 16 high under a glass roof, four gold blocks in the middle, a Basic Feeder and a Hanging Food Basket of Meat Rations and a trough (55 exhibits). Bench (BDS 1.26.60, a Wild Griffin summoned in the glade at -480 -534): runs 1 to 4 found dives that dropped 3 or 4 and ended 11 to 14 over the ground (it stooped before the climb was done, some at a crown); fixed (atDiveTop, DIVE_SLOPE, a wild dive only from 10+ over the ground, at open ground with no leaves under the spot, within wildRange of home); run 5, 360 s: path 1,235, up to 27 over the ground, at most 28 from where it was put, glided 79 of 799 samples in the air, 13 dives (drops 5.3 to 13.3, ending 3.1 to 6.7 over the ground), 4 landings on grass; a screech at a Survival player 8 off within 0.2 s, facing them, and 22 blocks off 12 s later; sneaking to 7 blocks three times, no screech within a second (a spontaneous screech is possible, so the window is short); a Meat Ration held 4 off brought it down 3.3 off in 2.4 s, facing them, staying 20 s; on a steep slope (-472 -530) no landing within 6 of the player had room (roomToLand), so the wild landNear now tries rings 3, 4.5, 6, 8 and 10 out from 5 over the player down 12: there 8.3 and 8.2 off within 3 s, facing them. A wild one left far from players despawns (vanilla despawn_from_distance), so later steps summon a fresh one. Spawn bench (weight 10): 6 in about 34 minutes at six places round two glades (4 single at gladeD, a pair at gladeF; coats 1, 2, 0, 0, 0, 0), all on glade grass_block; 8 unicorns and 2 dragons in the same time. The gladeF pair read plains at the feet: a weight-300 run (bench only, 18 minutes) logging the biome at the feet, the block below and 8 up and down found 11 griffins, all with the ground's biome the glade; one at the gladeF edge (278 103 692) had plains at the feet and 8 up, the glade below: Bedrock biomes are 3D and the rule is tested at the ground, so no change. Park sim (parkh scene_griffin.mjs, 53 checks, all pass on seeds 1 and 2): hall dives drop 6.5 to 10.2, lowest 3.0 over the floor; guard 2.8 to 3.1 off the gold, the clink; chick median 8.3 to 8.9 in the air (CATCH_UP: 1.6x speed past 6 off its spot); wild: landings on grass and mythwood_leaves, at most 26 to 29 from where it was met, scared to 26+ off, a Meat Ration brings it down 2.6 to 3.0 off. 304 tests. How: gen151/griffin151.py (art), sheet151.py, ents151.py, wire151.py, griffin.js, griffinRules.js, tests151.py, test151.mjs, docs151.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Griffin_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.152 (2026-10-31): the Glowhorn Stag (module 9, the Mythical Glade; research/mythical_plan.md), the glade's last animal. A great deer on an elk's frame at the "Elk-size" (about 1.6 blocks at the shoulder; collision 1.3 x 2.2, a fawn 0.65 x 1.1 at the client's 0.5 scale), vanilla-level detail; antlers bone-pale to mint and turquoise with a night skin (brighter antlers). Coats from minecraft:variant (0 Hind, 1 Stag; species.sexByCoat; wild weights 3/2); a fawn dappled with glowing spots. zoocraft:glowhorn_stag (zoo) and zoocraft:wild_glowhorn_stag (wild, with a spawn egg). Species: leafy diet (3 a park day, a Basic Feeder or a Hay Rack, species.rack), habitat grassland, graze 30 grass blocks each up to 40%, trunks (species.trunks min 1: the stag measure, glowhornRules.js measureStag in exhibits.js: the trunk blocks beside the floor and the floor cells to rub one from, rubFrom: a log of any kind RUB_GAP 3 cells straight ahead along a side, at the cell's level or a block up, with open cells between; comfort cap 50 without, the welfare cause "trunk"), social 2 to 8, carrier livestock, bait Leafy Ration, breeding 1 fawn (3, 5, 5 park days, scale 0.5), no research. Behaviour (animals/glowhorn.js, a 10-tick brain near players, RANGE 48, and a 1-tick mover for the line-up): zoocraft:act 0 none, 1 graze, 2 bellow, 3 rub, 4 alert, 5 rest, 6 glow, 7 step, 8 back. Interests by pickInterest (weights graze 4, feeder 2, rub 2, bellow 0.8, glow 0.6 (8 times at dusk and dawn), wander 3, rest 1); the engine walks it (zoocraft:seek_trunk, a move_to_block over every kind of log, goal_radius 3.2; zoocraft:seek_feeder). On BDS the walk to a log ignores the goal radius and ends with the body against the bark (its middle about 1.5 from the trunk's middle); so when it has stalled (moved under 0.15 in half a second) with no rub cell under it, lineUpForRub finds the nearest rub cell it can reach straight on or back (glowhornRules.js lineUp: up to 2.6 along and 0.75 to a side, over standable floor for its whole width, clearWay) and tickGlowhornMoves teleports it there a step a tick (0.09 forward, 0.06 back), facing the trunk, with act 7 or 8 (the client's legs keyed to q.life_time), then it rubs. The rub (4 to 7 s): knocks (hit.wood) once the antlers are down (0.6 s). The bellow: mob.camel.ambient at pitch 0.45, a breath puff (zoocraft:breath_puff) at the mouth 0.5 s in. The glow (5 to 8 s): motes (zoocraft:antler_mote) over the antlers and chime.amethyst_block once the head is up (1 s). Rest by day 15 to 30 s; at night lies down and renews till day, up within 1 to 10 s of sunrise (s.nightRest; a day rest that runs into the night now sets it too, so it gets up at dawn: the same fix in unicorn.js). A zoo one: someone sprinting within 6 freezes it (alert, 2 to 3.5 s, mob.horse.breathe). A wild one: someone in Survival or Adventure within shyRange 14, not sneaking and not holding a Leafy Ration: it stares facing them for a second (startleTicks 20), then the engine's avoid_mob_type takes it off (max_dist 14 and max_flee 18 at the top of the component, the entity_types entry max_dist 14, walk 1.4x, sprint 1.9x, ignore_visibility); a Leafy Ration within 8 tempts it. Spawn rule: biome tag zoocraft_mythic, on grass_block, surface, light 7 to 15, herd 2 to 4, density 6, weight 15. Journal tasks spot, antlerglow, antlerrub, stagbellow; the glade's Places text mentions them. The test zoo gets an 18 x 17 grass exhibit, walls 3 high, two oak trees (trunks 4 high under a cap of leaves), a Hay Rack and a Basic Feeder of Leafy Rations with a Hanging Food Basket and a trough, a stag and two hinds (56 exhibits). Bench (BDS 1.26.60, a 33-block pen with oak, birch, 2x2 dark oak and Mythwood trunks): run 1 found 1 rub in 2 minutes (the walk ends against the bark) and a day rest that never ended at dawn; run 2: 18 rubs in about 10.5 minutes of day and dusk (birch 10, mythwood 4, dark oak 3, oak 1), all with the trunk exactly 3 ahead, 0.00 off the cell's middle, the box clear; 9 line-ups, all stepping back (about 26 ticks each); glow 4 in 90 s of dusk, 3 in 6 minutes of day; midnight: all down within 5, 6, 7 and 26 s; morning: all up within 4, 4, 9.5 and 10.5 s; a Survival keeper walking up: alert at 9.2 to 9.8 (three; the fourth was lying down and got up), but the keeper reached 2.1 to 4.0 before they fled. Flee runs on an open field (bench stg:field, 97 x 97 grass at y 200, keeper walking up from about 18 three times, half-second samples, the flee's state logged): entity_types max_dist 10 only (as first built, and as the unicorn, okapi, deer, chorus deer, flamingo and zebra still have it): alerts 5.0 to 9.7, closest 1.4 to 5.3, one stood 7.6 off for 9 s; entity_types max_dist 14 with max_flee 18 in the same entry: alerts 10 to 13.7 but closest 3.4 to 5.7, flights stopped about 11 off (the top-level max_flee default 10), one noticed at 12.1 never moved (velocity 0 for 8 s, movement 0.22, the moving group on), another stood 4.9 off for 8 s; max_dist 14 and max_flee 18 at the top (the vanilla panda's layout) with the entry's max_dist 14: alerts 12.7 to 13.6 (11 of 12; one was lying and got up), closest 5.0 to 9.9, ended 17.0 to 26.6 off with the keeper still. The flight starts about 0.75 s after the stare ends. sprint_distance in an entity_types entry fails the whole entity on 1.26.60 ("not present in the Schema"), so it is left out. Sneaking to 4.3: no alert. A Leafy Ration held 7 off: all four to 1.1 to 2.4 within 5 s. Spawn bench (sites gladeA -480 -544 and gladeF 304 688, 6 minutes each, weight 15): gladeA 4 (coats 1, 0, 0, 0), gladeF a pair (coats 0, 1), all in zoocraft:mythical_glade on grass_block at light 15; a unicorn and a dragon in the same time. Park sim (parkh scene_glowhorn.mjs, 43 checks, all pass on seeds 1 and 2): the line-up backs off in 25 ticks, at most 0.058 a tick, ending 0.000 from the cell's middle. 305 tests. How: gen152/glowhorn152.py (art), sheet152.py, ents152.py, wire152.py, glowhorn.js, glowhornRules.js, tests152.py, test152.mjs, docs152.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Glowhorn_Stag_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.153 (2026-10-31): wild animals run off properly. The wild Deer, Chorus Deer, Flamingo, Lush Okapi, Unicorn and Zebra had max_dist and max_flee of minecraft:behavior.avoid_mob_type only in the entity_types entry, where Bedrock Dedicated Server 1.26.60 ignores max_flee (default 10) and, in part, max_dist (found with the Glowhorn Stag, 0.1.152); now each has both at the top of the component as well (the vanilla panda's layout) and the entry's max_dist the same: deer and chorus deer 10/16, flamingo 8/14 (sprint 1.5 to 1.8), okapi 14/18 (was 10), unicorn 10/16, zebra 8/14 (gen153/flee153.py). Scripts (gen153/wire153.py; balance.js shyRange = max_dist, fleeRange = max_flee, shyHoldTicks 200): a shy one is held shy of whoever came within shyRange while they're within fleeRange, for up to 10 s since they were last that close (shyNow in flamingo.js and zebra.js, heldShy in okapi.js and unicorn.js, the player by id, Survival or Adventure, not sneaking, no bait). Flamingo: no display while shy, a march or glide under way is cut, no march within fleeRange. Zebra: a shy herd ends its trek, gets no file, herding or rejoin moves, a member's glide is cut. Okapi: a wild one looks startleTicks 20 (was alertTicks 40 to 70), whatever it was doing stops. Unicorn: a wild one neighs (mob.horse.angry) and canters off at once (no rear first); once clear of them (not held shy) it turns to face them and rears, neighing, once, if they're within lookRange 24, in Survival, not sneaking, without a Grain Ration (lookBack); unicornRules.js text. Bench (BDS 1.26.60, cloud, bench-only bdstools main153.js: a 97 x 97 grass field at y 200, four of a kind in a row about 20 off, a simulated keeper in Survival walking up to the nearest at 4.3 b/s, half-second samples; fleesum153.py): before, closest 0.7 (unicorn) to 1.9 (flamingo), 3 of 12 deer never moved, animals stood within range 21 to 52 s a kind; entity files only, deer 6.2 and chorus deer 6.3 with 0 to 0.5 s stood, but the okapi 0.9 (its 2 to 3.5 s look), the unicorn 1.0 (its 2.2 s rear), flamingos 2.6 (displays started at about 9) and zebras 0.6 (the file walked them back; one stood 32.5 s); with the scripts, okapi 4.5 (median 7.3, stood 5 s), unicorn 4.9 (median 11.7, stood 1 s), deer 6.5, flamingo 1.5 once (a march under way, then fixed) and after the fix 3.1 in 24 (median 6.2, stood 2.5 s), zebra 2.6 once in 16 (grazing; the run starts about 0.75 s after the notice, and a zebra at 4.7 b/s against a keeper at 4.3 has little margin at 8) and 4.1 in 24 (median 6.5, stood 5 s); ends 9.7 to 33.7 off. Park sim: scene_unicorn.mjs's wild steps rewritten (a neigh and off; held shy; rears facing her once she's 20 off; up again; sneaking and the Grain Ration as before), all pass on seeds 1 and 2 (the stale exhibit count aside); the okapi scene's wild steps pass. 307 tests (gen153/test153.mjs: the seven flee entities' layout and numbers, the scripts' ranges matching, the hold logic in all four scripts). How: gen153/flee153.py, wire153.py, tests153.py, test153.mjs, docs153.py. A full Minecraft restart. Test plan: docs/Wild_animals_flee_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.154 (2026-10-31): the Mythical Glade's amethyst stands up (Austin's report and screenshot: the buds and clusters lay on their sides). Cause, confirmed on Bedrock Dedicated Server 1.26.60 (a cleared field at y 200, 36 patches): a single_block_feature's may_attach_to turns the block it places (may_attach_to.auto_rotate, on unless false), overriding places_block's minecraft:block_face "up" (0 of 500 up; all west); randomize_rotation false changed nothing; "auto_rotate": false gave 500 of 500 up. The four features mythic_amethyst_cluster, mythic_large_bud, mythic_medium_bud, mythic_small_bud get "auto_rotate": false (gen154/amy154.py). The other may_attach_to features in the pack place blocks with no facing (a test checks any with one keeps it). Land made before: world/glade.js straighten() every 100 ticks per player in the glade reads a BlockVolume of 33 x 13 x 33 round them (getBlocks, includeTypes the four crystals) and sets block_face "up" on each that lyingCrystal() passes (gladeRules.js: one of the four, facing west, on CRYSTAL_GROUND amethyst_block, mythwood_log or mythwood_wood (a tree's root put in the patch block's place), and not hung from CRYSTAL_HOLDS amethyst_block or budding_amethyst east of it, so builds and geodes are left). Bench (the 0.1.153 glade at -480 -544, seed 12345, a simulated player): set test crystals stood up or stayed as listed in docs/Glade_amethyst_test_plan.md within 10 s; all 43 crystals the world grew round the player stood up (the 5 others were the test ones left on purpose), among them one grown on a tree root (-474 100 -547, mythwood_log under it, mythwood_wood east). Bench note: a west-facing crystal hung from nothing pops off as soon as a block next to it changes (setting the block east of a test crystal to air broke it). 309 tests (gen154/test154.mjs). How: gen154/amy154.py, tests154.py, test154.mjs, docs154.py. No new files: rejoin the world. Test plan: docs/Glade_amethyst_test_plan.md.
- 0.1.155 (2026-10-31): the Lava Falls look like lava falls (Austin: /locate's places "just looked like normal warped forests"). Cause: 0.1.141's roof springs were tried only from y 32 to 44 and kept only over the lava sea, so the land /locate points at had none. Now (gen155/falls155.py, world/lavaFallRules.js, world/lavaFalls.js): both spring features snap and set into the rock (snap_to_surface_feature, format 1.26.50, embed_in_surface true): lava_falls_roof_spring (surface ceiling, search_range 64, rule y 32-110, 10 a chunk) and lava_falls_cliff_spring (surface wall, search_range 16, rule y 36-100, 16 a chunk), both feature_to_snap lava_falls_spring (single_block_feature, may_replace netherrack, blackstone, basalt, magma, soul_soil; no may_attach_to). A spring pours from each open side (below or the four sides); springStreams follows each down through air and Nether plants; springFate: all into the lava sea at least MIN_DROP 8 (sea), or all onto FLOOR blocks at one height at least MIN_LAND_DROP 6 (pool), else rock (netherrack). planPool: the floor within POOL_R2 5 of the foot that is level (soft over it, solid under it), leaving out any next to lower ground, flood-filled from the foot (every foot must be in it); two deep where within DEEP_R2 1 and nothing open beside or under; RIM_TO_MAGMA neighbours to magma at RIM_MAGMA 0.4; plants over it cleared top first (twisting vines). lavaFalls.js sets air, magma, lava, then the spring, then sets the open side to netherrack and back to air (the neighbour update lava needs to flow). Glow: lava_falls_glow_rule 8 a chunk (was 4), glow_blob count 20 (was 14). Bench (BDS 1.26.60, seed 12345, x 352-447 z 2-97, 90% Lava Falls, fresh worlds per version): falls 3 -> 61; 96 pools, 35 sea falls, 232 springs to netherrack (152 no stream fit, 80 no pool); loose lava over y 33 on the ground with an open side 25 (0.1.154) vs 26 (0.1.155), the same vanilla spots; glowstone 1,152 -> 4,303; no content log errors. Let's move them to the lava falls"). spawn_rules/wild_dragon.json: has_biome_tag zoocraft_lava_falls, spawns_on_surface and spawns_underground, on warped_nylium or netherrack, weight 4 (8 in the glade), herd 1, density 1/1, no light rule. species.js: origin custom_nether, homeDimension minecraft:nether (releases, Nether visitors), where text; dragonRules.wildLine; trenchRules PLACES: mythical_glade about/found drop the dragon, lava_falls about/found gain it. dragon.js, the wild in caverns: kinds() reads open/liquid/solid (land is open over solid; over lava it is air to fly over); wildSpot takes open air at its height or 6 or 12 over it, the floor under it (groundUnder overLiquid: the lava sea counts) and the roof over it (roofOver), the spot wildHeight over the floor kept ROOF_ROOM 5 under the roof, skipped where under g + 2 (a low cave); landAnywhere skips columns that are rock at its height; TREE takes stems, hyphae, wart blocks and shroomlight (no landing on a fungus); a wild one in the Nether keeps its day (no night there); a wild one looks LOOK_STEPS (7) steps ahead (boxOpen at pos + 7v) and with lava or rock there slows to 0.15 (stops if its very next step is blocked) and picks another spot (gives up a landing), at most every 10 ticks (the game lets a body through lava: the first bench run's take-off went through a fall); fleeing, wildSpot tries 12 far spots, 12 nearer and wider ones, then wander spots farther from the scarer than now (the first bench run hovered a minute 7 blocks from the player in a cavern). Bench (BDS 1.26.60, seed 12345, the 0.1.155 Lava Falls patch x 352-447 z 2-97): 1,316 flight samples, 3 in lava for a moment, 0 in rock (46 in lava before LOOK_STEPS 7); 7 landings, all on warped nylium; never into a low cave; bait 4.9 blocks; scared, 26 blocks off in 20 s; spawns at weight 4 with the area cleared: one dragon 8 s in, then held at one for 20 minutes. 313 tests (gen156/test156.mjs; the 0.1.150, 0.1.151 and 0.1.135 tests' glade lines and weights brought up to date). - 0.1.157 (2026-10-31): pools of lava in the Lava Falls (Austin: "I also want pools of lava in the lava falls biome as well"). World generation, first_pass (before the warped forest's plants: in surface_pass and final_pass the bench had sprouts, roots and fungi standing on the pools): feature_rules/lava_falls_pool_rule (2 tries a chunk, middle x/z 7-8, y 34-100) -> snap_to_surface floor, embedded, on nylium or netherrack -> shift -6, -6 -> aggregate of 13 x 13 fixed_grid scatters: top (lava where a mask of radius 4.0 + 1.2 noise + 0.8 edge noise says, each block only with its 4 sides rock or lava, rock under it and air over it: single_block may_attach_to, so sealed in any order), tidy twice (lava with 3 or 4 rock sides back to nylium), deep (y -1 inside radius - 1.6, with lava over it), rim (nylium or netherrack beside lava with air over it -> magma 2 : netherrack 3). The game's may_attach_to (bench, 20 cases): a listed top or bottom must match, min_sides_must_attach counts the four sides only; fixed_grid with a negative extent skips columns after its first row, so the grid is 0..12 from a shifted origin. Bench (BDS 1.26.60, seed 12345, x 352-447 z 2-97, 36 chunks): with an orange concrete marker, 21 pools, 6-62 blocks each, 3-10 across, 241 more two deep, every block with rock or pool on all four sides and under it; first_pass pools had nothing standing on them, surface_pass and final_pass ones had sprouts, roots and fungi; with real lava, the only plant on lava between y 34 and 100 was one vanilla twisting vine stack (the game grows 6 over its own lava up by the roof); no content log errors. Picture previews/lava_pools_0157.png. trenchRules PLACES lava_falls hint/about tell of the pools. 316 tests (gen157/test157.mjs). How: gen157/pools157.py, text157.py, tests157.py, test157.mjs, docs157.py, ship157.py; bench gen157/attach157.py, grid157.py, poolcensus157.py, bdstools/steps_lpa/lpg/lpp.txt. - 0.1.158 (2026-10-31): Obsidian Armor. Austin: "Let's add some lava resistance armor that also lets you see in lava like our water one"; card: Full suit; "It should be crafted from obsidian. Call it obsidian armor"; "Helmet needs 5 obsidian like the helmet and tinted glass in the middle". Four items (BP items/obsidian_{helmet,chestplate,leggings,boots}.json: wearable head/chest/legs/feet, protection 2/7/5/2, durability 300/440/410/360, repaired with obsidian, enchantable value 10), recipes in the iron armour shapes with obsidian, the helmet OOO/OTO round a tinted glass (crafting table and Zoo Workshop), attachables on Mojang's humanoid armour geometries (geometry.humanoid.armor.helmet/chestplate/leggings/boots, armor materials, the matching *_layer_visible 0, controller.render.armor) with two 64 x 32 armour skins painted here (RP textures/models/armor/zoocraft_obsidian_1 and _2: purple-black obsidian with violet specks and rims, the helmet's visor a band of smoky amber glass over an open face), four 16 x 16 icons, four lang lines, RP fogs/obsidian_helmet_fog_setting.json (zoocraft:fog_obsidian_helmet: lava and lava_resistance only, fog_start 8, fog_end 48, #991A00, fixed; Mojang's lava fog ends 0.64 and 4 with fire resistance). Script equipment/obsidianArmor.js (rules in obsidianRules.js), a 10-tick loop in main.js: the helmet's fog pushed with `/fog @s push zoocraft:fog_obsidian_helmet zoocraft_obsidian` while it is on (a remove before every push and on taking it off; the first look after load clears a left-over entry); with all four on, fire resistance topped up to 200 ticks every 10 (a potion's longer one left alone) and taken off with the suit if it is the suit's; flames put out once out of lava and fire; an action-bar line when the suit goes on and while some of it is on in lava or alight. Bench (BDS 1.26.60, world ob1, bdstools/obs_bp, steps_ob1.txt): the whole suit 10 s with the head under lava, health 20 throughout, fire resistance 194, never alight, none after stepping out; three pieces 20 to 0 in about 5 s (killed by fire); the fog push succeeded. The fog can't be seen on a server. 318 tests (two new: the items, recipes, attachables, skins, icons, names and fog; the rules).
